# OpenWOP Spec — Changelog

All notable changes to the OpenWOP spec corpus — v2 (the current major, `spec/v2/`) and v1 (the maintained parallel track through the overlap, `spec/v1/`) — its schemas, OpenAPI/AsyncAPI, conformance suite, and contract artifacts.

Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1/) loosely. Versions are spec-corpus-wide (one date, multiple artifact updates per row); per-artifact versions live in their respective `package.json` / schema `$id` fields.

> **Status legend** (per [`/governance/spec-status/`](https://openwop.dev/governance/spec-status/)):
> Stable · Stabilizing · Draft · Experimental — see individual doc headers for current state. The legacy `STUB / DRAFT / OUTLINE / FINAL` vocabulary still appears in older releases below; both are valid in the corpus.

> **Package version map.** The version headers below are **spec-corpus** versions, not package versions — each package advances independently on its own per-package release tag. So a corpus version may be skipped here when its number was already consumed by a single-package release, and not every published package version has a corpus row. Notably **there is no corpus `1.1.6`**: that number was taken by a TypeScript-SDK-only npm patch (`@openwop/openwop@1.1.6`, published 2026-05-31, the agent-platform/roster helper catch-up), so the corpus skipped straight to `1.1.7`. A deep-link like `#v1-1-6` therefore has no anchor here by design — the TS SDK's own history lives in [`sdk/typescript/CHANGELOG.md`](https://github.com/openwop/openwop/blob/main/sdk/typescript/CHANGELOG.md) and on npm. Current published per-package versions (as of the `1.1.7` corpus release): `@openwop/openwop` **1.2.0** (npm) · `@openwop/openwop-conformance` **1.18.1** (npm) · `openwop-client` **1.1.7** (PyPI) · `github.com/openwop/openwop/sdk/go` **v1.1.7** (Go modules).

---

## [Unreleased]

- **RFC 0198 lands: the v2 MCP server mount maps long runs to MCP Tasks, and a disconnect cancels only the run it owns** (`Active` unchanged; additive). `spec/v2/core/interop.md` gains §"MCP tasks and cancellation" (+200 core words): a host MAY serve `io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks` (revision 2026-07-28), advertised in `server/discover` `capabilities.extensions` plus `extensions` in `mcp.features[]` and nowhere else; a declaring `tools/call` on a non-terminal run is answered `CreateTaskResult`; `taskId` is the projected tenant-bound `runId` (≥128-bit opaque segment, never a credential); `tasks/get` appends nothing. Until the host has sent its whole response, a request owns the run it starts or continues and a disconnect cancels it (`run.cancelled.reason: mcp-request-cancelled`, registered in the v2 `runCancelled.reason` description); after the response no disconnect touches the run; the host never sends `notifications/cancelled` except to end `subscriptions/listen`. `spec/v2/interop-map.json` gains `pins.mcpTasks` and `mcp.tasks` (status projection total over `RunSnapshot.status`, `tasks/get|update|cancel` ↔ `getRun|resolveInterruptByRun|cancelRun`, the `taskId` / `ttlMs` / `statusMessage` / capability field rows). New protocol invariant `mcp-task-tenant-scoped` (critical; unreadable ≡ nonexistent `-32602` on `tasks/*` and `subscriptions/listen`); `SECURITY/threat-model-interop.md` gains the task-handle and orphaned-run rows; RFC 0153 `Amended by` (UQ4 reversed for v2 only); `spec/v1/mcp-integration.md` gains a one-sentence informative pointer, v1 unchanged.
- **RFC 0197 prose: v2 surfaces are retired, never reshaped** (`Active` unchanged; `breaking` governance amendment, no wire change; the registers, gates and scenario land last in the cycle, wave W5). `spec/v2/core/overview.md` §0 now says removing a member is a major except under the new §0a, which forbids reshaping a v2 surface in a 2.x minor and admits a removal only when `check-v2-retirement.mjs` proves six predicates; readers keep accepting retired shapes on replay, fork and poll. `capabilities.md` §8: a record's `status` MUST NOT exceed its `spec/v2/declaration.json` row. `versioning.md` §1.3 points at the new `COMPATIBILITY.md` §2.4 (core +165 words, 25,132 → 25,297 / 29,200). `COMPATIBILITY.md` covers v1 and v2: new §2.4 (adding an OPTIONAL property to a closed v2 object is additive; REQUIRED/closing/narrowing is a major; the §C maturity ceiling binds bundles cut on the first suite release shipping RFC 0197, and earlier bundles are reported, not failed, per D1, with MyndHyve's 38-family re-cut owed as `openwop.gap.0197.1`), new §3a (retirement rationale P1–P3; no v2 removal until the gates run in `openwop:check`), a §7 v2 paragraph and an SDK row. The `GOVERNANCE.md` breaking-change row and `RFCS/README.md` gain the v2-retirement class.
- **RFC 0201 lands: Standard Webhooks 1.0.0 as an opt-in, per-subscription companion signature scheme (`standard-webhooks-1`)** (`Active` unchanged; additive). `registerWebhook` gains `signatureAlgorithms` (v1 + v2 OpenAPI; absent ⇒ `["v1"]`, byte-identical to today); an opted-in subscription supplies a `whsec_` secret, is endpoint-verified before its `201` (`schemas/v2/webhook-verification.schema.json`; new error `webhook_endpoint_unverified`), and every delivery adds `webhook-id` / `webhook-timestamp` / `webhook-signature` beside the unchanged five `OpenWOP-*` headers under scheme `v1` — the one named exception to the `OpenWOP-*` naming rule, generated into `headers.md` by `derive-v2-api.py`. New optional facet `webhooks.secretRotation` + `rotateWebhookSecret` (`POST /webhooks/{webhookId}/rotate-secret`, a v2 OpenAPI operation derived like RFC 0188's read; the v1 route `/v1/webhooks/{webhookId}/rotate-secret` is defined in v1 prose only, because a new canonical v1 operation would oblige the openwop-sdks parity manifest, which vendors a published tag); the facet enum grows to `["v1","standard-webhooks-1"]` with `contains: v1` (no `uniqueItems`, R11). v1 `webhooks.md` §"Standard Webhooks companion scheme (RFC 0201)", v2 `webhooks.md` §Standard Webhooks (+243 core words, 24,281 → 24,524 / 29,000). Two protocol invariants (`webhook-endpoint-verification-opt-in`, `webhook-message-id-stable`; 203 → 205), threat-model §4.11, Amended-by rows on RFC 0165 / 0171. Four v2 scenarios ride the open 2.36.0 suite (see `conformance/CHANGELOG.md`). No floor, profile or requirement-id predicate changes.
- **RFC 0209: v2 `ui.a2ui-surface` is A2UI v0.9 at per-kind schema version 2** (`additive`; v1 unchanged). `schemas/v2/envelopes/ui.a2ui-surface.schema.json` becomes an `anyOf` of `$defs/payloadV1` (the seeded seven-component tree, content unchanged; `component` → `componentV1`) and `$defs/payloadV2` (`{version: "v0.9", catalogId, surfaceId, messages[1..64]}` — `createSurface`/`updateComponents`/`updateDataModel`/`deleteSurface` in a closed OpenWOP profile of the basic catalog that excludes `functionCall` actions incl. `openUrl`, `FunctionCall` values, `obscured` text fields, `Image`/`Video`/`AudioPlayer`/`Icon` and `theme.iconUrl`); an engine validates against the one branch the version selects. `spec/v2/ext/a2uiSurface/README.md` is now the kind's v2 contract (versions, profile, actions, the §C.9 fold guard, replay, sticky trust). `events.md` restores the `ui.*`/`media.*` kind carve-out and points at the ext contract (+17 core words; 24,281 → 24,298 / 29,000). The RFC 0114 delta frame and `deltaTransport` facet are deprecated for major 2 (two `deprecations.json` rows, `removeIn: 3.0` until RFC 0197's gates land; earliest removal minor 2.38). New invariant `a2ui-surface-no-secret-input` (203 → 204) and five `a2ui-*` notes restated for version 2; `threat-model-prompt-injection.md` gains the credential-phishing row. New v2 seam `POST /conformance/seams/sample/a2ui/emit-surface` (`api/seams-v2.yaml`). RFC 0102/0114 headers carry `Amended by: RFC 0209 (major 2 only)`; the 0102 register G5/G7 are transferred for v2. Upstream A2UI v0.9 `server_to_client.json`, basic `catalog.json` and `common_types.json` are vendored under `conformance/fixtures/upstream/a2ui-v0.9/`, re-fetched from a2ui.org on 2026-09-22 and matching the RFC's SHA-256 pins.
- **RFC 0203: a `remote` node-pack runtime may name its MCP server by an inline MCP Registry record, and `nodePackRuntimes` gets its v2 home** (additive; RFC 0197 §B.5 — an OPTIONAL property on a closed v2 object). `schemas/v2/node-pack-manifest.schema.json` `$defs/Runtime` gains OPTIONAL `mcpServer` (new closed `$defs/McpServerRecord`: `name`, `description`, `version`, optional `$schema`/`title`/`websiteUrl`/`repository`, exactly one `streamable-http` remote with an `https://` URL; no `packages[]`, `headers`, `variables`, `icons` or `_meta`), refused under any other `language` by an `if`/`else`. `entry` MUST equal `remotes[0].url` (`pack_validation_failed`). NEW `spec/v2/core/node-pack-runtimes.md` is the family's `normativeText` (`v1Dependent` 43 → 42; core budget +294 words, cap +200 → net +94; the banner cites only Accepted RFC 0008, RFC 0203 is named in the section it adds). `unsupported_runtime` (400, v1 convention) registered in `spec/v2/errors.json` (102 codes). The v1 schema is unchanged (G2). Threat rows in `SECURITY/threat-model-node-packs.md`; `Amended by` pointers on RFC 0008 and 0138. Witness: the corpus-coherence test `node-pack-mcp-server-record` (conformance 2.36.0).
- **RFC 0208: v2 homes the A2A and MCP operation mappings** (`Active`; MCP/A2A review P1 finding 4). New `spec/v2/interop-map.json` + `interop-map.schema.json` carry the rows v1 held only in `a2a-integration.md` §C–§E and `mcp-integration.md` §A–§E — 12 A2A operations, the card, the total `RunSnapshot.status` → `TaskState` projection and its reverse, the nine A2A errors at **1.0.1** plus the invalid-parameters row, the field rules, and the MCP features / methods / headers / `_meta` / MRTR / cache / authorization rows — and `spec/v2/core/interop.md` §"The operation mappings" incorporates them normatively (serve every implemented row as stated under the caller's Subject; refuse a row whose `requires` facet is unadvertised; list every required feature; an unreadable task answers exactly as a nonexistent one; `ListTasks` is the `listRuns` set; A2A §3.4.3 contextId inference and mismatch refusal; a retained terminal task is `UnsupportedOperationError`). `capabilities.md` §3.1 makes `implementation` self-reported (a client SHOULD NOT key behaviour or authorization on it) and gains optional `implementation.url` (generated from `generate-from-declaration.mjs`); §3.2 requires a breaking extension change to ship under a new key. New gate `scripts/check-interop-map.mjs` (in `openwop:check`) checks every row's `v2Operation`, error code, run status, stored state and `requires` facet against the v2 wire; six sabotages each red. v1 gains informative pointers only; RFC 0152/0153 gain `Amended by` rows. Core words +297 (24,281 → 24,578 / 29,000), no family homed.
- **`derive-v2-schemas.mjs --write` no longer reverts hand edits, and the gate now runs its `--check`** (tooling; no wire change). On `main`, `--write` rewrote 32 of the 66 files still marked `x-openwop-seeded-from: v1`, deleting RFC 0186's `onTimeout` (suspend-request) and `ConversationExchangedPayload` alias (conversation-event), `eval-summary.pinnedNodeIds`, and the RFC 0187 / 2.6.1 description corrections. The script had no `--check` and nothing in `openwop:check` ran it, so edits made to marked files by #1232, #1236, #1317, #1371, #1383, #1401, #1404 and #1484 went unnoticed. The mechanical edits are now seed rules: id binding from `spec/v2/id-field-bindings.json` (plural `*Ids` included), the manifest-scoped `/v1/<op>` → `/<op>` rewrite (moved to `scripts/v2-path-spellings.mjs`, shared with `generate-from-declaration.mjs`), and example repair (`$comment` dropped, bare ids bound to their tenant-bound kind). The five files with v2-only edits drop the marker and belong to their RFCs. 11 marked files are re-serialized byte-for-byte, with no semantic change. `--check` (stage 10) fails when a marked file differs from its derivation or a set member is missing. Sabotage-tested: a one-word edit to `goal.schema.json`, a deleted `residency.schema.json`, and the marker restored on `suspend-request` each fail the check.
- **Three v2 core contradictions ruled, four medium-tier trims applied, and the last dangling `§SSRF` anchors repointed** (arch-trim defects 5, 7–9). (7) A v1 resume token is recognised by the absent `ow2.` prefix, never a segment count — `identity.md` §4 and `persistence.md`'s store template now say so, matching the #1211 ruling already in `persistence.md`. (8) `runs.md`'s `residency.region` rejection binds only on a host advertising `dataResidency`, as RFC 0129 §3 and `runs.md` §"Conversation and residency capabilities" already said; a non-advertising host MAY ignore or reject. (9) `idempotency.md` §Why now binds Layer 2 on advertising `idempotency`, as RFC 0173 §B (adversarial review 5) and §Layer 2 already said. No scenario, no bundle outcome and no requirement moves: `data-residency-admission` and `v2-effect-identity-business-key` are already gated on the advertisement, and `v2-interrupt-token-scheme` refuses an un-issued un-prefixed token under both readings. Trims CF-SIX, EVT-ERA2, INT-TOKENS and PER-CORPUSTAG (−193 words; each removed MUST survives in RFC 0168 §B.2, `persistence.md` §The codemap is data / §The reader rule, `identity.md` §4 or `versioning.md` §4); ERR-ONECODE skipped (its run-scoped `409` after cancellation is stated nowhere else). `SECURITY/invariants.yaml` and RFC 0196 cite `webhooks.md` §Egress instead of the nonexistent §SSRF (anchor only).
- **The `a2a-1.0` error table is re-pinned to A2A 1.0.1, and a message to a retained terminal task is `UnsupportedOperationError`** (MCP/A2A review C-F1, C-F12). `a2a-integration.md` §B, D.6 and D.7 carry upstream 1.0.1's corrected gRPC/HTTP+JSON statuses (six rows moved; JSON-RPC codes and the JSON-RPC floor unchanged); servers SHOULD emit them, clients MUST accept either table and MUST NOT identify an error by status alone. D.2 no longer answers a terminal-but-readable task with `TaskNotFoundError` (upstream §3.1.1/§3.1.2); purged/unknown/unreadable stays `TaskNotFoundError`. Recorded as a conformance-affecting correction in `COMPATIBILITY.md` §3; no scenario changed.
- **Spec v1 status markers agree with RFC status, and the gate now sees every form.** `check-rfc-status-coherence.mjs` checks inline ``RFC NNNN, `S` ``, ``RFC NNNN (`S`) `` and linked-RFC markers on every spec/v1 line (23 were stale — all now `Accepted`), and requires a `Status: Draft` banner resting only on Accepted RFCs to say so and name its predicate; `frontend-plugin-packs.md` → Stable (predicate fired: `app.openwop.dev` advertises `uiPlugins.supported`), the other six Draft banners restate their predicate (`compensation.md` stays Draft on open gaps G5–G7/G9); the `auth.md`/`profiles.md` "not yet registered" notes corrected; `agent-ref-positioning.md` drops its RFC 2119 keywords (non-normative); `a2a-integration.md` §1 cross-references the three meanings of "skill". No wire change.
- **Thirteen RFCs filed `Active` for the MCP/A2A alignment program (0197–0209), with windows waived** — eight by explicit steward override of RFC 0147 §A.6 (0197, 0198, 0199, 0200, 0201, 0202, 0208, 0209: certification, replay, external effects, idempotency, isolation, identity/authorization), five as routine waivers (0203–0207). Recorded in each RFC's `Updated` line, the `MAINTAINERS.md` ledger and the RFC 0156 §B register (`not-reviewed`); acceptance of the override cohort is provisional. RFC documents and gap registers only: every spec, schema and scenario change lands in each RFC's own spec PR (0208 after openwop#1481; 0198/0199/0200/0202/0207 after 0208). A cross-RFC architect pass resolved the conflicts before filing — 0208's registry defers every credential/`AUTH_REQUIRED` row to 0199 and keys `a2a.taskState` by `(status, interruptKind?)`; 0207 keeps the HTTP `traceparent` header a conforming carrier with `_meta`/`Message.metadata` SHOULD-also (MyndHyve emits the header); one owner per `interop.md` section. The batch nets about +1,960 core words against 1,716 of headroom, so the spec PRs are budget-ordered. `spec/v1/gaps.json` (and its `spec-artifacts/` mirror, unpublished 2.36.0) regenerated from the new registers.
- **RFCs 0197–0209 amended per the implementation review, before any implementation lands** (`Active` unchanged; no wire change). RFC 0199 §E.2 drops the connection-pack `allOf` that would have made `provider.auth.issuer` REQUIRED — a registry census found six published `reach.mcp` + `oauth2` packs (`core.openwop.connections.{github,jira,notion}`, v1 and v2) without it — for the RFC's own host-side rule (refuse the grant, never the document). Every requirement id a Falsifiability table names is now minted where `check-accepted-predicate` can see it: v1-only ids are re-homed to major-2 scenarios (0199, 0200, 0207), made non-gating with no id (0200 §C, 0204 `raw`/`catalog-order`, 0207's debug-bundle rows, which no v2 host can serve), and `(corpus)` rows move to `conformance/src/coherence/` (0200, 0205, 0206, 0209). 0205 splits `turn-parts-shape` into a server-free id and the behavioural `turn-parts-emitted`, and 0209 splits `catalog-pinned` from the seam-gated `catalog-equality`. Short-form ids in 0199/0200/0201/0207 are spelled in full, since rule 4 read them as no id at all. 0197 lands its gates and baseline last in the 2.36.0 cycle and states when a branch re-cut is a new surface. 0207 restates D4: a header-only carrier passes.
- **The 2.36.0 cycle opens, and the first release-bound corrections ride it** (MCP/A2A review P1-F). `@openwop/openwop-conformance` and `@openwop/spec-artifacts` move to `2.36.0` (unpublished; npm still serves `2.35.1`) so later edits to shipped files don't collide with the published tarball; `spec/v2/release.json` stays `2.35.0` until the release PR that cuts the tag. Descriptions only, no `$id` or structure change: `dispatch-config` `workerDispatchModel` no longer says hosts MAY add `vendor.<host>.<model>` values its closed enum rejects; `a2a-task-state` `state` names the `cancelling` and `waiting-external` projections and the A2A 1.0 `TASK_STATE_*` rendering, and `interruptKind` says its "present iff" rule is prose, not schema-enforced, and that `waiting-external` has no enum value. `spec/v2/core/capabilities.md` §3.2 lists all four `reservedOrgs` from `spec/v2/declaration.json` (`openwop`, `vendor`, `effect-seams`, `events`), not two. The RFC 0114 G4 gap row (and `spec/v1/gaps.json`) names `a2uiSurface.deltaTransport`, not `ui.a2ui-surface.deltaUpdates`, and matches INTEROP-MATRIX: one witness, implemented behind an env flag, not advertised live (observed 2026-09-22).
- **Added (non-normative): `docs/integrations/mcp.md`, correspondence notes between OpenWOP and nearby MCP surfaces** (MCP/A2A review, Phase 2 findings 4 and 5). Four tables and two notes, each describing today's wire only: `ToolDescriptor` `safetyTier` / `replayPolicy` / `egress` beside MCP `ToolAnnotations` with a why-not-equivalent column (MCP defaults `destructiveHint` and `openWorldHint` to `true`; `tool-catalog.md` §C still forbids inferring `safetyTier`); the card/form field vocabulary beside elicitation `requestedSchema`; front-end plugin packs beside MCP Apps (SEP-1865); reserved `exportFormats` identifiers beside IANA media types; the prompt library beside `prompts/list`; and a pointer for the OpenTelemetry `mcp.*` conventions. Upstream facts checked against MCP 2026-07-28 `schema.ts`, SEP-1865 and the IANA registry on 2026-09-22. One informative pointer sentence added to each of `tool-catalog.md`, `chat-card-packs.md`, `form-content-packs.md`, `frontend-plugin-packs.md`, `artifact-type-packs.md`, `prompts.md` and `mcp-integration.md`. No wire, schema, suite or count change.
- **The v1 A2A/MCP composition prose stops citing upstream surface that does not exist.** `a2a-integration.md`: the failure row's MUST on `Task.error.message` (no such A2A field) → `Task.status.message`, `Task.createdAt` → `Task.status.timestamp`, the error carrier unified on `metadata.openwop.error`, the `ListTasks` row no longer cites a nonexistent `GET /v1/runs`, the push "HMAC per A2A §4.3.3" attribution removed (A2A defines no HMAC contract), the 0.3 resume example rewritten in 0.3 wire form with the 5-action vocabulary, two `RFC 0128`/`RFC 0100` markers read `Accepted`, and an informative note that A2A 1.0.1 re-mapped six gRPC/HTTP error statuses (JSON-RPC unchanged; the re-pin is a later correction). `rest-endpoints.md`: the agent card defers to `agentCardUrl` / `/.well-known/agent-card.json`. `mcp-integration.md`: MCP Logging's deprecation (SEP-2577) recorded beside Sampling/Roots. Both documents: nine stale "not yet registered" notes for invariants that are registered. `tool-catalog.md`: the tool-session rationale no longer cites MCP stateful connections. Recorded as a Class 3 correction in `COMPATIBILITY.md` §3; no schema, code, status or scenario changed.
- **Eleven SECURITY invariants now cite the upstream MCP 2026-07-28 / A2A v1.0.1 rule they restate or strengthen** (MCP/A2A review §5, P1-D). In `SECURITY/invariants.yaml` notes only: `secret-leakage-error-envelope`, `secret-leakage-auth-middleware`, `http-client-ssrf-guard`, `delegation-tenant-audience-bound`, `authorization-fail-closed`, `auth-key-rotation-no-canary-echo`, `egress-credential-audience-bound`, `trigger-ingestion-ssrf`, `a2a-push-egress-ssrf`, `mcp-header-body-consistent`, `callback-url-egress-guarded` each name the upstream section and URL (checked live 2026-09-22) and say whether OpenWOP's rule equals it (3) or strengthens it (8). No id, tier, test or count changes (203).
- **Editorial prose fixes (no wire change).** `grpc-transport.md` calls `StreamRunEvents` server-streaming, matching `api/grpc/openwop.proto` (it said bidirectional); `debug-bundle.md` gives `spanId`/`parentSpanId` as 16 hex characters (8 bytes, W3C Trace Context), not "16-byte"; `positioning.md` drops the six RFC 2119 keywords its own non-normative banner disclaims and aligns its A2A row with RFC 0072 (`GET /v1/agents` is the tenant-scoped inventory, the A2A AgentCard stays the cross-protocol discovery path); `agent-ref-positioning.md` gains an informative note separating A2A `AgentSkill` (= Workflow), Agent Skills `SKILL.md` bundles, and the RFC 0131 `role: "skill"` manifest.
- **INTEROP-MATRIX no longer claims openwop-app advertises RFC 0114 A2UI delta transport live.** A read-only probe on 2026-09-22 of `app.openwop.dev/.well-known/openwop`, `app.openwop.dev/api/.well-known/openwop` and the `*.run.app` origin finds no `a2uiSurface` key; the host implements `a2uiSurface.deltaTransport` behind `OPENWOP_A2UI_DELTA_TRANSPORT` / `OPENWOP_TEST_SEAM_ENABLED` (default off), so the row now reads "implemented behind an env flag, not advertised", and the 2026-07-06 behavioral witness stays on record. RFC 0114's status is unchanged.
- **The v2 core kernel sheds 2,838 words without changing a requirement** (trim review, decision D2; no wire change). `check-core-budget` 27,284 → 24,446 / 29,000: history and rationale narrative, clauses restated from another core doc (each now a pointer to the doc that states it), and the `C2`/`C5`/`C6` migration-row tables that copied `spec/v1/migrations.json`. Every cited heading and anchor survives except `persistence.md` §Forking a v1 run (a subset of `replay.md` §Forking a v1 run, cited only in one scenario comment); `check-v2-normative-home` ratchets are unchanged. The capabilities.md §5 family stubs are kept, and five that had drifted from `spec/v2/declaration.json` are corrected (`supportedEnvelopes`, `schemaVersions`, `envelopeStrictness` are owned by RFC 0193; `workflowChainPacks` and `packs` now name their hand-decided facets). Editorial corrections: events.md's codemap row count 117 → 118; overview.md's reading order drops the nonexistent `streams.md` and adds `conversation.md` and `host-services.md`, and its "under 25,000 words" becomes a pointer to the enforced budget; interrupt.md cites `webhooks.md` §Egress (there is no §SSRF). Eight v2 scenarios cited sections that never existed as headings, so their `req()` section strings (and `conformance/requirements.json`) now name the real one: `webhooks.md` §SSRF → §Egress, `replay.md` §Endpoint → §The surface, `replay.md` §Effect identity → `idempotency.md` §Layer 2: effect identity, `runs.md` §configurable → §Run options, `events.md` §run.cancelled / §run.paused → `runs.md` §Cancel / §Pause and resume, `events.md` §Reading an era-2 log → §Era-2 logs, and `conformance.md` §Fixtures → RFC 0168 §C.3 (the advertised-fixture rule has no v2 core home). No requirement id changed. `spec-artifacts/` regenerated.

- **openwop-app certifies `openwop-core-standard` in production for the first time, and its committed bundle is replaced.** Suite 2.35.1, build `commit:b5066568d` verified against the live `/api/readiness`, nothing relaxed, `167 / 0 / 0 / 75 / 19`, all three profiles certified (`openwop-discovery-core`, `openwop-core-standard`, `openwop-conformance-seams-v2`). The file is byte-identical to the bundle production serves; verified by the steward with the published suite under the key `openwop-app-bundle-2` fetched from live discovery; the verifier derives four opt-outs and none is advertised. The committed bundle it replaces was a 2.3.3 cut with core-standard uncertified. What cleared the last rows, host-reported: RFC 0194 enforced in the store across instances (the cut caught a cross-instance cancel race), bulk cancel routed through the single cancel path, Cloud Run CPU always allocated (a no-op run fell from 5.75–16 s to 0.14–0.28 s), and a stale throttled revision that held delivery leases deleted. It is RFC 0194's third witness and its first from openwop-app's production host.

- **RFC 0195 returns to `Accepted` — provisional pending RFC 0156 §B review — now that its opt-out rule demonstrably fires.** MyndHyve re-cut on the published suite 2.35.1 (build `commit:bca0e9b9`, verified against the live `/readiness`, nothing relaxed, `185 / 0 / 0 / 59 / 17`, both profiles certified). The published verifier, under the key fetched from live discovery, derives exactly its three declared opt-outs from the signed rows — `family.forms`, `family.sandbox`, `family.workflowChainPacks`, all absent from live discovery — where the 2.35.0 rule derived none. Negative control: the same bundle with `forms` added to its captured document is rejected `opted-out-but-advertised`. Evidence tier: tier-2. MyndHyve's bundle is replaced by this cut. Tally **Accepted 189 · Active 2** (0111, 0121 — both Parked).

- **MyndHyve's bundle is replaced again: it now serves and witnesses the `compensation` family.** Suite 2.35.0, build `commit:bca0e9b9` verified against the live `/readiness`, nothing relaxed, `185 / 0 / 0 / 59 / 17`, both profiles certified, rung re-derived; verified by the steward with the published suite under the live key, digest matching the handover. `0173.compensation-read-projection` and `.not-found` are `executed-pass` — a production witness where only the reference host stood — and every row the previous bundle carried (RFC 0194's two, RFC 0196 §A.3, `0187.bound-id-kinds.webhook-emitted`) still passes. Opt-outs are down to three (`family.compensation` dropped); the three are absent from the live discovery document. The bundle is 2.35.0, so it derives no opt-outs under RFC 0195 §3 — the 2.35.1 fix needs a 2.35.1 cut.

- **RFC 0195 returns to `Active`, and suite 2.35.1 fixes why.** Its §3 opt-out rule derived an empty set from every real bundle — it read rows that never reach a bundle, and its self-tests used synthetic rows. Found by verifying MyndHyve's 2.35.0 bundle, which declares four opt-outs and derived none. The opted-out name now rides on the signed per-test rows. The RFC re-flips when a committed bundle shows the rule deriving real opt-outs. Tally **Accepted 188 · Active 3**.

- **RFC 0196 flips `Active → Accepted` — provisional pending RFC 0156 §B review — on a production witness.** MyndHyve now advertises `interrupt.callbackDelivery: true` and, on the published suite 2.35.0 (build `commit:ace23a7e`, verified against its live `/readiness`; nothing relaxed; `181 / 0 / 0 / 60 / 20`; both profiles certified; verified by the steward with the published suite under the live key), refuses a loopback, cloud-metadata and hex-mapped-loopback `callbackUrl` with `400 validation_error`, `details.field: callbackUrl` — `0196.callback-url-guarded` `executed-pass`, the RFC's central obligation witnessed on the only host that delivers. Evidence tier: tier-2 (steward-affiliated). The same bundle is RFC 0194's second, production witness. MyndHyve's bundle is replaced (supersedes #1464). Tally **Accepted 189 · Active 2** (0111, 0121 — both Parked).

- **RFC 0194 and RFC 0195 flip `Active → Accepted` — provisional pending RFC 0156 §B retrospective review.** Both went `Active` by steward override of RFC 0147 §A.6 (their comment windows waived), and RFC 0147 requires a cohort accepted under waived review to be reviewed cross-organization or held provisional; the register rows stay `not-reviewed`. Evidence, tier-1 single witness: the v2 reference host (a reference example, not a production host) re-cut on the published suite 2.35.0 — build `commit:8477d75`, guard closed, nothing relaxed, `246 / 0 / 0 / 8 / 7`, all three profiles certified, verified with the published suite from a clean install. It carries both RFC 0194 rows (four runs: completed ×2, failed, cancelled; and a staged duplicate delivery) and verifies under all three RFC 0195 rules. **RFC 0196 stays `Active`:** its central obligation (§A.3, a delivering host guards `callbackUrl`) has no witness — no committed host advertises `interrupt.callbackDelivery` — and accepting it on "no host advertises it" would accept a MUST nobody has shown. Tally **Accepted 188 · Active 3**.
- **RFC 0187's falsifiability table gains `0187.bound-id-kinds.webhook-emitted`** (openwop#1450), now that a committed bundle carries it.

- **The `schemaRef` descriptions in both artifact-type pack manifest schemas are aligned with their normative prose** (openwop#1465). Each still said the target schema "MUST set `additionalProperties: false`"; RFC 0075 (Accepted) relaxed that to a SHOULD, for `closed`-validation types only, in `spec/v1/artifact-type-packs.md`, and the descriptions never followed. Editorial — no obligation changes. The `$id` sentence now names the *publishing host's* `{HostBase}` and says what that is for a registry-published pack: the registry's origin, with no pack version in the `$id`. That rule was satisfiable all along; the registry's own `<pack>/<version>/<file>` convention is what has to yield for artifact-type schemas, and `core.openwop.artifact-types` 2.0.0 — published with the registry convention — is corrected in the registry.

- **MyndHyve's bundle is replaced by a 2.34.2 cut that witnesses `0172.min-client-version`.** Build `commit:9ce6969f` verified against the live `/readiness`; `175 / 0 / 0 / 60 / 20`, both profiles certified, rung re-derived; verified by the steward with the published suite under the live key, digest matching the handover. An announced client below `minClientVersion` 0.1 gets `426 client_version_unsupported` in the closed envelope. Measured with the predicate and the reference bundle removed: **3 ids in 1 RFC** (0175's negotiation rows) now rest on the reference bundle alone, down from 4 in 2 — and they stay there by MyndHyve's choice, stated: it speaks only legacy A2A/MCP versions, has no negotiation client, and does not advertise families it does not honour. The same revision carries its `callbackUrl` and egress-guard fixes.

- **Three RFCs filed `Draft`, each with its full public comment window to 2026-09-28 — and one of them late.** RFC 0147 §A.6 requires the full window, unshortened by bootstrap waiver language, for any RFC affecting identity, authorization, isolation, idempotency, replay, external effects or certification.
  - **RFC 0194** (openwop#1445) — a run's terminal event is emitted once and ends its *forward* execution; `compensation.*` and `run.dead-lettered` may follow it, as `compensation.md` requires after a cancelled parent; a fork point after the terminal event is refused. Affects replay.
  - **RFC 0196** (openwop#1449) — `callbackUrl` delivery becomes discoverable (`interrupt.callbackDelivery`), a delivering host is bound to the webhook egress guard at create time and at delivery, and an address embedding IPv4 is judged by the IPv4 it embeds. Affects external effects.
  - **RFC 0195 came late.** Its three certification rules (an unobserved requirement is `blocked` at major 2; declared relaxations inside the witness digest; opt-outs checked against the signed discovery document) were merged into `conformance.md` §Bundle v3 with suite 2.35.0 (openwop#1462) before §A.6 was applied to them. They affect certification, so their window cannot be waived: the merged sentences are now marked *proposed*, bind no host until RFC 0195 is `Active`, and suite 2.35.0 is not published before its window closes.
  - Specs and scenarios for 0194 and 0196 land when each is `Active` — a scenario may not cite a `Draft` RFC.

- **openwop-app now holds RFC 0158's `durable-single-instance` rung on its production image** — three of three hosts witness it. Suite 2.34.2 (whose transport fix openwop-app's report produced), a docker side host at the digest serving production, build `090017634c6b` verified against the live `/readiness`, a real restart supervisor, nothing relaxed; all five rows `executed-pass`: `kill-after-accept` 12.0 s against the 65 s `unleased` class, `kill-during-execution` **727.5 s against the 750 s `leased` class** — the row that had been missing, resuming by lease expiry as its terms predict. Verified by the steward with the published suite under the key fetched from live discovery. **Recorded in `INTEROP-MATRIX.md`, not committed as a bundle:** the same cut certifies no profile (`181 / 5 / 15 blocked`), so openwop-app's committed bundle is unchanged.

- **MyndHyve's bundle is replaced again the same day: it now witnesses the dead-letter sink, and each RFC 0158 kill is bound to its own recovery class.** Suite 2.34.1, build `commit:44c8d4e0` verified against the live `/readiness`, nothing relaxed, `175 / 0 / 0 / 60 / 20`, both profiles certified, rung re-derived; verified by the steward with the published suite under the live key, digest matching the handover. **`webhooks.deadLetter` is advertised and witnessed** — `0173…dead-letter` (6 assertions, no partial witness), `0188.dead-letter-read`, `0188.dead-letter-content-free` — with the host's real 15/30/60/120 s retry schedule exhausting inside `OPENWOP_WEBHOOK_RETRY_WAIT_MS=300000`: the 2.34.1 fix did what it was for, and the production schedule was not shortened to fit the instrument. **The after-accept kill now declares its own class** (`dispatch-hold`, 45 s; observed 15.4 s) instead of sharing the 100 s lease class it resumed well inside. Measured by running the acceptance predicate with the reference bundle removed: **4 ids across 2 RFCs** (0172, 0175) now rest on the reference bundle alone, down from 7 across 4. *Disclosed by the host:* an earlier attempt of this cut came back `150 / 14 / 11` during a network outage on the cutting machine (`sendmsg: network is unreachable`, DNS timeouts; every failure a transport failure, production answering throughout) and was discarded and re-run; the committed bundle is the re-run.

- **Suite 2.34.2 — a host that died on schedule failed the rows that were watching it recover.** RFC 0158's kill rows read status and log through calls that threw when the host died *after* its seam answered (`during-execution` dies at the first `node.started`); on a tier-1 host behind an accept-then-close port proxy, four of five rows failed on `other side closed`. A transport failure mid-watch is now "unreadable, poll again" (still bounded), seam rows wait for the host before probing, and suite setup waits out a restarting host instead of declaring discovery unreadable for every later file. False fail only; reported by openwop-app.

- **MyndHyve is the first PRODUCTION host to hold RFC 0158's `durable-single-instance` rung, and its bundle is replaced by the cut that says so.** Suite 2.34.1, build `commit:0259dc9e` — **verified against the live host** through its new unauthenticated `/readiness` build stamp, where every earlier MyndHyve build on this page was host-reported. Nothing relaxed; `173 / 0 / 0 / 62 / 20`; both profiles certified; `durability.rung: durable-single-instance`. Verified by the steward with the published 2.34.1 from npm under the key fetched from the live discovery document; negative controls rejected. Bound 100 000 ms in one class, `dispatch-lease` (60 s lease + 10 s redelivery + 30 s slack); observed **71.2 s** for `kill-during-execution` — the lease path the bound describes — and **16.4 s** for `kill-after-accept` under the same label, recorded as measured: inside the bound, plausibly lease-free before dispatch, and exactly the shape class-binding can make visible but not refuse. RFC 0182's list is now served and passes (`family.runList` leaves the opt-outs, five → four). Tier-2 is steward-affiliated; the rung still has no independent witness, and no host holds a higher one. `check-cut-gates` Coexistence fails on this bundle exactly as on the one it replaces (three rows `inapplicable` for surfaces the host does not serve) — unchanged, not a regression.

- **Suite 2.34.1 — a hard 90 s window convicted a host for retrying at its production pace.** `v2-webhook-durable-delivery` read the dead-letter sink ~120 s before a host retrying at 15 / 30 / 60 / 120 s (five attempts) exhausts, and recorded `executed-fail` — "MUST be routed to the sink, not dropped" — about a delivery still in flight. The window is now operator-raisable and never lowerable (`OPENWOP_WEBHOOK_RETRY_WAIT_MS`), and a window that closes before the advertised `maxAttempts` arrive records `blocked`, not a conviction; a host that exhausts and has nothing in the sink still fails. Measuring the fix found that a `blocked` after setup assertions folds into a certifying `partial-witness` pass, and that `0188.dead-letter-content-free` had been recording under another row's id whenever its sink was empty; both fixed (`blockedDespiteAssertions`, rows named up front). Reported by MyndHyve before it advertised `webhooks.deadLetter`.
- **RFC 0184 gains a host note:** a front door that decodes `%2F` needs the re-encode for every tenant-bound path parameter, not only `/runs/` — measured on a tier-1 host whose webhook `DELETE` answered 404 through its public origin and leaked every cut's subscriptions.
- **`spec/v2/core/persistence.md`'s Stable banner lists RFC 0158 §A–§D** — the line the RFC's flip owed and could not make under an already-published package version.

- **RFC 0158 — durable execution and disaster-recovery qualification — flips `Active → Accepted`. Tier-1, single witness, and the entry says what that is worth.** The reference host was re-cut on the published suite 2.34.0 (build `commit:2e6e42d`, egress guard closed, nothing relaxed, `242 / 0 / 0 / 6 / 7`), and its bundle is the first from any host to **carry** the rung: `durability.rung: durable-single-instance`, re-derived by the verifier from signed rows — class `single-instance-restart`, bound 6 000 ms = 1 000 (supervisor restart, the operator's term) + 5 000 (boot re-entry budget), resumption observed at 1 787 ms and 1 883 ms after a real `SIGKILL`. Verified in a clean directory against the suite installed from npm; a tampered interval is rejected (`witness-digest`) and a higher rung refused (`rung-not-derivable`). Criterion 1 was ticked only once a bundle carried the fields — a field no bundle has carried is a schema, not a publication. **Not witnessed by anyone:** peer resume, `durable-multi-instance`, `multi-region-qualified`, and the lowest rung on any *production* host — openwop-app holds four of five rows on a local lane with no certified bundle, MyndHyve claims no rung. `bound-is-derived` is a paper check; class-binding cannot refuse a wrong-but-declared class that resumes inside the longer bound. Tally: **Accepted 186 · Active 2** (0111, 0121 — both Parked).
- **Owed to the next release:** `spec/v2/core/persistence.md`'s Stable banner does not list RFC 0158. It could not before today (a Stable banner may cite only `Accepted` RFCs) and it is not edited now, because the file ships inside the already-published `@openwop/spec-artifacts@2.34.0` and a version must keep identifying its contents.
- **The certifying cut died twice before the suite started, and the cause was the cut script.** `cut-public.sh` looked a tunnel name up before its A record existed; Google Public DNS cached the empty answer for the zone's 1 800 s negative TTL, and with no IPv6 route the name stayed unresolvable while three healthy tunnels sat open beside it. The four earlier cuts were lucky, not correct. Fixed in openwop-examples#66 (settle, probe a resolver the host never reads, fail fast on a poisoned cache).

- **MyndHyve's evidence bundle is replaced by its first STRICT cut — suite 2.33.2, from the deployed revision, nothing relaxed.** 165 pass / 0 fail / 0 blocked / 66 inapplicable / 24 skipped, 1739 assertions, `openwop-discovery-core` and `openwop-core-standard` certified. The committed bundle had been cut on 2.3.3, before `--require-behavior` did anything, and carried a `0171.webhook-delivery-shape` pass now known to have been vacuous. **Verified by the steward rather than accepted on handover:** file sha256 matched; the signature verifies under the public key *fetched from the live discovery document* and is `REJECTED` under the retired key; the five declared opt-out families are absent from the captured AND the live document; a secrets scan of the file is clean. The build commit is host-reported only — that endpoint answers `401` unauthenticated — and the matrix row says so. No retirement date moved. Re-measured: nothing rests on MyndHyve's bundle alone; 7 requirement ids across 4 RFCs (0172, 0173, 0175, 0182) rest on the reference bundle alone, each because MyndHyve does not advertise the surface. The cut surfaced two real host defects (both fixed and deployed) and three suite false-fails (2.33.1, 2.33.2).

- **`INTEROP-MATRIX.md`'s openwop-app rung row went stale within the hour it was written, and is corrected against the live host rather than on report.** The host deployed its effect-fencing fix (`build.commit` `27315b41c8f4`, `deployedAt` 2026-09-21T12:34:42Z — fetched from `/api/readiness`), so "not deployed" was wrong; `duplicate-delivery` is `executed-pass` on its local lane; and `kill-during-execution` is recorded as what it is — two passes at 726.8 s, then a fail on 2.32.0 from the suite's own read-order race, not yet re-run on 2.33.1 — so the rung stays **not held** and the row is not upgraded on a report. The kill seam answers 404 in production on both majors (fetched): built and deliberately unmounted. Suite 2.33.2 is published.

- **The reference host's evidence bundle is replaced by the first cut it has ever had with nothing relaxed, and the undeclared-relaxation entry in `docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md` closes.** Suite 2.33.1, build `commit:a084218`, **egress guard closed, `host.relaxations` empty**: 242 pass / 0 fail / 0 blocked, 2014 assertions, all three profiles certified, all ten `check-cut-gates` predicate groups passing, `check-accepted-predicate` still 27 of 27. It carries **all 8** requirement ids the superseded bundle was the sole witness for (RFCs 0175, 0182, 0187, 0188), so those RFCs are re-grounded and none was reverted. The suite checked the posture itself — eight destinations the guard MUST refuse, eight refusals. **Cutting relaxation-free found what seven relaxed cuts could not:** the reference host had never been able to reach *any* hostname (a custom DNS `lookup` in the wrong shape; only IP literals worked), and two suite defects reachable only from that posture (2.33.0, 2.33.1). The host's default cut and both CI legs are now declared regression lanes that refuse to certify.

- **RFC 0158's `durable-single-instance` rung is witnessed, and RFC 0158 stays `Active`.** Tier-1, the v2 reference host: five rows `executed-pass` under a real `SIGKILL`, the restart supervisor itself counting 2 deaths, `duplicate-delivery` one arrival at a receiver the suite owns; declared bound 6 000 ms, the supervisor's share declared as the operator's. Acceptance criterion 2 is ticked and criterion 4 with it (`INTEROP-MATRIX.md` gains the per-host rung-and-bound table; its RFC 0158 section had read `Draft` long after the RFC was `Active`). **The flip was prepared and then stopped on reading criterion 1:** §E publishes the rung and the bound *in the evidence bundle instead of advertising them*, and `certification-bundle.schema.json` has no field for either; `replay.md` and `capabilities.md`, both named in `Affects`, carry no text. Counting boxes said ready; reading them said not yet. No production host has witnessed the rung.
- **Suite 2.33.2 — `v2-run-snapshot-etag` was a timing lottery that failed a correct production host.** It slept 1 s after creating a run, took the snapshot's `ETag` and demanded `304`; a host whose strong ETag tracks the event-log sequence is still appending while a noop executes, so `200` with a new tag is the right answer and `304` would be a stale-cache bug. Measured 4 of 4 on a tier-2 production host, which had passed the row three cuts running and failed the fourth. The scenario now waits for the run to be terminal and fails only on the real violation — a `200` whose ETag *equals* the `If-None-Match` sent. False fail only.

- **Suite 2.33.1 — a conformant host failed RFC 0158's `kill-during-execution` on a race between two reads.** `v2-durability-recovery` read a run's log, then its status, and concluded "completed without re-execution" from a status newer than the log it was judged against; a host that recovered between the two requests failed, with a message that printed the very re-execution it denied. Measured on a tier-1 host after a real SIGKILL and a correct 727 s lease-expiry recovery. Status is now read first and the log second — a log read after a `completed` status can only add evidence — and the ordering is unit-tested without a host. **False fails only, never false passes**, so passing rows on 2.32.0 / 2.33.0 stand. Introduced in 2.32.0 by the change that made the row assert both clauses of §E item 11.

- **Suite 2.33.1 also fixes the one failing row of the first relaxation-free cut.** `v2-webhook-delivery-shape` never unregistered its subscriptions. Invisible on loopback, where each leg has its own ephemeral port; behind a public front every leg shares one URL, so leg 1's live major-2 subscription delivered its v2 rendering into the major-1 leg, which failed a host that had rendered both contracts correctly. Reproduced without any ingress by pinning the receiver port on a loopback run; fixed by unregistering per leg and by refusing a delivery that names another subscription. **A relaxed cut and a relaxation-free cut exercise different suite code** — this, like the reference host's broken hostname egress, was only reachable from the posture nobody had ever run.

- **The steward's reference host has been certifying under a relaxation its bundles never declared, and the steward found it on the way to flipping RFC 0158.** `security-defaults.md` §Relaxations requires every relaxation a host runs under to be recorded in `host.relaxations[]` and forbids certifying the profile it belongs to. The v2 reference host cuts with `OPENWOP_WEBHOOK_ALLOW_PRIVATE=1` — *"required, not optional"*, because every suite fixture is a loopback listener — and every committed reference bundle records no relaxation and `certified: true` on all three profiles. It was recorded in the host's prose; the rule says the bundle. **Measured reach: 8 requirement ids across 4 RFCs (0175, 0182, 0187, 0188) are witnessed by that bundle alone**; the other 23 v2-era Accepted RFCs are also witnessed by MyndHyve's bundle, which was cut through a public `https` receiver and relaxes nothing. No status is reverted — the RFC 0165 precedent is to re-ground, and no measurement calls a requirement into question — and **RFC 0158 stays `Active`**: its fresh 2.32.0 cut certified and was not committed. Three suite defects surfaced with it and are recorded in `docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md`: a host advertising `a2a` or `mcp` **cannot** be cut relaxation-free, because those fakes bind loopback with no public-front override; a **declared** relaxation denies nothing on any real host, because the verifier matches the obligation against the profile *id* and no real id contains a family name; and the egress guard's refusal is **unwitnessed at major 2** in every bundle, its scenarios having never left the major-1 lane.

- **The RFC status index counted a `Superseded` RFC as awaiting repository work.** `generate-protocol-status.mjs` bucketed every non-`Accepted`, non-Parked RFC as pending, so when RFC 0035 became the corpus's first `Superseded` RFC the index read "2 await repository work" with one RFC (0158) actually open. The sentence now counts only `Draft` and `Active` RFCs — `Superseded`, `Withdrawn` and `Rejected` are terminal — and agrees in number ("1 awaits"). `RFCS/README.md` regenerated: 4 open, 3 Parked, 1 awaiting work.

- **A declared facet and the advertisable wire surface could drift in both directions, and nothing compared them.** The facet properties in `schemas/v2/capabilities.schema.json` come from a hand-decided `spec/v2/facets/<key>.schema.json` override, or are **seeded from v1** when there is none — they are not derived from `declaration.facets`. `check-declaration.mjs` rule 12 now compares the two sets per family. A facet **on the wire but not declared** is always a failure: it carries no RFC 0189 coverage obligation, so nothing ever demands v2 text for something a host can advertise — which is exactly how `workflowChainPacks.deferredParameters` and `.hostExpansionSeam` survived until 2.8.2, and why removing them from the declaration alone would have made the gate green while leaving both advertisable. A facet **declared but not on the wire** fails *unless* a `peer-dependency-aliases.json` row explains it: `secrets.resolveInPack` is a legitimate case of that shape — three published registry packs name it, its alias row carries `removalTrigger: v1-end-of-support`, and the alias generator builds its family map from `declaration.facets`, so deleting the entry would make the alias `unresolved`. Sabotage-proven in both directions; the clean tree reports 72 facet sets compared, 1 alias-anchored.

- **The three surfaces a third-party implementer touches first were all broken, and they are the tier-3 tripwire the governance model waits on.** (1) The **npm package page** — the corpus's actual front door — told the world to install `2.0.0-rc.57` while `latest` was 2.4.5, and declared *"Tracks the FINAL v1 protocol contract."* Both corrected, and `--legacy-peer-deps` is now documented as REQUIRED rather than a footnote, with the npm 10.9 failure named (`Cannot read properties of null (reading 'edgesOut')` — use npm ≥ 11). (2) **INTEROP-MATRIX §"Add A Host" was dead v1 text** — *"Implement the openwop v1 wire contract… publish a result file or Markdown summary"* — with no bundle, no signing, no `--certify`. Rewritten as the actual v2 path, pointing at `docs/IMPLEMENTER-PATH.md`. (3) **`check-cut-gates.mjs` — advertised to newcomers in three places as their self-check — could not pass for a newcomer**: it requires an INTEROP-MATRIX row that only exists after the PR that adds it, and returned a bare failure. A missing row is now `blocked` with the reason *"PRE-REGISTRATION, which is expected before your first submission… Nothing about your host is failing here."*
- **A newcomer had no way to produce a publishable signing key.** The suite signs with a PKCS8 private key; hosts publish the raw 32-byte public key as base64url-unpadded (exactly 43 characters) in their own `signingKeys[]`. Nothing bridged the two, and every `openssl genpkey` recipe in the repo belongs to the *pack* surface and emits SPKI, which fails the pattern. The consequence was silent: a bundle whose key is absent or malformed **attests integrity only** — it proves nothing about who signed it. `docs/IMPLEMENT-CORE.md` now carries a verified two-command recipe and states why the trust root is self-asserted: a verifier resolves `keyId` in the host's own discovery document, so there is no key list the steward keeps and no approval step.
- **`docs/IMPLEMENTATION-CERTIFICATION.md` and `docs/PROFILE-DECISION-GUIDE.md` handed out profile names deleted at the v2 cut** — `openwop-core`, `openwop-stream-sse`, `openwop-stream-poll`. The first is named in the README as *how a host publishes a conformance claim*. Both now carry a banner naming the three v2 profiles and pointing at the v2 on-ramps, and are kept only for hosts still serving v1 through the overlap.
- **`docs/IMPLEMENTER-PATH.md` gains a pointer to RFC 0180 §A.2**, the self-serve vendor-org registration. It was discoverable only from a `$comment` inside `declaration.json` and the RFC itself, and its bar — *disambiguation, not endorsement*, with an anti-discrimination clause — is worth saying where an implementer will see it.

- **The reference host's bundle is re-cut on 2.4.5 — the first cut in which `--require-behavior` did anything.** `231 / 0 / 0 / 7 / 7`, all three profiles certified. The first genuinely strict run returned **7 `executed-fail` rows**, none of them a protocol defect: strict mode requires a capability-gated scenario to find its family advertised **or** find an explicit opt-out, and the host had neither for `family.forms`, `family.memory` and `connections.packsSupported`. It had been soft-skipping all three silently. The three are now DECLARED, which is why `skipped` reads 7 — an opt-out is a claim a reader can audit in the bundle, not a silence. Every INTEROP-MATRIX row that names the flag on an earlier cut recorded a command that ran non-strict, and the matrix now says so.
- **RFC 0173 §B's webhook-durability row was one row claiming two halves.** It named a single id for "retry + dead-letter", so the retry pass stood in for both and the sink half read as witnessed on every bundle in the corpus. Split in two: retry keeps `witnessable — gated`; **dead-letter is `negative-existence`**, because `openwop.requirement.0173.webhook-durable-delivery.dead-letter` ends in an unconditional soft-skip on every host — `api/v2/openapi.yaml` serves no dead-letter read, and the `deadLetter` capability family declares `retentionDays` that nothing reads. **Recorded limitation, not papered over:** 2.4.5 is the first suite that annotates the leg `partial-witness:` and rule 4 refuses such a row, but MyndHyve's cited bundle was cut on 2.1.7 and carries an un-annotated pass — so the gate still accepts it from there, and this row reads honestly only once every cited bundle is re-cut on 2.4.5 or later.

- **RFC 0167 — the v2 umbrella — flips `Active → Accepted`, last of the program.** Both halves of its criterion hold: all eleven children (0168–0178) are `Accepted`, and §G.2 reports `0 failed, 0 blocked` with all ten predicate groups passing against the reference host's certified bundle. It flips last by rule, not by convention — a parent that is `Accepted` is no longer `Active`, and RFC 0174 §B.1 requires a program child's parent to be `Active` at the child's flip, so any child still awaiting one would be stranded permanently. The gate enforces this since 2.4.2.
- **Two things §G.2's green does not mean, recorded in the RFC so no one reads more into it.** It passes on the *reference host's* bundle only — run against either production host's committed bundle it fails several groups, because openwop-app's is uncertified and MyndHyve's measured suite 2.1.7 and predates five of the 79 scenario files applicable at major 2. And its Witness group audits the 140 requirement ids the generated registry declares; before 2.4.3 the same gate harvested only literal `req('…')` call sites and audited 113, so any earlier green §G.2 covered less than this one does.
- **The v2 program is now fully `Accepted`: 179 of 184 RFCs.** Three remain `Active` — RFC 0111, 0121 and 0158, none of them v2-era — and RFC 0035 is the corpus's first `Superseded`. No v2-era RFC is `Active` any more.

- **RFC 0173 flips `Active → Accepted`, and RFC 0035 becomes the corpus's first `Superseded` RFC** — both in one PR, as RFC 0174 §A.1 requires and RFC 0001's timing rule allows (`Superseded` is flipped *when the replacement reaches `Accepted`*). Gap G1 closes on exactly the condition it named. **Said plainly rather than elided: 0035's tripwire asked for a NON-STEWARD host, and it is retired by replacement, not satisfied.** `openwop.requirement.0173.pack-isolation` is `executed-pass` across ten legs on the steward's own reference host and `inapplicable — profile not advertised` on both production hosts; no non-steward host ever advertised `capabilities.sandbox.supported`. The seven protocol-tier `node-pack-sandbox-*` invariants and the four `sandbox_*` error codes keep RFC 0035 as their v1 citation through the overlap to 2026-12-04, which is why this flip waited for its replacement instead of leading it.
- **Naming the standing v1 authorities in a `Supersedes` cell made RFC 0173 claim to supersede nine Accepted RFCs.** `check-rfc-status-coherence.mjs` harvests *every* four-digit number in that cell, and the leading `—` was the only thing suppressing it; removing the dash to name RFC 0035 produced eight spurious failures at once. The cell now names 0035 and nothing else, and the "leaves standing" list moved to `Affects`, where no supersession rule reads it.
- **RFC 0173's two remaining host clauses fail for opposite reasons, and the acceptance says which.** `webhook-durable-delivery` is `blocked` on openwop-app because production **correctly refuses** to call back to a loopback receiver — unobservable there, not unmet — while `compensation-read-projection` is `inapplicable` on both production hosts because neither advertises the profile. Only the reference host witnesses the second.

- **RFC 0168, 0170, 0175, 0176 and 0177 flip `Active → Accepted`** — the five whose criteria name a clause openwop-app does not satisfy. Each keeps the clause's text and records an explicit reason rather than re-wording the criterion to fit the evidence. **0170 needed no reason for its host clause**: all four ids it names are `executed-pass` on openwop-app's own bundle. **0175's three remaining ids cannot be witnessed there by construction** — openwop-app advertises neither the `a2a` nor the `mcp` facet, so `refresh-sla` and one `legacy-profiles-absent` leg record `inapplicable` with that reason and `negotiation-decided-emitted` has no row at all; MyndHyve advertises neither either, so the reference host is the only witness. **0176 and 0177 turn on the distinction `GOVERNANCE.md` insists on** — *the seam is not mounted* is not the same fact as *the seam does not exist*. openwop-app has built the seams profile and does not mount it on the production revision, so it can witness those ids on a tagged side revision; that is a deployment choice, not a build gap, and the reason says so. **0168's last clause says openwop-app's bundle *verifies*, and it does** — its attestation verifies under the host's published key — while it does not *certify*: 3 blocked rows leave both profiles `certified: false` under 0168's own §E.1, so it supplies no §B.1 witness.
- **RFC 0177 is the only v2 child that no single host witnesses.** `provider-conflict` is `executed-pass` on MyndHyve only — the reference host does not advertise the connections profile — while the pack-manifest ids are `executed-pass` on the reference host and `inapplicable` on MyndHyve. Its acceptance cites both certified bundles because neither alone satisfies the falsifiability table.
- **The registry's corpus pin was bumped to `v2.4.3` before RFC 0177's "schemas pinned" clause was ticked** (openwop-registry#67). It stood at `v2.4.1`; no vendored file actually changed, but ticking that clause against a two-release-stale pin would have claimed something unchecked.

- **RFC 0171, 0172, 0179 and 0187 flip `Active → Accepted`** — the four that carry no reservation. **0171 and 0172 cite `tier-2`**, the higher independence tier: every declared requirement id is `executed-pass` on MyndHyve's certified bundle, a genuinely separate production deployment, and on the reference host. **0179 is the only acceptance in the corpus a stranger can re-derive with `curl`**: its advertisement half was checked against the live `/.well-known/openwop` on both production hosts at 2026-09-18T06:08Z, as `GOVERNANCE.md` §"Deployed, not merged" requires. **0187 cites `tier-1` with one witness only** and says so — MyndHyve's bundle (suite 2.1.7) and openwop-app's (2.0.11) predate the scenario file, so neither carries a row, not even `inapplicable`.
- **Every `Updated` field states the reference host's provenance rather than eliding it.** That host is tier-1 by `GOVERNANCE.md` §"Acceptance evidence tiers" (a reference host in `openwop-examples`), but its bundle's `discovery.url` is `http://127.0.0.1:3838` — it is run from the pinned example and does not serve traffic. §"Deployed, not merged" says *"a host is a thing that serves traffic"*, so no acceptance here calls it deployed. Where a criterion is a live-surface claim, it is paired with a dated production fetch instead.
- **Two acceptance criteria named files that do not exist.** RFC 0171 cited `errors.json` at the schemas path; the error registry is `spec/v2/errors.json` and `schemas/v2/error-envelope.schema.json` is the generated half. RFC 0172 cited `spec/v2/operation-path-manifest.json`, which has never existed; the operation set is `spec/v2/path-manifest.json`. Both corrected in the same edit that ticks the box.
- **`INTEROP-MATRIX.md`'s MyndHyve row had drifted from the bundle it describes.** It read suite `2.0.4`, build `commit:0dbdddf44`, `136 / 0 / 88 / 0 / 0`, and called `openwop-core-standard` unclaimed; the committed bundle says suite `2.1.7`, build `commit:46917218d19f`, `149 / 0 / 0 / 82 / 0`, and claims both profiles `certified: true`. Re-derived from the bundle, with two disclosures added: the measured origin is the Cloud Run URL rather than `api.myndhyve.ai`, and the bundle's suite predates five of the 79 scenario files applicable at major 2, so its totals say nothing about them.

- **RFC 0173's gap G1 described a resolution that would retire a still-live authority.** It read "`open` — flipped `Superseded` with a forward pointer in the Phase 3 PR", which reads as a standalone step. Executed as one it would orphan live v1 normative text: RFC 0035 is still the cited authority for four error codes in `rest-endpoints.md` (`sandbox_memory_exceeded`, `sandbox_timeout`, `sandbox_capability_denied`, `sandbox_escape_attempt`), **seven protocol-tier** `node-pack-sandbox-*` rows in `SECURITY/invariants.yaml`, and seam §8 in `host-sample-test-seams.md` — and v1 is served until 2026-12-04. RFC 0001 fixes the timing (`Superseded` is flipped *when the replacement reaches `Accepted`*) and RFC 0174 §A.1 requires both edits in one PR, so the resolution now names RFC 0173's own `Active → Accepted` PR and records what else must move with it, including stripping the `(**Parked**)` annotation that `rfc-lifecycle-coherence` refuses on a non-`Draft`/`Active` RFC. The disposition stays `open`, which is the only honest token while the condition is 0173's own flip. **RFC 0173's header was right all along:** its `Supersedes` cell begins with `—`, which RFC 0001 defines as a partial amendment and not a supersession, so nothing in the corpus currently claims 0035 is superseded.

- **RFC 0167 §G.2 audited 113 of 166 requirement ids and said nothing about the rest.** `check-cut-gates.mjs` harvested ids with a regex over `req('…')` **literals**, so every id a scenario hoists into a `const` — about thirty v2 files use that idiom, including `v2-bound-id-kinds`, `v2-run-cancel` and `v2-payload-seats-0186` — was invisible: the gate never demanded those rows of any host. §G.2 is the predicate that decides a host is certifiable for the v2 cut, so a gate that silently measures a subset is the same failure shape as rule 4's vacuous pass before 2.4.2. It now resolves ids through the generated registry, which already resolves module-level `const` bindings, and audits **140** ids on the same bundle with no new gap. A new gate row pins the two harvests together: a literal `req()` id the registry does not know is minted outside an `it()`, which means no bundle can ever carry a row for it while the gate demands one — the inverse divergence, and exactly how §G.2 failed on `…0173.pack-isolation.seam`. The INTEROP-MATRIX row now states what §G.2 measures rather than claiming it "passes completely".

- **RFC 0174 §B.1 rule 4 refused a corpus that had done everything right.** The rule demanded an `executed-pass` row for every requirement id a falsifiability table names, and 2.4.2 added the row-level rule that a row be "id-witnessed **or** verdict-declared". The two do not compose: a row that is BOTH — an id plus a declared verdict like `witnessable — gated` — cleared the row rule and then fell into the id rule, which demanded the pass anyway. RFC 0177 §E.4's `form-when-reuses-edge-conditions` is the proof: both certified bundles record it `inapplicable` with the reason "profile not advertised in the captured discovery set", `check-cut-gates.mjs` §G.2 accepts exactly that, and rule 4 refused the flip regardless. The pressure that creates is to mint a vacuous leg so the gate goes green — the precise defect 2.4.2 was cut to close. §B.1 now accounts for an id **either** by an `executed-pass` row **or**, when the verdict cell declares a non-executable witness class (`seam-gated`, `negative-existence`, `unwitnessable`, `witnessable — gated`), by a non-pass row on a **certified** bundle that carries a reason. What it never admits is silence: strip the reason from both certified bundles and the id is refused again, which is how this was proved.
- **`check-falsifiability.mjs` and `check-accepted-predicate.mjs` disagreed about which RFCs they govern.** The predicate enforces rule 4 against every RFC ≥ 0167; the falsifiability gate validated verdicts only for the RFC 0167 family, so RFC 0179 and 0187 had their tables enforced by one gate and unchecked by the other. RFC 0187's table was three columns with verdicts outside the closed vocabulary and nothing said so. The boundary is now RFC ≥ 0167 for both, which took strict validation from 12 tables to 21 and immediately found RFC 0183 (Accepted) carrying a three-column table — converted here. The verdict vocabulary also now lets every class carry a trailing reason, as `unwitnessable` always could: a `seam-gated` row that names the seam is more useful than a bare token, and refusing the reason pushed authors toward the bare one.
- **RFC 0187's three unwitnessed ids are re-verdicted rather than back-filled with legs.** §B.1 (a v2-only type passes through on the v1 wire) is `seam-gated`: the era-seed seam pins `eventLogSchemaVersion: { const: 2 }`, so no suite can seed the era-`3` log the rule is about. §C.1 (eligibility binds every writer) is `unwitnessable`: a census of writers is a property of the host's storage topology, and a black-box probe reaches a record only through routes — so it cannot enumerate the writers that are not routes, which is exactly the fourth writer the rule exists to catch. §D.1 (the legacy writer marks the row) is `negative-existence`: §Unresolved deliberately leaves the mark's wire spelling undecided, and a requirement with no declared spelling cannot have a wire witness. Writing three vacuous legs to turn those rows green would have been the dishonest fix.
- **RFC 0187's motivation named a mapped type as its example of an unmapped one.** It cited `interrupt.requested` as a type with no `event-codemap.json` row. Measured: the codemap carries a row for every one of the 118 members of `run-event.schema.json`'s `type` enum, `interrupt.requested` included, and `event-codemap-complete.test.ts` asserts that bijection at the corpus gate. The rule's real subjects are registered vendor-org types and any future 119th core type seated without a row; the paragraph now says so.

### Changed

- **`auth-profiles.md` OIDC `aud`: the SHOULD restatement is merged into the existing MUST** — a token whose `aud` does not include the host's configured audience MUST be rejected with `401 unauthenticated`; the OAuth2-CC section states the same `401` for the wrong-audience case it already listed, without requiring `aud` to be present. Class 3 correction, no requirement moved (COMPATIBILITY.md §3 entry, 2026-09-22).
- **RFC 0180, 0185 and 0186 flip `Active → Accepted`** — the first flips computed by the RFC 0174 §B.1 predicate since 2.4.2 closed its five holes, and the only three of the fourteen Active v2-era RFCs that clear it on committed evidence. 0185 (`openwop.requirement.0185.payload-vendor-hatch`) and 0186 (`openwop.requirement.0186.payload-seats`) cite `tier-1 — steward-verified`: both ids are `executed-pass` on the reference host's **certified** bundle (suite 2.4.1, witness `b8a7d1d6941d…`). 0180 cites `corpus gate — no host tier`, which is the honest label rather than a convenience — every obligation it carries is a property of the corpus and the registry declaration, so no host bundle can witness it and none is cited. All three gained a `### Falsifiability` table, because 2.4.2's rule 4 no longer passes an RFC that names nothing to check: each row is now either id-witnessed or verdict-declared, including 0180 §A.4's deregistration rule, which is witnessable **in the negative only** — the absence of a removal procedure is the requirement.
- **The remaining eleven stay `Active`, each for a stated reason** rather than for lack of attention. The eight RFC 0167 program children name a certified openwop-app bundle in their own acceptance criteria and the committed one reads `certified: false` (3 blocked rows, RFC 0168 §E.1). RFC 0173 additionally carries the program's only `open` gap row. RFC 0179's sole criterion is unmet — 2.4.2 stopped the gate reading "(Phase 4 leg)" as an excuse for it. RFC 0187 names three requirement ids that no scenario mints. RFC 0167 itself flips **last**, which 2.4.2 made a rule: the moment the umbrella stops being `Active`, every child still `Active` becomes permanently ineligible.
- **The interrupt `data` union is bound to `kind`** (MCP/A2A review P3-H7, Class 3 correction — COMPATIBILITY.md §3). `schemas/suspend-request.schema.json` and its v2 twin typed `data` as an unbound `oneOf`, so the minimal `conversation.start` and `conversation.close` payloads (`{ conversationId }`, which satisfies both shapes) failed validation in v1 and v2, and a payload carrying another kind's `data` passed. `data` is now an `anyOf` and a root `allOf` binds each kind to its own shape with one `if`/`then`; no per-kind shape or `$id` changed. Witnessed by `conformance/fixtures/interrupt-payloads/` (suite 2.36.0).

## [2.35.0] — 2026-09-21 — blocked means blocked, the bundle's declarations are signed, and a run ends once

- **RFCs 0194, 0195 and 0196 are `Active` by steward override of RFC 0147 §A.6.** Each affects a risk class (replay, certification, external effects) whose comment window §A.6 says bootstrap waiver language MUST NOT shorten. The steward waived the windows anyway on 2026-09-21; each RFC's `Updated` line and the `MAINTAINERS.md` ledger record it as an override outside the waiver grant, not as a routine waiver.
- **RFC 0194 — `spec/v2/core/events.md` §The terminal event and a `replay.md` fork-point clause.** One terminal run event per run, no forward execution after it (compensation and dead-lettering excepted), and a fork point after the terminal event is refused `422 fork_point_invalid`. Scenario `v2-terminal-event-once`. Closes the gap in openwop#1445.
- **RFC 0196 — `interrupt.callbackDelivery`, `interrupt.md` §Callback delivery, and the embedded-IPv4 rule in `webhooks.md` §SSRF.** A host that does not deliver to `callbackUrl` SHOULD refuse it; one that advertises delivery MUST hold it to the webhook egress guard at create time and at delivery. An IPv4-mapped IPv6 address is judged by the IPv4 it embeds (MUST); other translation forms SHOULD be, without denying NAT64 or 6to4 wholesale. Scenario `v2-callback-url-guarded`, invariant `callback-url-egress-guarded`. Addresses openwop#1449.
- **RFC 0195's rules in `conformance.md` §Bundle v3 are no longer marked proposed.**

### Security

- **The webhook egress refusal row now includes addresses written as IPv4-mapped IPv6.** A URL parser emits them in hex, and a guard that recognises only the dotted mapped form lets loopback, cloud metadata and RFC 1918 through. They are covered by `webhooks.md` §SSRF as written; the row enforces an existing MUST. See `conformance/CHANGELOG.md`.

### Changed

- **`spec/v2/core/conformance.md` §Bundle v3:** at major 2 a requirement a test did not observe records `blocked` even after setup assertions; `witnessSha256` covers `host.relaxations[]` whenever it is non-empty; a verifier derives opt-outs from the signed `skipped` rows and rejects a bundle whose captured discovery document advertises one. Additive — every committed bundle verifies unchanged.
- **RFC 0187** records the emitted half of §A.1 (`0187.bound-id-kinds.webhook-emitted`, openwop#1450) as a later addition, joining its falsifiability table once a committed bundle carries it.

## [2.34.0] — 2026-09-21 — the rung and the recovery bound, in the bundle RFC 0158 said they were in

### Added

- **The certification bundle gains a seat for RFC 0158's rung and recovery bounds.** §E.10 mints no discovery capability and publishes both in the bundle instead — "where a reader can recompute it" — and until now `schemas/v2/certification-bundle.schema.json` had no member for a rung, a bound, or its terms. A requirement row gains an OPTIONAL closed `evidence` object (`recovery { class, boundMs, observedMs }` on the two kill rows; `recoveryBounds[] { class, bound, terms[{ name, ms }] }` on `bound-is-derived`, per class, no aggregate), and the root gains an OPTIONAL `durability { rung }`. Evidence is on **rows** because the attestation covers the witness digest and the digest covers rows; it enters the digest only when present, so every bundle already cut verifies unchanged. The rung is a **claim the verifier re-derives** and rejects when it cannot (`rung-not-derivable`). Suite 2.34.0; see `conformance/CHANGELOG.md`.
- **RFC 0158 §A–§D has a v2 normative home: `spec/v2/core/persistence.md` §"Durable acceptance and recovery"**, and `spec/v2/core/conformance.md` §"Recovery evidence" states the bundle fields and the verifier's derivation. Restatement only — no new obligation. Its v1 homes (`idempotency.md`, `storage-adapters.md`) retire with v1 on 2026-12-04, and until now v2 core named the RFC once, as a "model" in `security-defaults.md`.

### Changed

- **RFC 0158's `Affects` line is amended to what the RFC affects.** It named `spec/v1/replay.md` and `capabilities.md`; the RFC never gave either anything to carry — no clause of §A–§D touches replay or fork, and §E.10 mints no capability, so there is nothing to advertise. Dropped with a dated note rather than filled with text written to satisfy a header. **The RFC stays `Active`:** criterion 1's fields and text now exist, but no host bundle carries them until a host is re-cut on the published 2.34.0.
- Additive under `COMPATIBILITY.md`: a closed schema gains optional members, and an older verifier meeting a bundle that carries them fails closed on the witness digest.

## [2.33.0] — 2026-09-21 — a declared relaxation denied nothing, an undeclared one was invisible, and two fakes made both unavoidable

### Fixed

- **The three suite defects behind the reference host's undeclared relaxation** (`docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md`; disclosed in #1446). `security-defaults.md` §Relaxations says a relaxation MUST be recorded and its profile MUST NOT certify. In practice: (1) a host advertising `a2a` or `mcp` **could not** be cut without relaxing its egress guard, because those fakes had no public front — so the rule made a host implementing more of the protocol less able to certify; (2) a **declared** relaxation denied nothing on any real host, because the verifier matched the obligation against the profile *id* and the emitter against the whole obligation string, and no real profile id contains a family name; (3) an **undeclared** one was invisible, because no major-2 row asserted the egress guard refuses anything. All three are closed: both fakes take a validated public front; ownership is read from `spec/v2/profiles.json` `predicate.families` by one function the emitter and verifier share; and the new `v2-webhook-egress-refusal` both witnesses `webhooks.md` §SSRF at major 2 and turns an open, undeclared guard into a denial of every profile built on `webhooks`.

  The pinning scenario for (2) had been green throughout because its fixture profile was named `openwop-webhooks` — the one id the broken rule could match. **Restoring the old rule leaves that leg green and reddens only the new one, asserted against the real `openwop-core-standard`.**

  Measured on the reference host: guarded → pass; `OPENWOP_WEBHOOK_ALLOW_PRIVATE=1`, nothing declared → **fail, accepted 8 of 8** (the flag opens the scheme check and the cloud-metadata address, not only loopback); same flag, declared → `inapplicable` with the owning profile denied. **This release is expected to un-certify a loopback cut; a host cut through public fronts is unaffected.** No spec, schema or wire change. RFC 0158 stays `Active` until the reference host is re-cut relaxation-free on this suite.

## [2.32.0] — 2026-09-20 — every published v2 pack failed the corpus schema, and two kill rows that mandated a fast bound

### Fixed

- **No published v2 pack validated against the corpus's own bare-manifest schemas — 0 of 190** (openwop#1367). `spec/v2/core/packs.md` §Signing and RFC 0177 §C.3 fix one scheme, `{ keyId, scheme }`; migration row `openwop.migration.C10.1` renames `signing.publicKeyRef` → `signing.keyId` **in the pack manifest**; and the registry re-signed every v2 tarball that way. But `v2-one-signing-scheme` pinned only `registry-version-manifest`, so six bare manifests (`artifact-type`, `chat-card`, `form-content`, `node`, `prompt`, `workflow-chain`) kept the v1 `{ publicKeyRef, signatureRef, method }` block, closed, and `connection-pack-manifest` had no `signing` seat at all. openwop-app rejected 16 of 16 registry-installed chain packs at production boot (its ADR 0713) before working around it. All seven now carry the identical closed block. `signing` stays **OPTIONAL** on a bare manifest — an authoring-time `pack.json` exists before it is signed. The issue named five schemas; there were seven. No new RFC: this is the schema catching up to an `Accepted` RFC's own migration row.
