- **A v2 chain node could carry no config at all.** `derive-v2-schemas.mjs` closes "an explicit open object with declared properties". `FragmentNode.config` documents one key (`subChainRef`, RFC 0133) on a free-form map its own description calls "host-validated against the referenced typeId's config schema", so the seed closed it — while `WorkflowNode.config`, the node a fragment **expands into**, stayed open. 73 of 81 published chain packs failed on it. Reopened. v2's closure is otherwise deliberate (RFC 0167 Axiom 2) and is not touched: of the 88 objects open in v1 and closed in v2, this is the one whose closure contradicts the schema's own prose **and** the published corpus.

  | published v2 `pack.json` validating | before | after |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | all kinds | **0 / 190** | **185 / 190** |

  The five that remain fail on constraints **identical in v1** (a node `category` outside the enum, three connection packs missing `provider.apiHosts`, one artifact-type pack using inline `schema` for `schemaRef`). That is registry data, not a schema defect, and it is reported to the registry rather than absorbed here. **The measurement is the method:** the published registry is the only corpus of real v2 documents there is, and nothing had ever validated it against the schemas that claim to describe it.

- **`v2-durability-recovery` mandated a fast recovery bound, which RFC 0158 rejects.** Both kill rows read the event log **once**, the instant discovery answered again. §E item 11 says resumption is observed "within the declared recovery bound" and §B.6 makes a long bound conformant. Both rows now observe until the host's own declared bound elapses — the per-class figure the seam names for the staged work when there is one, under an observation ceiling the operator can raise, since a fixed ceiling would only have moved the defect from 0 s to 240 s and left a conformant 12.5-minute leased-class bound uncertifiable. `kill-during-execution` also asserted only item 11's first clause and so **passed on a host that never resumed the work**; it asserts both now. `duplicate-delivery` named a fixture that records no effect and so could only ever record `blocked`; worse, it counted rows per identity on the host's own effect ledger, where a ledger keyed on identity admits one row per identity by construction — **an unambiguous double-fire left the count at 1 and the row green**, measured by sabotage on a tier-1 host. The effect is now counted where it lands: the suite owns the receiver, the seam aims one effect at it and delivers the work twice, and the row asserts exactly one arrival. Recorded as a dated note in the RFC; no normative requirement changed.

  The scenario also read the run log from `GET /runs/{runId}/events` — SSE-only at major 2, where the JSON read is `/events/poll` — so on a host serving exactly what the OpenAPI states, `poison-exhaustion` soft-skipped its load-bearing clause and recorded **`executed-pass`, `partial-witness: blocked`**: a vacuous pass in the reference host's bundle. And "during execution" is now stated to mean *after the host holds its execution claim*, after a tier-1 seam killed at `run.started`, before the dispatch lease, and witnessed the easy recovery class under the hard one's name.

  Four of these were found by the openwop-app host **reading the scenario against its own sweeper before building the seam** — and it noted that a first witness on a host that re-enters runs at boot would have passed the single read and hidden the race. No host had witnessed these rows, so no bundle is invalidated.

- **Security — `openwop-conformance --certify` leaked the bundle signing key into its child's environment.** The vitest child was spawned with `{ ...process.env }`, carrying `OPENWOP_BUNDLE_SIGNING_KEY` (the private key PEM) into a process that never uses it — the signer runs in the parent. A host operator found it during a production cut: a process listing on macOS printed the key in full. Fixed in 2.32.0 (`conformance/src/lib/child-env.ts`). Exposure is same-uid or root, on the operator's own machine; there is no remote vector. **Operators who listed processes during a `--certify` on any suite ≤ 2.31.1 should rotate the bundle key**, since a terminal transcript is a durable copy.

- `spec/v2/facets/workflowChainPacks.schema.json` `$comment` said RFC 0124 was "still `Active`" (it is `Accepted`) and that "no v2 host bundle advertises" `deferredParameters` (one committed bundle does, and fails `capabilities-root-closed` for it). Neither changes the decision the comment records — v2.0 does not define the facet — so only the two facts are corrected.

## [2.31.0] — 2026-09-19 — the fifth rung row, and the projection helper three separate mistakes asked for

### Added

- **`durability/poison-exhaustion` ported to major 2, completing the `durable-single-instance` rung.** It existed — at **major 1 only**, reading a hard-coded `/v1/host/sample/test/runs/{runId}/events` seam — so **no v2 bundle could carry a durability row of any kind**, and RFC 0158's rung was unwitnessable at major 2 no matter what a host did. A host could have passed the other four and still not moved the RFC.

  **The port needs no seam.** At major 2 the canonical `GET /runs/{runId}/events` answers the same question the v1 sample seam was invented to answer, so the row is black-box and **cannot record `blocked` for want of infrastructure a host did not wire** — a strict improvement on its v1 twin, which records `blocked` in exactly that case. The v1 scenario is untouched.

  This was the blocker openwop-app was about to plan around. Told them directly rather than leaving it in a changelog for whoever cut next: they had asked whether RFC 0158 needed a *current* bundle or a *certified* one, and the honest answer was **neither** — their cut was on suite 2.3.3 and the rung scenarios landed in 2.27.0, so there was nothing there to witness either way.

- **`src/lib/v2-projection.ts` — the v1→v2 capability projection, in one place.** Three hand-written projections in one week, each silently wrong in a different direction:

  | who | what |
  | --- | --- |
  | openwop-app | `aiProviders.selfHosted` `string[]` → `boolean` |
  | steward | `aiProviders.input` flattened to `additionalProperties: true`, losing the RFC 0091 `modalities` enum |
  | steward | `aiProviders.policies` flattened the same way |

  That is not three mistakes; it is **one missing function**. `stripSupported()` strips the retired flag **at every depth** — v1 owners carry `supported` nested *inside* each facet, which is how 26 facet descriptions came to condition a MUST on a field the closed v2 schema forbids — and drops it out of any `required[]`, because a `required` naming a retired field makes a closed record unsatisfiable by any honest host.

  **It deliberately does not invent shape.** `carriesUnspliceablePayload()` reports an array, map, enum or scalar rather than converting it: that is RFC 0193's named-seat problem, the seat name cannot be derived because the v1 value *was* the whole property, and guessing one is how `supportedEnvelopes: {"status":"stable"}` reached a production host. Five unit tests, each written against one of the instances above.

## [2.30.0] — 2026-09-19 — the gate printed "(ratchet)" after a number nothing ratcheted

### Fixed

- **`check-registers` has printed `open risk rows 330 (ratchet)` on every run, and nothing compared that count to anything.** The baseline in `docs/witness-baseline.json` covered `openGaps` only. A word in a gate's own output that the code does not back is precisely the defect this corpus spent the day removing from its *prose* — and it was sitting in the instrument.

  This session's own docket repeated the claim ("a ratcheted backlog with a gate that already tells the truth about them"), which is how an unbacked word survives: it gets quoted by someone who trusted the output.

  `openRisks: 330` is now a real baseline and the summary names it: `open risk rows 330 (ratchet, baseline 330)`. Sabotage-proven — lowering the baseline to 329 fails the gate by name.

- **Zero is deliberately NOT the baseline here, unlike `openGaps`.** An open *gap* says a steward owes work, so zero is the only self-policing value. An open *risk* is a legitimate standing state — a hazard recorded as live rather than pretended away. What the baseline buys is that the count cannot climb **in silence**: a new open risk must lower another or move the number deliberately, in the same commit, with a reason.

### Verified, not changed

- **The tier-3 tripwire is stated honestly across the corpus** and needs no work. `INTEROP-MATRIX.md` says **"no independent-organization row exists"** in its own header vocabulary; every graduation records its tier explicitly (*"Not dual-witness, not independent-organization evidence"*); `MAINTAINERS.md` records the condition that retires the bootstrap waiver. Checked rather than assumed — the docket said "leave it alone", which is a conclusion that still has to be earned.

## [2.29.0] — 2026-09-19 — the front door was misreporting the corpus's own size, and I caused it

### Fixed

- **Three documents said `spec/v2/core/` holds "twenty documents". It holds twenty-two.** README line 11, README's Document-index banner, and `docs/IMPLEMENT-CORE.md`. The drift landed in **2.23.0** — this same day — when `host-services.md` and `conversation.md` were added to give the last homeless families a home, in the release whose entire point was that a claim should match what is measurable.

  These are the **first numbers a newcomer reads**: README line 11 sizes the implementation job, and `IMPLEMENT-CORE.md` is the document the README tells an implementer to start with *instead of* the corpus. A wrong number there is the corpus misreporting its own size to the one reader the project most needs.

- **`capabilities.md` §5 was headed "Core families (71)" against 72 core rows** in `spec/v2/declaration.json`. `check-declaration` already fails when a *heading* names a family the declaration does not; it never compared the **totals**.

- `QUICKSTART.md`'s two remaining `apps/workflow-engine/` labels now name the repo they actually link to. The links resolved to `openwop-app` and the visible path had not existed for months.

### Added

- **`v2-front-door-counts.test.ts`** holds both counts against the tree. Sabotage-proven: reverting one site names the file, the stated word and the real number.

### Declined, and this is the more useful half

- **The plan proposed ratcheting README's `spec/v2/` link count above its `spec/v1/` count (18 vs 93). That instrument is wrong and the gate was not built.** The Document index **is** the v1 tree by design and says so in its own banner; `spec/v2/` cites `spec/v1/` **101 times** because the 25,000-word core budget makes v2 a declaration surface; and three v1 documents have no v2 counterpart at all. The ratio measures **tree size, not navigational intent** — and the README already opens its v1 reading order with *"Building a v2 host? The v2 reading order is `docs/IMPLEMENT-CORE.md`."* A gate demanding the ratio invert would have forced dishonest prose to satisfy a number.

  The docket's "the front door still describes v1" item was itself a measurement error — counting links and calling the result intent. A file count and a row count are mechanical facts; those are the ones worth gating, and those are what shipped.

## [2.28.0] — 2026-09-19 — the bundle's two arrays, and two v2 facets that validated anything

### Fixed

- **A certification bundle carries the same requirement id in two arrays with different field sets, and nothing said which to count.** `results.requirements[]` is the ledger (`id, scenario, result, assertions, detail`); `detail.nonPass[]` re-lists the same ids as a derived view (`id, result, reason`). A consumer walking the document generically sees every non-pass id **twice**. Two sessions did exactly that on one file within an hour of each other: one read **227** rows and the other **317** (227 + 90), and a blocked-row count came out as **3 or 6** by the same mechanism. Reported by openwop-app-1, who hit it from the other side. `certification-bundle.schema.json` now says which array is the ledger and that the other MAY omit fields **by design, not by accident**.

- **`aiProviders.input` and `aiProviders.policies` validated anything at major 2.** The hand-authored v2 facet override had reduced both to `{type: "object", additionalProperties: true}` — open objects with no properties, no enum and no description — while v1 defines them **closed**: `input` with a `modalities` enum (`text`/`image`/`audio`/`document`, RFC 0091) and `policies` with `{modes, scopes, errorCode}`. They were the **only two** open-and-empty facets in the entire v2 schema. A misspelled modality would have shipped silently.

  Restored from the v1 owner. **This rejects nothing published**, measured rather than assumed: MyndHyve's live major-2 document already advertises exactly the v1 shapes (`input: {"modalities":["text","image"]}`, `policies: {modes, scopes, errorCode}`), and all three host documents in `evidence/v2-host-bundles/` validate clean against the tightened schema. Sabotage-proven in both directions — a typo'd modality and an invented `policies` key are now refused.

  Same family as RFC 0193 (a v2 projection that silently lost a constraint), different mechanism: 0193 was the **generator** dropping payload it could not splice; this was a **hand-authored override** replacing a closed shape with an open one.

### Timing

- Landed deliberately **before** openwop-app's post-tunnel cut, which is about to advertise `aiProviders.input.modalities` at major 2 for the first time. They flagged the change and asked to hear if any of it looked wrong before their evidence baked it in — it did, and this is the answer.

## [2.27.1] — 2026-09-19 — two stale register rows, and a gate I measured and declined to add

### Fixed

- **RFC 0121's G1 gated the un-parking tripwire on a flip the RFC had already made.** The row read *"this IS the un-parking tripwire — the gating condition for `Draft (Parked) → Active`"*, while the RFC's header has said `Active` (**Parked**) for weeks and its `Updated` field records 6 of 8 `Accepted` criteria met. A reader checking whether the tripwire had fired would have measured the wrong flip. Target corrected to `Active (Parked) → Accepted`.

- **RFC 0121's G6 asserted a defect that does not exist.** It filed *"`credential_scope_forbidden` is absent from `spec/v2/errors.json`"*. Re-measured: it **is** registered — `spec/v2/errors.json` carries it at `403`, `spec/v2/core/errors.md` lists it, and `schemas/v2/error-envelope.schema.json` carries it in both the code enum and the status map. The claim is struck rather than left standing: a register row asserting a defect that does not exist sends the next reader to fix nothing.

### Declined, with the measurement

- **A gate for "a live gap gated on a flip the RFC has already made" was written, measured, and reverted.** It found **50 rows**, and they were not defects: the Blocks column is a *deadline* ("resolve by this flip"), not a prediction, so a `carried` row on an `Accepted` RFC naming "Active status" is history, not a live claim. What made 0121's G1 wrong was its **prose** asserting a present gating condition — a semantic claim, and no regex decides a semantic claim. Narrowing the rule until it matched only the row already fixed would have bought a gate that catches nothing. The reasoning is recorded instead of a rule.

### Not changed, deliberately

- **RFC 0121 is NOT withdrawn**, reversing this session's own docket recommendation. Its G1 is tokened `externally-gated:provider-tos-clearance` with a named un-parking tripwire. `Withdrawn` implies a decision not to pursue and would **lose** the tripwire; `Active (Parked)` with an externally-gated gap says more, and says it precisely.
- **RFC 0111 is NOT un-parked.** The docket said it had "five open design gaps answerable by design work". It has **zero** open gaps: four `closed` and one `externally-gated:non-steward-host`, which is its own parking tripwire — workstream G, unownable. The docket's count came from grepping the word "open", which appears inside that row's prose explaining why it is *not* open.

## [2.27.0] — 2026-09-19 — the four RFC 0158 recovery rows that never existed, and the disposition rule that keeps them from stripping the fleet

### Added

- **`v2-durability-recovery.test.ts` — the four `durable-single-instance` rows RFC 0158 names and no scenario has ever covered.** `kill-after-accept` (a **hold-dispatch** exercise, not a termination-timing one — on a host where acceptance and dispatch are microseconds apart, racing a kill into that window cannot reliably hit it), `kill-during-execution` (work executing at a real process death MUST NOT be observable as completed without re-execution), `duplicate-delivery` (invocation counts **per effect identity**, because a legal end state is exactly what a double-fire produces), and `bound-is-derived`.

- **The disposition rule is the load-bearing decision, and it protects fleet certification.** §E says an unmet operator precondition is `blocked` with the precondition named; §E also says the seam is a non-normative host-extension route that *"advertises nothing"* and that a host never running the exercises *"exposes no such route"*. Those are **different states**, and collapsing them would deny certification to every host the day these rows entered the major-2 lane, because a `blocked` row denies certification (RFC 0168 §E.1):

  | state | disposition |
  | --- | --- |
  | no seam route at all | **`inapplicable`** — §E.10 mints no capability field, so seam *presence* is how a host claims a rung; blocking a host for a claim it never made is the false-refusal half of the same error as a vacuous pass |
  | seam present, precondition unmet | **`blocked`**, precondition **named** — a restart supervisor is required and a black-box suite cannot supply one |

  Verified against the reference host: `/host/durability/kill` answers `404` while `/host/events` answers `200`, so the four rows record `inapplicable` and **all three profiles still certify**.

- **`peer-resume` is deliberately not written.** It is the `durable-multi-instance` discriminator, not part of the rung these rows witness, and §E makes it **bundle-witnessed** via an opaque per-boot incarnation token rather than black-box observable. A scenario for it could only ever record `blocked` — which is precisely the defect found in `0188.dead-letter-content-free` this same day, and writing a second one knowingly would be worse than the first.

### Fixed

- `conformance/README.md`'s two scenario-file counts (522 → 523). The gate compares them against the live tree, as it should.

### Known gap, stated rather than hidden

- **`durability/poison-exhaustion` is registered at MAJOR 1 ONLY**, so no v2 bundle has ever carried a durability row of any kind. RFC 0158's acceptance needs all five rows of the rung at major 2; porting it needs the v2 event-log read, and it is tracked rather than quietly assumed. Until then the rung is unwitnessable at major 2 even with these four passing.

## [2.26.0] — 2026-09-19 — RFC 0188 Accepted: the read existed, the shape did not

### Changed

- **RFC 0188 `Active → Accepted`.** Evidence tier **tier-1 — steward-verified**: the reference host advertises `webhooks.deadLetter` and serves the canonical §A read, with `openwop.requirement.0188.dead-letter-read` and `.dead-letter-content-free` both `executed-pass` on a bundle carrying **0 blocked** and all three profiles certified (suite 2.25.0, witness `c27516af1841`, openwop/openwop-examples#63).

- **The RFC's own acceptance criterion understated the work, and the docket repeated it.** It read as though the host merely had to *"advertise the facet, move the path off `extensions.<org>.deadLetterRead`, and re-cut."* The host had indeed served `GET /webhooks/{webhookId}/dead-letters` for some time — but as a **vendor extension whose shape did not conform**. Advertising a facet over a non-conforming shape publishes exactly the vacuous claim RFC 0193 exists to stop, so the projection was rewritten to §A first: the closed `{deliveries, nextCursor}` page, the nine required record fields, §A.2's tenant check **before** the lookup, and §A.3 pagination with a subscription-bound cursor.

- **`lastError` was the field that mattered.** The old record carried the subscriber's response text. §B.1 makes the record content-free *by construction* precisely because a dead-letter queue is the traffic the subscriber never received — a record carrying any of the exchange turns one read scope into a replay of it for the whole retention window.

- **`0173.webhook-durable-delivery.dead-letter` is now a clean `executed-pass`.** It had carried a partial-witness note on **every bundle ever cut** — first "the corpus serves no dead-letter read surface", then "the host does not advertise the facet". The sink half of `webhooks.md` §Durability is witnessed for the first time by any bundle.

- Reference-host evidence re-checked in at suite 2.25.0: **233 / 0 / 0 / 6 / 7**, 1996 assertions across 246 rows. `INTEROP-MATRIX.md` updated.

## [2.25.0] — 2026-09-19 — the §B.1 leg asserted nothing, and that denied certification to the first host that advertised the facet

### Fixed

- **`openwop.requirement.0188.dead-letter-content-free` could never pass on any host.** The leg registered a **fresh** subscription and read its dead-letter sink. A fresh subscription has no dead letters by construction, so the loop over `deliveries` ran zero times, the test asserted nothing, and RFC 0148 §A resolves a silent return to **`blocked`** — which denies certification under RFC 0168 §E.1.

  It went unnoticed because every host recorded `inapplicable` for want of the facet. The first host ever to advertise `webhooks.deadLetter` was refused certification by this leg on its first cut, with 232 other rows passing and zero failing.

  §B.1 is a claim about what a **real** record carries, so the leg now makes one: it exhausts a delivery against a receiver that never succeeds — the same move the sibling `0173` dead-letter leg already performs — then reads the sink and asserts content-freeness across the rows it actually got. An empty sink now records `blocked` **with a reason** rather than passing silently.

- **The shape of the bug is worth naming: a loop over a possibly-empty collection is not an assertion.** It type-checks, it runs, it reports success, and it witnesses nothing. The disposition machinery caught it correctly — the defect was that nothing had ever exercised the path.

### Changed

- The leg now filters receiver attempts by **subscription as well as run**, matching the sibling leg: a host that cannot reach loopback shares one tunnelled receiver URL across scenarios, so another scenario's attempts otherwise land in this leg's budget.

## [2.24.0] — 2026-09-19 — a capability record is an object, so a v1 array needs a seat (RFC 0193)

### Fixed

- **Three families reached major 2 with their payload silently deleted.** `generate-from-declaration.mjs` builds a capability record by splicing the seeded v1 property's `properties` in as siblings of `status`/`since`/`until`/`witness`. A v1 family that **was** an object kept its payload — `limits` still carries `clarificationRounds`/`schemaRounds`/`envelopesPerTurn`. A v1 family that was an **array**, a **map**, or an **enum** has no `properties` at all, so its payload was dropped with no error and the record still validated. That is exactly three families, and they are one flow: `supportedEnvelopes` (the envelope-kind catalog), `schemaVersions` (the per-kind version floor), `envelopeStrictness` (the drift mode). Each now holds its value in a named seat — `kinds`, `kinds`, `mode` — decided by hand in `spec/v2/facets/`, because the generator cannot mint a name for a value that *was* the whole property.

- **The defect was published, and a production host is wearing it.** MyndHyve's live v2 discovery document carries `limits` whole and, three keys away, `supportedEnvelopes: {"status":"stable","since":"1.0","witness":"witnessable-gated"}` — a `stable` claim to an envelope-kind catalog **containing no catalog**. The host did nothing wrong; it filled in every field the schema offered it. The schema was what was vacuous.

### Security

- **`prompt-injection-envelope-typecheck` had no input at major 2.** `unknown_envelope_kind` is *defined* by reference to the list — `ai-envelope.md` §"Refusal taxonomy" reads "Unknown `type` (not in `supportedEnvelopes`)" — and the protocol-tier invariant says the same. With no list in the v2 record, the invariant's stated mechanism did not exist, and an implementer reading "no list" as "nothing to check" admits every kind an attacker can name. Same fail-open class as the RFC 0132 guard found deleted in 2.14.0: a control whose input quietly went missing while the rule citing it stayed on the page. `events.md` §"The envelope-kind catalog" now pins it **closed**: an absent `kinds` is not an empty catalog and is not an unrestricted one — an engine MUST refuse every non-universal kind. The invariant's `note` records the major-2 mechanism and names the fail-open so the permissive reading cannot be re-derived.

### Changed

- **The seats are OPTIONAL, and that was decided by measurement.** MyndHyve already publishes `supportedEnvelopes` and `schemaVersions` without them, and the v2 root is `additionalProperties: false` — a required seat invalidates a live closed document with no host change, the rule that already kept `observability.testSeams` at 2.x. The obligation lives in prose, at the point of use, where absence can be given a *safe* meaning instead of an undefined one.
- **`versioning.md` axis 6 contradicted its own legend.** It ruled `schemaVersions` **first-class** — "own schema-enforced grammar and negotiation rule" — while the generated schema gave it the shape the same legend calls **`retire`**. One document, two dispositions for one axis; the same shape as the `packs.md:37` defect corrected in 2.22.0. The row's stated grammar was also wrong on its own terms: `additionalProperties: false` over *declared* kinds forbids vendor kinds, which `ai-envelope.schema.json` **requires** to be namespaced and host-published, never corpus-declared. A closed enum would forbid the entries the catalog exists to carry. It now reads `propertyNames` = the kind grammar, and says why it is not an enum.
- **`unknown_envelope_kind` and `unknown_schema_version` are registered** in `spec/v2/errors.json` (422 each, `retriable: false` — retrying an identical payload never succeeds). v1 prose already required a host to emit both; an unregistered code cannot be asserted against at major 2.
- **Three more families resolve in v2.** All three move their normative home to `events.md`, so `resolved` goes 26 → 29 and the RFC 0190 kernel cap rises 28,400 → 29,000.

### Added

- **The guard that stops the fourth.** The generator now REFUSES a family whose v1 payload it cannot splice, naming the family and pointing at the seat file. It is green on day one — no fourth family exists — so it lands as a regression floor, not a burn-down. Sabotage-proven: removing a seat file makes the generator fail by name. `conformance/src/coherence/v2-envelope-catalog.test.ts` carries six witnesses, including the optionality leg that keeps a future edit from breaking the published record this release was careful not to break.
- RFC 0193 — `Accepted` 2026-09-19. RFC 0147 §A.6 is **engaged** (prompt-injection surface) and **discharged, not waived**: the change is strictly restrictive, with no configuration of seats under which a kind admitted after this RFC would have been refused before it.

## [2.23.0] — 2026-09-19 — every core family has a home; the end-of-support gate passes 76 days early

### Changed

- **`undeclared` reaches ZERO.** All 72 core families carry a declared normative home: **26 resolved in v2**, 46 v1-dependent with recorded banners. `check-v2-normative-home.mjs --deadline` run past end-of-support (`OPENWOP_NORMATIVE_HOME_TODAY=2026-12-05`) **exits 0** — the RFC 0189 §D terminal predicate is satisfied on **2026-09-19**, seventy-six days before the date it was written for.
- **G11 — `capabilities.<key>` is the family's name.** G5 is right that `owner.workspace` does not name `workspace`; it was wrong that `capabilities.prompts` does not name `prompts`, which is the canonical way v1 prose names a family. Restricted to `capabilities.` alone: measured, `host.` adds 8 further pairs, buys **zero** additional honest closures, and admits `aiEnvelope` against an editorial *"a future v1.x evolution MAY widen the projection"* note. `prompts` and `nodePackRuntimes` declare on real anchors — the latter a textbook home at `node-packs.md` I had twice classified "no candidate anywhere".

### Added

- **`spec/v2/core/host-services.md`** — the ruling that `**Required methods:** generate.` is **not** an obligation. `§host.promptLibrary` and `§host.agentRuntime` carry **zero** RFC 2119 keywords outside fences, in a document whose sibling sections carry 25, 18 and 15. A lowercase section label is not a chosen normative form, and no predicate can admit it without admitting the word "required" throughout descriptive prose. v1 is frozen, so the contract is written in v2: `aiEnvelope`, `promptLibrary` and `agentRuntime` resolve **in v2** rather than carrying.
- **`spec/v2/core/conversation.md`** — `multiPartyConversation`, `channelPresence` and `conversationTurnModelProvenance`. Their RFCs are `Accepted`, their wire shapes frozen in `schemas/v2/`, and conformance covers them; each deliberately mints no client route for *opening* a conversation, and so none was ever given an operative home. *"Mints no normative wire-route"* is a statement about routing, not an argument against a home — the obligation an advertising host takes on is real regardless of who calls it.
- **`replay.md` §"Declared nondeterminism"** for `nondeterminismPolicy`, and facet prose in `events.md` for `envelopes.tierOneSubsetCompliance`, `feedback.targets` and `providerUsage.costEstimates` — three facets with no prose anywhere in the corpus.

**The budget was never the constraint.** The cap is `25,000 + 200 × (resolved − 9)`, so writing v2 prose for a homeless family *buys* budget: this release spent ~470 words and raised the cap from 27,000 to **28,400**, ending at **25,820 / 28,400**.

**Compatibility: additive.** The new v2 prose states obligations that already bind an advertising host — each is discoverable-gated on a capability that exists in both schemas today. No wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract or error meaning changes; no host's conformance pass is invalidated.

## [2.22.0] — 2026-09-19 — a false home I shipped yesterday, caught by the gate I shipped today

### Fixed

- **`envelopeContracts` was a false home, live in the tree, landed by me in 2.21.0.** G4 split *tables* so a `MUST` in row 15 could not satisfy a family named in row 6. It left **bullet lists** fused, and the same exploit lives one syntax down: `spec/v1/ai-envelope.md`'s `envelopeContracts.advertised` bullet carries **no** RFC 2119 keyword, and satisfied §B(c) only because the **adjacent** `envelopeStrictness` bullet says *"MUST cause refusal"*. It shipped hand-read — right about the family, wrong about the anchor — and the §D fallback would have asserted it as operative prose on 2026-12-04. Closed as **G14**.
  - Unlike G4 and G12 this tightening is **not free**: it removes 27 pre-existing candidate pairs and breaks exactly one declared family — the one that is false.
  - `envelopeContracts` is re-anchored to a real v2 home: `events.md` §"Envelope contracts" states the per-node permission-set refusal and that it *stacks* with the capability-gated `typeId` refusal rather than substituting. It becomes **resolved**, not falsely carried, which raises the cap to **27,000**.
- **G16 — a conformance-seam catalog could be a normative home.** `spec/v1/host-sample-test-seams.md` banners *"observation seams, not application endpoints"*, and its `multiPartyConversation` bullet says in its own matched text that RFC 0101 *"mints no normative client wire-route"* — the paragraph that would have satisfied §B(c) is the paragraph disclaiming the contract. G13's informative-banner refusal does not catch it, because the document is genuinely `Stable` and genuinely normative *about seams*. Refused as a class, sabotage-proved.

### Changed

- **G15 proposed and REJECTED on measurement.** The proposal — strip fenced code blocks from the facet arm as G12 does for the obligation arm — came with the claim that it costs zero declared families. Measured: it breaks **sixteen** facets (`credentials.encryptionAtRest`, `oauth.grants`, `kvStorage.maxTtlSeconds` and thirteen more), every one named in a **JSON advertisement example**, which is precisely how a facet is legitimately named.
  - The two arms are **not symmetric**: §B(d) is a naming count *by design*, not an obligation check. A code block cannot carry an obligation; it can perfectly well name a field. The `aiEnvelope.await` case that motivated the proposal is a **homonym**, and stripping fences is the wrong instrument for it. Recorded in the gate so the proposal is not re-made.

**Compatibility: editorial + gate.** No wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract or error meaning changes. The new `events.md` section states an obligation that already binds an advertising host.

## [2.21.0] — 2026-09-19 — nine families for zero words, and a trap closed before it was walked into

### Changed

- **Nine core families declared v1-dependent**, every anchor hand-read: `secrets`, `modelCapabilities`, `envelopeContracts`, `artifactTypes`, `uiPlugins`, `memory`, `aiProviders`, `agents`, `production`. `undeclared` **21 → 12**; `v1Dependent` 33 → 42. First red moves **2026-11-11 → 2026-11-21**. **Word cost: zero** — a v1 home adds nothing to the measured set and nothing to the cap.
- A sweep offered **16**; hand-reading rejected **7**. `prompts` matched the plural noun in a content-free redaction list; `feedback` matched a *channel reducer* of the same name; `agentRuntime` matched a sentence whose whole purpose is *"Distinct from `host.agentRuntime`"*; and `aiEnvelope`'s facet `await` was "covered" by the phrase **"before the first `await`"** — the JavaScript keyword.
- **`agents` is the one place G10 is load-bearing and correct.** Its anchor is `capabilities.md`'s ``### `agents` `` section, which G4 had severed from the contract beneath it.

### Fixed

- **A sabotage test would have silently stopped testing.** `declare()` in `v2-normative-home-gate.test.ts` inserted `normativeText` after `"kind": "family",` — the exact position an already-declared family carries it — producing a **duplicate key** whose later (original) value `JSON.parse` keeps. The moment `production` landed, its own sabotage would have mutated nothing, passed the gate, and failed the assertion for an unrelated-looking reason. **A test whose mutation no-ops is worse than no test.** Now replaces rather than prepends.
- **G12 — a fenced code block is not an obligation.** G10 made the heading supply the family name, which removed an accidental defence: a code block under `## §host.<key>` spells the family `ctx.<key>.…` and was preceding-dot-dead before G10. After it, §host.secrets' TypeScript sketch satisfied §B(c) on a `// pack SHOULD treat as advisory` **comment**. Fenced blocks are now stripped before splitting — measured across every declared family, the set that loses its obligation is **empty**.
- **G13 — a document that declares itself non-normative could be a normative home.** `spec/v1/structured-output-subset.md` is titled *"(informative)"* and banners *"Non-normative snapshot"*, and it is the **only** prose site of `envelopes.tierOneSubsetCompliance` corpus-wide — so declaring it was the cheapest way to resolve `envelopes`. The gate checked only that a banner *existed*. At end-of-support the §D fallback would have asserted an informative document is operative prose, and `facetsUncovered` would have welded it in permanently. Now refused by name, sabotage-proved with the exact declaration. **`envelopes` is consequently NOT declared** — it needs one facet sentence in a v2 core home, and resolves rather than carries.

**Compatibility: editorial + gate.** No wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract or error meaning changes; the nine declarations record where obligations that already bind v1 hosts are written.

## [2.20.0] — 2026-09-19 — four RFCs Accepted, and the witnesses they named into existence

### Added

- **RFCs 0189, 0190, 0191 and 0192 are `Accepted`** — Accepted count **21 → 25**. None of it needed a host, a server, or a re-cut. I had planned to re-cut the reference-host bundle on the premise that it would unblock all eight Active RFCs; an architecture review falsified that premise completely. A fresh certified bundle unblocks **zero** of the eight on its own, and four were unblockable today with no server at all.
- **Eleven requirement ids minted into `evidence/corpus-ledger.json`.** Every `(corpus)` witness these four RFCs named in their falsifiability tables **did not exist** — the ledger's RFC numbers stopped at 0177. The RFCs cited machinery nobody had built. `conformance/src/coherence/v2-normative-home-gate.test.ts` and `v2-facet-advertisement.test.ts` now mint all eleven; the ledger goes **345 → 357** ids.
  - **They are sabotage witnesses, not exit-0 wrappers, wherever the RFC row says the gate *refuses* something.** A bare exit-0 assertion against an already-green gate passes just as happily with the predicate deleted — the vacuous-witness shape this corpus keeps catching in others. So: an RFC declared as a home is refused; `§host.promptLibrary` is titled for its family and carries zero MUSTs and is still refused; `production` against `security-defaults.md` is refused for want of a marker; a reintroduced `supported` ghost fails the generator by path.
  - **Sabotage runs against a throwaway copy of the corpus, never the working tree.** Mutating a tracked file in place raced `v2-spec-artifacts-digest` in the same vitest run, which compares the packed tree against the corpus and observed it mid-sabotage. The copy removes the race rather than sequencing around it.

### Fixed

- **`security-defaults.md` still said `permanent` where the schema says `persisted`.** The 2.6.0 erratum fixed the enum table and missed the sentence one line above it. A host following the normative prose literally emits `permanent`, is rejected by `certification-bundle.schema.json`, exits 2 and writes **no bundle** — the exact defect 2.6.0 was filed to remove, still live three releases later, sitting directly on the re-cut path.
- **RFC 0192 §A cited a corpus gate that did not exist.** Its falsifiability row said *"the corpus gate reads it"* of `capabilities.md` §2's facets row; nothing read it. The row asserted machinery never built. Now it names the test that actually asserts it, including the absence exception `memory.writable` depends on.
- **RFCs 0190, 0191 and 0192 shipped with no `## Acceptance criteria` section at all** — I wrote all three. The predicate reports *"Acceptance criteria section is empty or has neither checkboxes nor a table."* Each now carries one, with an `Active → Accepted` bar that is a real test rather than a restatement of Draft → Active.

**Compatibility: editorial + gate.** No wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract or error meaning changes. The `permanent → persisted` correction is a safety-fix in effect — it can only unblock a host that was previously refused.

## [2.19.0] — 2026-09-19 — seven families did not need a design decision; their contract was already written

### Fixed

- **G4 severed obligations from the section headings that scope them.** G4 (2.13.0) split table rows into separate paragraph units so a `MUST` in row 15 could not satisfy a family named in row 6. That was right, and it had a cost nobody measured: it also cut those rows loose from their **section heading**.
  - `spec/v1/host-capabilities.md` carries `## §host.kvStorage`, `## §host.blobStorage`, `## §host.vectorStore` and five more. Each is a real contract with **three to five MUSTs** — and every one is a table row reading *"A `get` for tenant A MUST NOT return values written by tenant B, even with identical keys."* The family's name is in the heading; the obligation is in the row; G4 put them in different units, and the gate concluded the contract did not exist.
  - **Seven families had been classified as needing a design decision** about where a `ctx.*` host service lives in v2. They needed no decision. Their contract was written, and unreadable to the predicate.
  - Closed as **G10**: an obligation inside a section **titled for** the family counts as an obligation about it. The heading must *be* the section title (`## §host.kvStorage`, `### § packs`) — not merely mention the key, which is the G1 homonym hazard one level up. `host.` is admitted because it is the v1 capability namespace those sections are named in. Sabotage-proved: `promptLibrary`'s section *is* titled for it and carries **zero** MUSTs — still refused.

### Changed

- **Seven families declared v1-dependent**: `fs`, `kvStorage`, `tableStorage`, `nosql`, `vectorStore`, `searchIndex`, `blobStorage` — all with **zero facets uncovered**, all with recorded banners.
- **Twelve families declared v1-dependent**, all with zero facets uncovered and recorded banners: the seven storage surfaces above, plus `cache`, `sql`, `deadLetter`, `queueBus` and `scheduling` — each of whose obligation is a table row under its own `## §host.<key>` section, and each of which I had rejected by hand one release earlier *because* it was a table row. G10 is what made the difference between "a table row, therefore not an anchor" and "a table row under a heading that names the family, therefore exactly an anchor".
- `undeclared` **33 → 21**; `v1Dependent` 21 → 33. First red moves **~2026-10-28 → ~2026-11-12**.

**Compatibility: editorial + gate.** No wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract or error meaning changes.

## [2.18.0] — 2026-09-19 — fourteen families declared, and a decline I got wrong

### Fixed

- **The burn-down measured something RFC 0189 §D does not make fatal — and I had declined to fix it on a premise that no longer held.** §D's terminal predicate is `fatal = undeclared + uncarried + broken`: at end-of-support a **carried** v1-dependent family is acceptable. The daily clock counted `v1Dependent + undeclared`, so it demanded v2 text for families §D says are fine.
  - A review recommended aligning them. **I declined**, citing RFC 0190 §C, which had rejected excluding `v1Carried` because *"`v1Carried` is a hand-written array edited by the same PR that declares, so excluding it would make 'add your family to `v1Carried`' a zero-work way to lower `open`."*
  - **That premise was falsified by work that landed after §C was written.** **G7** requires `v1Carried` to *equal* the computed v1-dependent set, and **§B(b)/(c) + G5** require the v1 target to genuinely name the family and carry a 2119 obligation about it — the same predicate a v2 resolution must pass. Sabotage-proved: pointing `promptLibrary` at an unrelated v1 document is refused by name. The hatch §C feared does not exist any more.
  - I had applied §C's *conclusion* without rechecking whether its *reason* still held — the same error class this register exists to catch, committed against my own RFC. The burn-down now measures `undeclared + uncarried`, literally the expression §D makes fatal; `openAtT0` re-seeded **68 → 66** (what `undeclared` was at t0). `v1Dependent` stays reported and unconstrained upward, because §C is still right that punishing the first honest declaration is what made silence cheapest.

### Changed

- **Fourteen families declared v1-dependent**, each with a hand-verified anchor: `selfHostedRunner`, `purposePropagation`, `anonymousActor`, `credentials`, `subWorkflow`, `httpClient`, `triggerBridge`, `supportedEnvelopes`, `envelopeStrictness`, `content`, `budget`, `i18n`, `portability`, `workspace`.
  - A mechanical sweep offered **28** candidates. Hand-reading the anchor paragraph rejected half: `production` matched *"the `production` scale tier"*, `secrets` matched a paragraph about NodeModule model capabilities, `agentRuntime` matched a sentence whose whole purpose is *"Distinct from `host.agentRuntime`"*, and `cache`, `sql`, `deadLetter` matched table rows. Consistent with every sweep this session.
  - **`supportedEnvelopes` and `envelopeStrictness` land here for a structural reason.** Their v2 records are `{status, since, until, witness}` with no payload, so the envelope-type allowlist their obligation is about — backed by the protocol-tier invariant `prompt-injection-envelope-typecheck` — cannot be expressed on the v2 wire. A v2 home would have been an unfireable MUST. v1 carries the real contract.
  - `workspace`'s target is `Status: Draft`. That is fine under **G7**, which compares a carried banner against the one recorded at declaration rather than testing whether it currently reads `Stable` — precisely the defect G7 fixed.
- `undeclared` **47 → 33**; `v1Dependent` 7 → 21. First red moves **2026-10-03 → ~2026-10-28**.

**Compatibility: editorial + gate.** No wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract or error meaning changes.

## [2.17.0] — 2026-09-19 — the 27th ghost, a docblock that claimed a fix it had not made, and a red that can no longer drown an alarm

### Fixed

- **RFC 0192 swept 26 `supported` ghosts out of schema descriptions and missed the one in core prose.** `spec/v2/core/runs.md` read *"`provider` MUST be in `aiProviders.supported`, else `400 validation_error`"* — and `supported` is not a facet of `aiProviders`; the list is `providers`. A MUST naming a field the closed schema forbids, in the paragraph a mechanical sweep would have used as that family's normative home. Corrected.
- **The G5 docblock claimed to have closed a bleed it had not.** It listed `i18n` ← *"see i18n.md"* among the cases the tightening removed. It does not: the trailing boundary class excludes `-`, `_` and `/` but must leave `.` open for the `key.facet` arm, so a **following** dot still bleeds — `see i18n.md` names `i18n`, and `` `channelPresence.supported` `` names `channelPresence`. Only a **preceding** dot was closed. Adding `.` to the class was measured and **breaks eight honestly-declared families**, so the bleed stands and the claim does not: the docblock now states exactly what G5 does and does not close, and the residual is filed as **G8**. A false claim in the file whose whole purpose is not making false claims is the defect this corpus keeps finding in others.

### Changed

- **The normative-home clock is its own workflow.** It was the second step of `pending-suite-release.yml`, whose first step is the unpublished-suite-tag alarm — the one that caught 25 unshipped bumps. This clock is **expected to go red**: the burn-down deliberately drives toward v2 text while §D's terminal fallback accepts a carried v1-dependent family. A job that is red-by-design for weeks trains you to stop reading the run it shares, which would have meant a standing red masking exactly the finding that alarm exists to surface. Split, so this one may stand red and stay informative and that one is only ever red for a reason.
- **`conversationPrimitive` and `dataResidency` declare homes** in `runs.md`, carrying forward obligations that already bind: the `core.conversationGate` refusal contract (`spec/v1/capabilities.md` §"Refusal contract (normative)") and RFC 0129 §3's honor-or-reject rule, whose `residency_unavailable` is already a registered v2 code. `undeclared` **49 → 47**; budget **25,303 / 26,800** — 109 words spent, 400 granted.

### Declined

- **Aligning the burn-down denominator with the terminal predicate.** A review recommended changing `open = v1Dependent + undeclared` to `undeclared`, on the accurate observation that §D accepts a carried family at end-of-support while the daily clock does not. **RFC 0190 §C already considered and rejected exactly this** — *"a fallback is what happens when you miss the target; it is not an exemption from the target."* Reversing a documented decision in the direction that makes my own schedule easier is schedule-shopping. The tension is real and intended; filed as **G9** with the reasoning, and answered by moving the red rather than muting it.

**Compatibility: editorial + gate.** No wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract or error meaning changes; `generate-from-declaration --check` byte-identical.

## [2.16.0] — 2026-09-19 — RFC 0192: twenty-six MUSTs that could never fire

### Added

- **RFC 0192 — a facet is advertised by the presence of its key.** v2 retired the `supported` flag, and `capabilities.md` §2 says what that means for a **family** (*"presence of the record is the claim"*) while saying nothing about a **facet**. The gap was precisely one table row, and **26 facet descriptions still conditioned a MUST on `supported: true`** — a field the closed schema forbids, so each was an obligation the corpus publishes and no host can trigger. **A MUST that cannot fire is a relaxed MUST.**
  - **The rule is not "presence of the object", and the difference is load-bearing.** Not every facet is an object — `httpClient.ssrfGuard` and `memory.writable` are booleans — so it is presence of the **key**. And `memory.writable` *inverts* the default: *"Absent ⇒ the host implements the full RFC 0004 four-operation MemoryAdapter… A read-only host MUST set `writable: false`."* The rule carries an explicit exception for a facet whose schema states a meaning for its own absence.
  - **Hosts had already converged on it, independently.** `httpClient.safeFetch` is emitted as `{}` — the v1 `{supported: true}` with the flag removed, presence carrying the whole claim. `agents.orgChart` appears only where `agents.roster` does, exactly as the unstatable `roster.supported: true` intended.

### Fixed

- **A `supported`-gated conditional is now migrated, not dropped.** v1 said *"if `supported: true` then these fields are required"*; under presence-semantics the antecedent is the facet being present, so the rule becomes an unconditional `required`. Dropping them had silently relaxed `memory.compaction ⇒ trigger` and `memory.injectionBudget ⇒ tokenCounter` — both of which were *still described* as "enforced via the `if/then` clause", naming a clause that was no longer there. Both obligations are restored, and the descriptions are now true.
- **All 26 descriptions rewritten by hand.** A bulk regex was measured and rejected: they carry at least five distinct semantics — opt-in, conditional-required, cross-family reference, and one three-way combinatorial explanation (`prompts.endpointsSupported`) that collapses entirely under presence-semantics. Two were hand-authored override text and were edited in place.
- **The 27th cannot appear silently.** The v1 seed is still the source, so a newly-seeded family arrives with the same idiom. `generate-from-declaration.mjs` now fails on both `--write` and `--check` when it would emit such a description, naming the path. It fails the **generator** rather than a separate checker deliberately: `generate-deprecation-annotations.mjs` also writes that file, and 2.10.0 established that a correction applied as a post-pass is overwritten by whichever generator runs last. Sabotage-proved.

**Compatibility: additive.** A JSON Schema `description` is an annotation, never an assertion (2020-12 §7 vs §10), so no host discovery document changes validity. The conditional migration *tightens* two facets that had been silently relaxed; all three bundles in `evidence/v2-host-bundles/` validate.

## [2.15.1] — 2026-09-19 — a generator comment's assumption had deleted a fail-open guard

### Security

- **A control-less `bounded-write-egress` tier validated at major 2.** `schemas/capabilities.schema.json` carries an RFC 0132 §B.2 clause whose own `$comment` states its purpose: *"`writeEgressControls` is REQUIRED and non-empty when `bounded-write-egress` is advertised, and MUST be absent otherwise (**a control-less write/egress tier is the fail-open shape this RFC forbids**)."* `generate-from-declaration.mjs` deleted it — twice over, as it turned out — and the shape the RFC exists to forbid became schema-legal in v2. Proved by construction before and after: `{tiers:["bounded-write-egress"]}` with no controls validated, and now does not; a host with `writeEgressControls:["hitl"]` still validates; the `else` branch still refuses controls on a read-only tier.
  - **First cause — a comment's premise.** `for (const k of ['allOf','anyOf','oneOf']) delete out[k]; // v1 if/then gates on \`supported\`; re-decided per child` deleted **every** conditional at **every** depth. Roughly half do not gate on `supported`. Also lost: `fs` `supported ⇒ sandboxRoot` (the path-traversal root), `aiProviders` `realtimeVoice.synthesis ⇒ speechSynthesis` (RFC 0106 §A), `memory.compaction ⇒ trigger`, `memory.injectionBudget ⇒ tokenCounter` — the last two still **described** as *"enforced via the `if/then` clause"*, naming a clause that was not there. A conditional is now deleted only when it actually mentions `supported`.
  - **Second cause — the record builder overwrote it anyway.** `familyRecord()` assigned `allOf` to the two injected `status`/`until` clauses, discarding the seeded family's own. Fixing only the first cause changed nothing; the guard came back only when both were fixed.
- **Seven families had lost a required field that had nothing to do with `supported`.** `required` was rebuilt from `['status','since','witness', ...override?.required]`, dropping the seeded family's own list for every family without an override: `limits`, `envelopeContracts`, `connections`, `dataResidency`, `anonymousActor`, `nondeterminismPolicy` (a floor predicate in `lib/profiles.ts`), `content`.

**Compatibility: safety-fix** per `COMPATIBILITY.md` §3. This tightens validation and could in principle refuse a document a host emits — verified against all three bundles in `evidence/v2-host-bundles/`, all of which still validate. A MUST that can never fire is a relaxed MUST, and a deleted fail-open guard is the CVE-class justification. No wire shape, endpoint contract or error meaning changes.

## [2.15.0] — 2026-09-19 — the rules already existed; two gates could not see them

### Fixed

- **The end-of-support fallback tested the banner's STATE, not whether it CHANGED — and was already failing.** RFC 0189 §D fails after v1 end-of-support only if a carried target is *"deleted or its `Status:` banner changes"*. The implementation tested whether the banner currently reads `Stable`/`FINAL`, which is a different question. `spec/v1/multi-agent-execution.md` has read `Status: Draft` since long before `multiAgent` was carried into it in 2.13.0 — so on **2026-12-04 the daily job would have gone permanently red on a banner that never changed**. Introduced by RFC 0190 §C, which implemented the fallback RFC 0189 had only printed, one layer down from the defect it was fixing. Filed as **G7**: the banner is now recorded at declaration time in `v1Banners` and compared. Sabotage-proved — an unchanged `Draft` no longer fails; flipping `tool-catalog.md` `Stable → Withdrawn` is caught and printed was/now; a carried target with no recorded banner fails by name so the map cannot go stale.
- **Facet coverage missed the spelling v1 prose actually uses.** `namesFacet`'s qualified form kept the *family* key's leading-boundary requirement, so it matched `limits.maxBudgetTokens` but not `Capabilities.limits.maxBudgetTokens`. `spec/v1/budget-policy.md` clamps to *"the host ceiling (`Capabilities.limits.maxBudgetTokens` / `maxBudgetCostUsd`)"* — one sentence, a coordinated pair, and the gate covered the second while missing the first. G5's closure note had stated the intent correctly; the implementation applied a family rule to a facet. Filed as **G6**.

### Changed

- **`oauth`, `heartbeat` and `limits` are recorded as v1-dependent.** This tranche was held back on the premise that five of their facets had no obligation anywhere and would need new MUSTs. **Four of the five already have rules** — `oauth.grants` at `spec/v1/host-capabilities.md` §"Token lifecycle (normative)" (*"Perform the advertised grant(s)"*, enforced by `oauth-authorization-code-roundtrip.test.ts`); the three `limits` envelope rounds at `spec/v1/capabilities.md` (*"engine-enforced — the server MUST emit a `cap.breached` event AND fail the run"*); `heartbeat.maxRuntimeMs` and the budget ceilings likewise. Nothing needed inventing.
- **`heartbeat.minIntervalSec` is the one that genuinely has none, and it is not a sentence I can write.** Its rule *was* shipped — `schemas/capabilities.schema.json`: *"requests below it clamp up"* — and the v2 rewrite dropped it. But no wire surface in the corpus accepts a heartbeat interval: no path in `path-manifest.json`, none in either OpenAPI document, and `api/asyncapi.yaml` says so outright. Writing that MUST would bind an antecedent that does not exist. It is covered for §B(d) by a v1 example, which asserts nothing — which is what §B.4's naming-count design is for — and the real question, *whether heartbeat registration is a wire surface at all*, is RFC 0060's deferred scope note, not burn-down work.
- `undeclared` **52 → 49**; `v1Dependent` 4 → 7. **Open debt is unchanged at 56 and the first red stays 2026-10-03** — declaring a family v1-dependent moves the burn-down by zero, by RFC 0190 §C's explicit design. This release buys no schedule; it buys two gate fixes and three families that are now measured instead of unmeasured.

**Also rejected:** deprecating the thin facets at 3.0, on the `packs.testMode` precedent. That row's load-bearing `authority` field cites an existing v2 rule forbidding the surface. Here there is none — an `authority` reading "v2 defines nothing for it" deprecates a facet *because the steward has not written its rule*, which is circular.

**Compatibility: editorial + gate.** No wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract, error meaning or MUST strength changes; **no new RFC 2119 keyword is minted**, which is the point.

## [2.14.0] — 2026-09-19 — three more homes, and the bleed that made fourteen others look valid

### Fixed

- **`-`, `_`, `.` and `/` are not word boundaries for a family key.** `namesKey` treated every non-alphanumeric as one, so a key was "named" by any token merely **containing** it. Measured at 2.13.0: of the twenty families that satisfied §B(c), **six did so only through this** — `fs` ← *"nodes are `fs-gated`"*; `budget` ← `token_budget_exceeded`; `i18n` ← *"see i18n.md"*; `portability` ← the path `spec/v2/ext/portability/`; `workspace` ← `RunSnapshot.owner.workspace`. Filed and closed as **G5**.
  - **The dot is asymmetric, and that asymmetry is the rule.** A *following* dot names a family only when what follows is a declared facet of it — `runList.maxPageSize` genuinely names `runList` — while a *preceding* dot never does, because `owner.workspace` is the tail of a field path, not the `workspace` family. Facet coverage takes the mirror rule, since `family.facet` is a facet's canonical spelling.
  - **It caught one of my own.** `a2a`'s Step 1 resolution rested on a path match: `interop.md` genuinely homes the family, but every occurrence in an obligation paragraph was inside `spec/v2/facets/a2a.schema.json` or `a2a-0.3-legacy`. Fixed by making the prose name the family it obligates, not by relaxing the rule.
  - Sabotage-proved **with the RFC 0191 marker present**, so the predicate is tested rather than the claim rule that fires before it. G5 does **not** catch English homonyms — `cache` the verb, `production` the adjective, `content` in `content-free` — and RFC 0191 §B already says that is what a marker and a reader are for.

### Changed

- **Three families declare homes:** `sandbox` and `compensation` → `security-defaults.md`, `authorization` → `identity.md`. Every facet sentence restates an obligation the corpus already carries — the three `sandbox` bounds are the ceilings of the `node-pack-sandbox-{network-gated,fs-gated,memory-cap,timeout}` invariants; the `compensation` facets bind the shape in `compensation-policy.schema.json`; `authorization.failClosed` advertises the `authorization-fail-closed` invariant without gating it.
- `undeclared` **55 → 52**; `facetsUncovered` stays **0**. Budget **25,153 / 26,400**. Burn-down **56 open** — first red moves 2026-09-30 → **2026-10-03**.

**Fourteen of the twenty candidates were rejected as false homes**, and the ratchet is why that mattered: `facetsUncovered` may never rise, so a dishonest facet sentence is not a correctable editorial error — once written it cannot be withdrawn without an RFC. The sharpest reject was `deadLetter` → `webhooks.md`, whose matching paragraph is about `webhooks.deadLetter`, the **delivery** sink — a different thing from the `deadLetter` family. Declaring it would have put a claim in the Status banner that the document contradicts in its own body.

**Compatibility: editorial + gate.** `generate-from-declaration --check` byte-identical; no wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract, error meaning or MUST strength changes.

## [2.13.0] — 2026-09-18 — four families find their homes, and a table that was one paragraph

### Fixed

- **A markdown table is not one paragraph, and the normative-home gate thought it was.** `check-v2-normative-home.mjs` split obligation prose on `/\n\s*\n/`, which collapsed an entire table into a single block — so an RFC 2119 keyword **anywhere** in a table satisfied **every** family named anywhere in it. `spec/v2/core/versioning.md`'s 18-axis disposition table is one 20-line block: `multiAgent` (row 13) and `schemaVersions` (row 6) were both satisfied by the `MUST` in **row 15**, which is about `minClientVersion`. Both rows name `events.md` as their owner *in their own Owner column*, and `events.md` mentions neither family. The gate green-lit both declarations **with the RFC 0191 marker in place** — so that RFC's "explicit, local and reviewable" guarantee was being read against a reference table where the claim is contradicted three rows away. Eleven of the then-57 undeclared families had a table-only match waiting. Filed and closed as **G4**; table rows are now their own units. Of the five tightenings measured across RFC 0191 and this one, it is the only one that breaks no honest declaration — it strictly narrows, and no resolved family relies on a cross-row match.
- **RFC 0189's `Active → Accepted` criterion was arithmetically unsatisfiable.** It required `facetsUncovered` and `undeclared + v1Dependent` *both strictly below their filing values*. `facetsUncovered` filed at **0**, and §C forbids it rising — so "strictly below 0" could never hold and the RFC could never have flipped, however many families landed. Amended in place while `Active` (RFC 0174 §A.4) to "has not risen".

### Changed

- **Four core families declare v2 homes:** `a2a` and `mcp` → `interop.md` (which states in its own first sentence that it is the v2 contract for both, and covers 18 of 18 facets); `auth` → `identity.md` (§"Every lane MUST verify … bind … check audience … fail closed"); `runList` → `runs.md` §List — where `capabilities.md` **already** pointed it, so the declaration is the corpus catching up to a cross-reference it had made.
- **Two families are recorded as honestly v1-dependent** rather than left unmeasured: `multiAgent` → `spec/v1/multi-agent-execution.md` and `schemaVersions` → `spec/v1/capabilities.md`. Both were candidates in the original sweep and both were **false homes** under G4. They cost no budget words (v1 is outside the measured set) and do not move the burn-down by design (RFC 0190 §C), but they put a real v1 dependency where the §D fallback can see it; `v1Carried` grows to four.
- `undeclared` **61 → 55**, `v1Dependent` 2 → 4. Burn-down **59 open against 62 allowed** on 2026-09-25 — the line that would have gone red in seven days now clears, with first red 2026-09-30. Budget **24,979 / 25,800**: the batch spent **13 words and earned 800**, leaving the next tranche 821 words of headroom it did not have.

**Compatibility: editorial + gate.** No wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract, error meaning or MUST strength changes; `generate-from-declaration --check` is byte-identical. The marker lines carry no RFC 2119 keyword.

## [2.12.0] — 2026-09-18 — RFC 0191: no regex over prose decides a semantic claim, and a sentence that described a structure the file does not have

### Fixed

- **`spec/v2/core/packs.md` §"Peer-dependency identifiers" stated a false structure.** It read *"a `peerDependencies` key MUST be a root key of `spec/v2/declaration.json`"*. The root keys of that file are `$schema`, `$comment`, `version`, `updated`, `witnessClasses`, `extensionsKeyPattern`, `reservedOrgs`, `extensions`, `metadata`, `families`, `profiles`, `aliases` — **`aiProviders` is not among them**; it is a `families[].key`. Implemented literally, a gate written from that sentence rejects **every** published peer-dependency declaration. The sentence also dropped the overlap-alias clause that the same document's §Errors table carries, and **23 of 28 distinct keys in the frozen v1 tree are alias-only** (`host.agentRuntime` ×41). Two sentences in one document disagreeing, with the wrong one being the normative rule.
- **`generate-cross-repo-evidence.mjs` witnessed a v2 rule against the v1 tree.** `registryPeerDependencyKeys()` was hardcoded to `registry/v1/packs`, so `evidence/cross-repo-manifests.json` — which `check-declaration.mjs` rule 7 reads to enforce `pack_peer_dependency_undefined` — measured the one tree the v2 rule does not bind. The v2 tree had never been inventoried. Now both: **44 keys, was 28**.
- **RFC 0190 G2, closed as a concrete defect.** `check-core-budget.mjs` counted a family as homed on `normativeText` being non-empty and not v1 — so a family homed at an RFC, or at a path that does not exist, granted **200 words of cap** while `check-v2-normative-home.mjs` hard-refused it. Inside `openwop:check` both run and the tree reds, but the budget is also a standalone script and reported a cap 200 too high.

### Added

- **RFC 0191 — the reciprocal normative-home marker.** A declared `spec/v2/**` home MUST itself claim the family, in a `> **Normative home:** \`key\`.` line under its Status banner. Nine core documents seeded in the same PR, because a predicate the corpus does not satisfy is not yet a rule.
  - **Why a marker and not a better regex.** Four syntactic candidates were measured against all eleven declared families and a 24-case known-bad set, and **all four failed**: requiring backticks keeps 7 of 11, key-in-a-heading keeps 5 of 11, key-in-the-H1 keeps **0** — because honest prose names a family **in words** ("Connection Packs", "Form Content Packs", "Replay and Fork"). Word-normalising the heading matcher immediately admits `replay` ← `webhooks.md` §"Replay". A concentration ratchet makes editing `runs.md` break `interrupt`'s home. The measured ceiling was also worse than the gap register recorded: at 2.11.0 `replay` and `interrupt` each have an obligation paragraph naming them in **nine** core documents, `idempotency` in eight, `packs` in seven including `security-defaults.md` and `versioning.md`.
  - **What it does and does not achieve, stated in the RFC rather than implied.** It does **not** make a false declaration impossible — someone can edit `security-defaults.md` to claim it homes `packs`. It makes one **explicit, local and reviewable**: the cheapest green path becomes writing a false sentence into a document whose own prose contradicts it, in the diff, in a file CODEOWNERS routes to the lead maintainer. Claiming mechanical soundness for a marker would repeat the defect RFC 0189's Motivation exists to end.
- **`spec/v2/core/packs.md` §"The engine range" gains the truth condition.** Admitting major M asserts that the manifest validates against the `schemas/v2/` schema **for its `kind`**, that every `peerDependencies` key resolves, and that the version carries a §Signing signature — **each conjunct keeping its own refusal code**, since folding them into `pack_engine_unsupported` would collide with `pack_peer_dependency_undefined`, which already exists. A mechanical ceiling bump is not a verification.

### Changed

- **RFC 0189 G1 closed** (by RFC 0191) and **RFC 0190 G2 closed**. Zero of 473 published manifests and zero of 156 source packs become inadmissible under the stated engine-range rule — measured across all three registry surfaces before writing it.

**Compatibility: additive.** The engine-range truth condition makes a previously-undefined relationship explicit (`COMPATIBILITY.md` §4 row 6) without changing any error code's meaning; §4 row 4 does not fire, because nothing published becomes inadmissible. The marker and the gate fixes are `editorial + gate`.

## [2.11.0] — 2026-09-18 — RFC 0190: the budget was measuring the wrong files, against a number set for a different problem

### Added

- **RFC 0190**, superseding **RFC 0174 §E.2/§E.2a**. Two defects, one instrument change.
  - **The denominator did not follow the gate's acceptance.** `check-v2-normative-home.mjs` accepts `spec/v2/ext/**` as a home for a **core** family; `check-core-budget.mjs` never counted it. A family's entire contract could move to `ext/` and resolve at **zero budget cost with no stub left in `core/` at all** — sabotage-proved with a 242-word ext README that resolved `agents` (19 facets) and left the budget unmoved. `readdirSync` rather than a recursive walk made `spec/v2/core/<sub>/x.md` the same hole one directory shallower. Nobody had to intend it; it was simply the cheapest way to satisfy the gate.
  - **The number was set against the wrong denominator.** §E.2a rejected raising the ceiling on 2026-09-17 reasoning that *"16 core RFCs have landed against it"*. RFC 0189 was created the **next day** and turned 61 undeclared families into dated work. Measured against the four families actually written (563 words, mean **140.75**), the remainder needs ≈ **9,170** words. The ceiling had **164**. The rejection's basis was falsified by information that did not exist when it was made.
  - **The rule now:** the budget measures every document a core family's `normativeText[]` points at — `spec/v2/core/**` **recursively**, plus any `spec/v2/ext/**` document a core family cites. Where prose is *filed* becomes an editorial decision with no budget consequence, which dissolves the unenforceable question "is this the base contract or a facet detail?" rather than answering it. The cap is `25,000 + 200 × (resolved homes − 9)` — **self-funding**, since 200 granted exceeds the 141 measured cost, and ungameable, since words arrive only when a family passes both the §B predicate and the `facetsUncovered` ratchet. At 72/72 it freezes at 37,600.

### Fixed

- **`spec/v2/ext/` is now a co-pointer, never a sole home** (RFC 0190 §B, amending RFC 0189 §A in place — legal while `Active` per RFC 0174 §A.4). Ext prose contributes facet coverage and nothing else. This also closed a hole the sabotage found: every declared ext README carries the boilerplate *"clients MUST NOT infer portable operations, payloads, or authorization semantics from its presence"*, which resolved the core `authorization` family against **thirteen** unrelated ext pages. Eight core families carry zero facets and would have had no ratchet backstop at all.
- **RFC 0189 §D's fallback was printed and never applied — by the RFC whose own Motivation exists to end exactly that.** The gate described the fallback (`spec/v1/**` frozen-but-operative for `v1Carried`, failing only on a deleted target or a changed `Status:` banner) and then `process.exit(1)` regardless; `v1Carried` was read into the baseline object and never used. On 2026-12-04 the daily job would have gone permanently red in the world §D calls acceptable. Now applied: sabotage-proved in both directions — past end-of-support it names each undeclared family, and flipping `spec/v1/tool-catalog.md`'s banner `Stable → Draft` is caught and quoted.
- **`v1Carried` must equal the computed v1-dependent set.** §C leaves `v1Dependent` unconstrained upward on purpose, and nothing checked that a newly v1-dependent family also entered `v1Carried` — which is what the fallback covers. A family could have been v1-dependent, uncovered by the fallback, and green until the day it ran.

### Changed

- **Gap G2 closed** — and its filed premise was wrong twice: no core family declares an ext home (so the fix had **zero** retroactive cost), and the hole needed no `core/` stub at all. **G3 closed with the opposite decision to the one it proposed**: `open` keeps counting `v1Carried`, because that array is hand-written and edited by the declaring PR, so excluding it would make "add your family to `v1Carried`" a zero-work way to lower `open`. A fallback is what happens when you miss the target, not an exemption from it. Closed in the code instead.

**Compatibility: editorial + gate.** No wire artifact, schema shape, endpoint contract, error meaning or MUST strength changes. What moves is a number in a corpus gate and the set of files it measures.

## [2.10.0] — 2026-09-18 — the last Tranche-1 family, and a deprecation row that certified its own absence

### Added

- **`spec/v2/core/packs.md` §"The `packs` capability"** — the family's advertisement contract, which the document did not carry. Before this, `packs` satisfied the RFC 0189 §B(c) predicate on exactly **one** paragraph, and it matched on a URL path segment (`registry/v2/packs/<name>/…` in §"The registry tree") rather than on anything about the family — the precise failure mode gap **G1** in `RFCS/registers/0189-normative-home.gaps.md` describes. `packs` now declares its home; `undeclared` **62 → 61**, `facetsUncovered` stays **0**.

### Deprecated

- **`capabilities.packs.testMode`** and **`capabilities.observability.testSeams`**, both `removeIn: "3.0"`. Both are test-seam flags in the capability namespace, which `spec/v2/core/conformance.md` §"The seams profile" forbids outright — seams are the profile `openwop-conformance-seams-v2` in the `/conformance/seams/` path space. **Neither could be removed at 2.x**, and that asymmetry against 2.8.2 is the whole architectural content of this release: the two `workflowChainPacks` facets removed then were advertised by **no** host, while `evidence/v2-host-bundles/myndhyve.json` emits **both** of these and `openwop-host-v2-reference.json` emits `packs.testMode`. The v2 root is `additionalProperties: false`, so deleting either property makes a live discovery document stop validating on the day the schema ships, with no host change available — `COMPATIBILITY.md` §2.2, with §3's second clause failing because deprecation is available and adequate.

### Fixed

- **A deprecation row certified its own absence, and the gate structurally could not catch it.** `openwop.deprecation.seam-operations-in-canonical-api` flipped `proposed → deprecated` on 2026-09-17 with the note *"the surface has no occurrence in spec/v2, schemas/v2 or api/v2 (token grep, W7)"*. Three residues were present at that moment. `check-removal-dates.mjs` only ever reads a row's **declared `sources[]`**, and that row declared exactly one — a v1 path — so a row could certify its own absence by declaring too few sources. The row is now narrowed to the path **operations** (whose `removeIn: "2.0"` it genuinely did satisfy) and the two capability facets carry their own `3.0` rows. The second lesson is the one worth keeping: **a row that bundles surfaces with different removal horizons cannot be honestly satisfied at either.**
- **An orphaned `packs-test` tag in `api/v2/openapi.yaml`** — zero operations, citing RFC 0025 as `Draft` (`Accepted` since 2026-05-29) and a `capabilities.packs.testMode.supported` gate whose field v2 dropped. The v2 document inherits `tags` wholesale from v1, so every tag whose operations were moved out (the seams) or deleted at the cut survived as an orphan. `derive-v2-api.py` now prunes a tag no operation carries, the same principle `prune_unused` already applied to components.
- **A tag-casing split in the same document.** The generator's injected operations carried `Host` / `Runs` / `Webhooks` while the inherited vocabulary is lowercase, leaving three tags used-but-undeclared. Now aligned: **zero** used-but-undeclared, **zero** declared-but-unused.

**Compatibility: three parts, deliberately not rounded to one.** The two facet rows are a **deprecation** (additive in effect — nothing stops validating and no host changes). The tag deletion and casing alignment are a **safety-fix** on dead metadata no host emits. The prose and `normativeText` are **editorial + gate** per RFC 0189 §Compatibility.

## [2.9.2] — 2026-09-18 — a suite scenario asserted a tool list its own fake server contradicted

### Fixed

- **`mcp-2026-07-28-discover` could not be passed by a faithful host.** `McpFakeServer` answers `tools/list` with three tools — `echo`, `needs_input`, `needs_input_loop` — unconditionally (`src/lib/mcp-fake-server.ts`), while the scenario asserted `['echo', 'needs_input']`. `needs_input_loop` is the MRTR tool added to the fake server in 2.4.0 so the `mrtr-rounds-ceiling` leg could reach a ceiling ≥ 1; the assertion was never moved with it, and the `it` is literally titled *"…and lists the MRTR tool"*. A host that proxies the fake server faithfully returns all three and reds.
- **It was invisible here and fatal there.** The corpus gate quarantines the scenario, so `openwop:check` stayed green while every host certify run — which forces `OPENWOP_CONFORMANCE_NO_QUARANTINE=1` by design, because a certification that skips its own known-bad rows certifies nothing — recorded `executed-fail`. Reported from the openwop-app host with two file:line citations against the same published release. A quarantine that hides a defect from the steward and not from the hosts is the wrong way round.

## [2.9.1] — 2026-09-18 — RFC 0189's own gap register

### Added

- **`RFCS/registers/0189-normative-home.gaps.md`** — RFC 0189 shipped without one, and three real gaps surfaced while operating it had nowhere to live except a CHANGELOG bullet, which is not a tracked row. **G1:** `namesKey` is a bare token match, so a family key that is a common English noun is satisfied by prose that merely uses the word — measured with the gate's own predicate, `packs` has an obligation paragraph naming it in **7** core documents (including `security-defaults.md` and `versioning.md`) and `idempotency` in **7**. Both obvious tightenings were measured and both misfire: requiring backticks fails 4 of the 7 families resolved today, because honest prose names a family in words rather than as a code token. **G2:** 62 families must find homes inside a kernel budget with **305 words free**, while `spec/v2/ext/**` is a legal home that the budget does not count — left implicit, `ext/` silently becomes "core, but unbudgeted". **G3:** the burn-down counts `v1Carried` families toward a target of zero, though §D explicitly tolerates them past end-of-support.
- All three are `carried`, not `open` — they are recorded and owned, and `docs/witness-baseline.json` `openGaps` stays at **0**, which is the ratchet that makes a new open row something a steward must argue for.

## [2.9.0] — 2026-09-18 — three families that v2 required but never wrote down

### Added
