- **`eventLog` was required by the `openwop-core-standard` profile predicate and had no v2 normative text at all.** Every v2 occurrence of the token was `eventLogSchemaVersion` — the era *metadata key* — plus a one-line stub in `capabilities.md`. Its four floor scenarios (`v2-era-key`, `v2-era-stamp-universal`, `v2-event-type-closed`, `v2-poll-cursor-v2`) all cite `persistence.md`, `runs.md` or `events.md`; none cited the family. A published profile predicate requiring a family whose behaviour is nowhere written in the current major is a profile-honesty problem. `persistence.md` §"The `eventLog` family" now states what the record advertises — the era stamp, the poll cursor, the closed event-type set — so the floor scenarios have a home that names what they gate on.
- **`replay.md` §"Cross-engine ordering"** carries the `eventLog.crossEngineOrdering` facet, whose only contract was `spec/v1/replay.md` (RFC 0036) — while the reference host advertises it on the wire today. It lands in `replay.md` rather than `persistence.md` because it is a fork-determinism property: it constrains observable state at the `fromSeq` boundary, next to the fork contract it bounds.
- **`idempotency.md` §"Multi-region"** distinguishes the two region facets, which claimed different things and neither of which had v2 text. `crossRegion` is the deployment posture (all three hosts emit `"single-region"` with no v2 contract behind the value); `multiRegion` is the behavioural claim. A client MUST NOT infer the second from the first.
- **`form-content-packs.md` §"Instantiation"** — the `forms.contentPacks` facet orphaned a **protocol-tier, high-severity** SECURITY invariant. `form-content-pack-string-trust-boundary` is normed in `spec/v1/form-content-packs.md` §"Instantiation", and the v2 capability schema cites that section — **which did not exist in the v2 document**. v2 carried the narrower escaping rule under §"Localized strings" but not the part that protects the prompt path: a pack-authored string MUST propagate `meta.contentTrust: "untrusted"` into an interpolated prompt segment. `contentTrust` appeared in four v2 schemas and **zero** v2 prose files.

### Changed

- `eventLog`, `idempotency` and `forms` declare their normative homes; `docs/normative-home-baseline.json` lowers `undeclared` 65 → 62, and the RFC 0189 burn-down gains about a week of runway (64 open against 67 allowed on 2026-09-20).

**Compatibility: additive.** No wire shape moves, no host's discovery document stops validating, and no new host obligation is created: each section carries forward a contract that already bound a conforming host — RFC 0036 for cross-engine ordering and `multiRegion`, `spec/v1/form-content-packs.md` for the `contentTrust` propagation, and MUSTs already in `persistence.md` / `events.md` for the era contract `eventLog` advertises. This is the remediation RFC 0189 §D schedules, not new protocol.

## [2.8.3] — 2026-09-18 — a host could not honour a protocol-tier MUST, because the code it names was unregistered

### Security

- **`credential_scope_forbidden` was absent from the v2 error registry while `spec/v1/capabilities.md` §B.8 makes emitting it a MUST.** §B.8 is the wire-level safety rail against sharing one individual's personal, single-seat subscription across a multi-user tenant: a host "MUST reject an attempt to bind one at that scope with the canonical error code `credential_scope_forbidden`" (SECURITY invariant `subscription-credential-user-scope-only`, **protocol tier**). The code was in none of the 98 rows of `spec/v2/errors.json`, and `schemas/v2/error-envelope.schema.json` generates `error` as a **closed** enum from that registry — the vendor-code branch does not admit it either, since it carries no vendor prefix. A v2 host obeying §B.8 therefore emitted an envelope that **failed its own published response schema**, and `conformance/src/scenarios/aiproviders-subscription-scope.test.ts` asserts that literal string. Registered at **403**, the status steward-curl-witnessed on the reference host (`INTEROP-MATRIX.md` §`aiProviders.authModes`). Not collapsed into `credential_forbidden` (also 403): §B.8 names this code specifically, and `credential_scope_unsupported` (400) is a different semantic — scope unsupported, not scope forbidden for this mode.

### Fixed

- **`spec/v2/core/errors.md` claimed twice to be generated, and nothing generated or checked it.** The doc said "Generated from `spec/v2/errors.json`" above a hand-written `Code | Status` table and named the code count in two places; no script wrote or verified either. Adding a registry row would have left a published table silently one code short — the same green-by-silence shape RFC 0189 exists to close. `scripts/generate-error-envelope.mjs` now derives both counts and the status table, and fails on drift (sabotage-proven: it exits 1 when a count is edited by hand).

## [2.8.2] — 2026-09-18 — two facets that advertised contracts v2 does not have

### Fixed

- **`workflowChainPacks.deferredParameters` let a host truthfully advertise a capability v2.0 does not define.** `spec/v2/core/workflow-chain-packs.md` §"Parameter substitution" states that the portable per-run deferral "is a named v2.x additive RFC and is not part of v2.0" — while `declaration.json` bound `deferredParameters` as an advertisable facet with a full property in the closed v2 capabilities schema, and its witness seam (`POST /v1/host/sample/chain/deferred-expand`) is in the set `spec/v1/deprecations.json` removes at 2.0 and is absent from `api/seams-v2.yaml`. A host reading the declaration could advertise a facet with no v2 text behind it and no seam to witness it. Removed from the family's facet set **and** from the published schema.
- **`workflowChainPacks.hostExpansionSeam` was a test-seam flag in the capability namespace, which v2 forbids.** Its own schema description calls it "a **conformance-only test seam** advertisement … NOT a product capability", serving `POST /v1/host/sample/workflow-chain:expand`. `spec/v2/core/conformance.md` §"The seams profile" is explicit: test seams are the profile `openwop-conformance-seams-v2` in the `/conformance/seams/…` path space, and "a `testSeams` capability flag does not exist and MUST NOT be advertised." Removed from both surfaces.
- **The facet set is now pinned by a file rather than seeded from v1.** `spec/v2/facets/workflowChainPacks.schema.json` joins the ten existing hand-decided facet overrides, so the v2 facet list is a decision on the record instead of whatever v1 happened to carry.

### Changed

- `workflowChainPacks` declares its normative home — `spec/v2/core/workflow-chain-packs.md`, whose §"Composition depth" already carries the `subChains` MUST. The family resolves under RFC 0189 §C with no new prose, and `docs/normative-home-baseline.json` lowers `undeclared` 66 → 65.

**Compatibility: safety-fix** per `COMPATIBILITY.md` §3. Both removals delete a property from a closed published record, but each advertised a contract v2 explicitly does not have or a seam path v2 deleted, and **no v2 host bundle advertises either** — verified against all three bundles in `evidence/v2-host-bundles/`.
## [2.8.1] — 2026-09-18 — the extension pages, formatted for reading

- **The v2 extension index and every extension page are re-formatted for reading.** `spec/v2/ext/README.md`'s status table — one row per label, cells carrying whole paragraphs — is now three `###` subsections (`Draft`, `Stable`, `Retired`) with each rule spelled out as prose and its gate predicate named. All seventeen extension READMEs re-wrap the long v1-migration links and field descriptions that ran past the margin, and three metadata cells are said plainly rather than in a parenthetical: `provider-idempotency`'s advertised-as is *"not applicable; supporting registry only"*, `sandbox-runtime-notes` names its owning RFCs as `RFC 0173 §D; RFC 0035 (superseded)`, and `portability` names `RFC 0168 §C.1; RFC 0174 §E.2; RFCs 0086 and 0087 (v1 text)`. No status, witness-class or RFC 2119 word changed.

## [2.8.0] — 2026-09-18 — RFC 0189: the gate that was measuring nothing, given a contract

### Fixed

- **The v1 normative-home deadline could not fire, for two independent reasons.** `check-v2-normative-home.mjs` computed the days remaining and **never compared them**, and **nothing scheduled the gate** — `openwop:check` runs on push and pull request only. Its own output said the open count *"becomes one on the date, silently, unless this number reaches zero first."* That sentence described a check the code did not perform, which is the exact shape this corpus has been burned by twice and written into two workflow headers. The comparison is implemented, the sentence is deleted, and a daily `--deadline` step runs it on `main`.
- **The ratchet rewarded silence.** It failed when `v1Dependent` ROSE — which is precisely what the first *honest* declaration does: recording that a family's behaviour genuinely lives in `spec/v1/` moves it out of `undeclared` and into `v1-dependent`, gaining information while leaving total debt unchanged, and the gate exited 1 on it. So the cheapest way to stay green was to declare nothing, on 70 of 72 rows. Now `undeclared` may never rise and the **sum** may never rise, while `v1Dependent` alone is unconstrained upward. Sabotage-proved: an honest `spec/v1/` declaration now passes where it used to fail.
- **`existsSync` was the entire content check** — `normativeText: ["README.md"]` on all 72 families would have printed `72 resolved` and exited 0. A declared home must now be a legal class, **name its family**, and carry an RFC 2119 obligation **in a paragraph that names it** (paragraph scope, because document scope passes on almost any v2 core doc). Both sabotage checks refuse: `README.md`, and the declaration site itself — the latter mattering because every core family's `section` is already `core/capabilities.md#<key>` and every one of those bodies is a one-line stub, so accepting it would have resolved all 72 for free.
- **The gate had no RFC at all** — `normativeText` appeared nowhere in `RFCS/`, so `check-falsifiability` and `check-accepted-predicate`, both strict for RFC ≥ 0167, could not see it. A gate invisible to the corpus's own acceptance predicate is the class of artifact this gate exists to catch.

### Added

- **RFC 0189**, with the four rules above and a falsifiability row each.
- **The deadline is a burn-down, not a cliff.** The baseline records `t0` and `openAtT0`; the allowed open count decays linearly to zero at end-of-support, so a slipping schedule is detected the week it slips rather than on the day nothing can be done. And the fallback is **named rather than discovered**: this RFC cannot move the date (the clock is generated from the matrix and bundle history), so at end-of-support `spec/v1/**` is **frozen-but-operative** for the families in `v1Carried` — end-of-support ends new v1 *wire* support, it does not de-normativize prose a v2 family still points at. After the date the gate fails only if one of those targets is deleted or its `Status:` banner changes, which is the real hazard.
- **`facetsUncovered`, a third ratchet.** A family's base contract can be fully v2-resident while one facet is written only in `spec/v1/`; counting rather than failing keeps one orphaned facet from blocking a real declaration, while making facet orphaning a number that may only fall.
- **Tranche 0: four families declared** — `webhooks`, `replay`, `interrupt`, `connections` — each verified against the new predicate rather than asserted. `undeclared` falls 70 → 66.

### Known

- **68 families remain, and one is urgent independently of the date:** `openwop-core-standard` **requires** the `eventLog` family, and `spec/v2/core/` carries no normative text for it at all — every v2 mention is the unrelated `eventLogSchemaVersion` metadata key. A published profile predicate requiring a family whose behaviour is nowhere written in the current major is a profile-honesty problem, and it is the next tranche's first item.

## [2.7.1] — 2026-09-18 — zero open gap rows, and a ratchet that keeps it there

### Changed

- **The corpus's last eleven `open` gap rows are disposed of, and nine of them were already answered.** RFC 0111's five and RFC 0121's six were the only `open` rows left anywhere. **Seven closed by fact** — the tree had answered the question and the register had not caught up: `tokenCounter` ships the exact portable enum the row proposed as its default; the transcript seam shipped as a host-sample seam, which is the "avoid a new normative REST surface" answer the row asked for; replay determinism is settled normatively in three places (reuse the recorded `summaryRef`, never re-summarize — a better answer than the question's framing); `keepLastTurns` shipped while `keepLastTokens` exists nowhere in the corpus but that gap row; the five-mode auth enum and its assertion have been shipping since conformance 1.47.0; no dedicated `subscription` schema shape was ever minted because the RFC 0046 credential reference sufficed; and the error envelope needed no extra fields. **Two closed by ruling**, both written down rather than left implicit. **Two re-tokened `externally-gated`**, because they say a steward owes work and no steward does.
- **RFC 0121 G1 is `externally-gated:provider-tos-clearance` — unownable, and now labelled as such.** Whether a provider's terms permit API-shaped automation under a reused consumer subscription is a legal question about third parties' contracts: no artifact in any repository answers it and no design work moves it. A new tripwire name is used deliberately; `unspecified` would erase the one precise thing about the gap.
- **RFC 0121 G5 is `transferred:docs/KNOWN-LIMITS.md`.** The row proposed an audit-event MUST for `subscription`-mode credential resolution. On a Parked RFC no host may advertise, that would be a requirement **nothing could ever witness** — no scenario could exercise it, no bundle could carry a row — which reads as a protection that does not exist. Recorded as a known limit instead, with the reasoning.

### Added

- **`openGaps: 0` in `docs/witness-baseline.json`, enforced by `check-registers.mjs`.** Zero is the only baseline value that makes the `open` token self-policing: with it, a new open row has to be argued for; without it, open rows accumulate and the count is a statistic rather than a bound. Sabotage-proved by re-opening one row and watching the gate name it.

### Fixed

- **RFC 0188 left a stub stale one release ago.** `spec/v2/core/capabilities.md` §webhooks still read *"owner no owning RFC (declaration row); facets `signatureAlgorithms`"* while the declaration says `owningRfc: 0188` and three facets. `check-declaration.mjs` verifies the heading exists and nothing about the body, so it passed clean — the same shape of gap this sweep exists to close.

### Known

- **`credential_scope_forbidden` is absent from `spec/v2/errors.json`** while `capabilities.md` §B.8 makes emitting it a MUST — so a v2 host implementing that section has no registered code to emit. Surfaced by the G6 disposition; it is a v2-era registry gap, not an RFC 0121 design gap, and is filed rather than folded into this sweep.

## [2.7.0] — 2026-09-18 — RFC 0188: the dead-letter read, and a bound kind that finally has somewhere to appear

### Added

- **RFC 0188 — `GET /webhooks/{webhookId}/dead-letters`.** `webhooks.md` §Durability has required since the v2 cut that a host route an exhausted delivery to a sink *"inspectable for `retentionDays`"* — and the corpus served no endpoint that could inspect it. Every committed host bundle records the same sentence: *"exhaustion was observed, routing to the sink was not."* A normative obligation no observer could check. This is that read, gated on a new `webhooks.deadLetter` facet, with the `listRuns` pagination idiom rather than a second dialect.
- **The `deliveryId` kind has a wire surface for the first time.** `identity.md` §5 bound it; its only `$ref` was an in-run payload that never reaches the durable log; so `v2-bound-id-kinds` recorded a **corpus** gap — *"the kind has nowhere to appear on the wire, so no host can be held to it."* The response seats it, and that leg now asserts the bound grammar and the caller's tenant segment instead of soft-skipping.
- **Two dead-letter sinks, disentangled.** Three documents routed *delivery* dead-letters to "the RFC 0053 sink". RFC 0053 is **run-scoped and says so twice**. The new facet is the delivery sink; the RFC 0053 family remains the run sink, whose `retentionDays` governs a dead-lettered run's fork-eligibility window. Meanwhile the `webhooks` family had `owningRfc: null` — a v2 MUST asserted by three documents and owned by none. RFC 0188 claims it.
- **`dead-letter-read-carries-no-payload`** (SECURITY invariant, protocol tier, with §4.10 of the secret-leakage threat model). A dead-letter queue is, by construction, **every event the subscriber failed to receive** — so a record carrying the delivered body would turn one read scope into a complete replay of exactly that traffic, retained for the whole window, wearing the costume of a diagnostic. The response schema is `additionalProperties: false` over a closed field list, so a host cannot add one without failing validation.
- **`expiresAt` makes `retentionDays` observable.** It was an advertisement nothing could check; a reader can now compare it against the facet.

### Fixed

- **RFC 0053's status note read as a live contradiction.** Its header says `Accepted` while a dated 2026-05-25 implementation note said *"Status stays `Draft`."* That was a record of the filing PR, not a standing claim — clarified, and scoped, since RFC 0188 now cites this RFC for the run/delivery split.

## [2.6.1] — 2026-09-18 — the correction that half-landed

### Fixed

- **2.6.0 removed the unsatisfiable `deliveryId == causationId` claim from one of the two schemas that asserted it.** The other survived verbatim: `run-event-payloads.schema.json`'s `triggerDeliveryAttempted.runId` still said the run's `run.started` *"carries this delivery's id as `causationId`"*. Same empty-grammar intersection — `deliveryId` requires a `/`, `causationId` is bound to the `eventId` kind whose class admits none — and **the surviving site was the more load-bearing of the two**: `triggerDeliveryAttempted` is a durable event-log payload that rides replay and `:fork`, while `trigger-event` is an in-run payload that never reaches the log. `COMPATIBILITY.md` meanwhile asserted the correction had landed, which is worse than not claiming one; that register entry now records both halves. Same Class 3 classification, same reasoning: nothing conforming stops conforming, because conforming was impossible.
- **The seat pointer named the wrong document, and the wrong direction.** `trigger-event.schema.json` said the v2 seat for the causing-delivery pointer *"belongs in `spec/v2/core/webhooks.md`"*. That document is **outbound** — a host POSTs matching events to a registered URL — while this pointer is **inbound**: an external event arrives and a run starts. The seat belongs on the durable event-log payload, in a trigger-ingestion document `spec/v2/core/` does not yet have. Corrected to say so rather than send the next reader to the opposite end of the system.

## [2.6.0] — 2026-09-18 — a relaxed bundle stops calling itself certified, and the umbrella stops passing on nothing

### Fixed

- **The emitter wrote `certified: true` for a profile that cannot certify.** `security-defaults.md` §Relaxations is explicit — *"A bundle that records a relaxation MUST NOT certify the profile the relaxed obligation belongs to"* (RFC 0173 §A.2) — and `cli.ts` computed `certified` with **no relaxation term**, parsing relaxations on the *next* line, so it was structurally impossible for one to affect the verdict. It then excluded `relaxed-profile-certified` from its own self-check and exited 0. The shipped artifact asserted a certification the verifier refuses, and only `--verify` disagreed with the file — so a reader who parses `.certified`, the obvious thing to do, got the wrong answer. Relaxations are now parsed *before* the verdict and folded into it, matched the same way the verifier matches them, and the emitter no longer excuses the contradiction.
- **`spec/v2/core/security-defaults.md` named a durability value that does not exist.** The table said `permanent`; the schema enum, RFC 0173 §A.2 and the TypeScript type all say `persisted`. A host following the current major's normative prose literally got an Ajv rejection and `exit 2`. One word, in the v2 tree, and it could only ever unblock — recorded as an erratum, not a relaxation.
- **RFC 0167 — the v2 umbrella — passed RFC 0174 §B.1 rule 4 on nothing.** Its falsifiability table named **zero** requirement ids, so rule 4 took the verdict-declared branch and never looked anything up: the largest flip in the program was vacuously witnessed. The evidence always existed and hard-failed in the merge gate; only the binding was missing. Five corpus ids now bind it — `child-parts` (asserting **both** scripts its row cites, not just the first), `migration-deprecation-bound`, `codemod-legs`, `codemod-at-active`, `alias-covered` — through five wrappers in `src/coherence/`, which by RFC 0168 §D.1 land in the corpus ledger and never in a host bundle. **One of them exercises a row the merge gate never ran:** `openwop-check.sh` invokes `check-codemods.mjs` without `--at-active`, so §D.3's second row was only ever checked at a cut. The wrapper spawns it explicitly.
- **RFC 0183's table named no id either, and now says why.** `interrupt-approval.test.ts` is registered at major 1 while every committed bundle is major 2, so no bundle could carry a row for an `openwop.requirement.0183.*` id — minting one would make rule 4 *fail*. The rows are verdict-declared for that reason alone, stated in the RFC rather than left looking vacuous. Its stale sentence claiming *"the fixture gains `refine`"* is corrected: two new fixtures shipped instead, and `conformance-approval` was deliberately left alone because widening it would force every host to re-register a workflow definition for a test-only change.

### Changed

- **RFC 0174 §B.1 admits a cross-RFC requirement-id citation**, editorially under §A.4 and recorded in `Updated`. Twelve rows already rely on it — this RFC's own included — and both gates already resolve an id by string lookup without comparing it to the citing RFC's number, so the sentence states existing behaviour and adds no obligation. What it refuses is the real hazard: a **new** normative requirement borrowing an old witness, a citation to an id whose owning RFC is not `Active`/`Accepted`, and a citation to an id carrying no `executed-pass` row of its own — a citation is never stronger than the evidence it points at.
- **The per-document version stamp is removed from all 20 `spec/v2/core/*.md` banners**, and from the generator that writes `headers.md`. It was stale on 19 of 20 and **never accurate**: the whole stamp landed in one bulk commit dated a day before the date it claims, and ten documents were edited afterwards without it moving. The deeper reason is structural — `spec/v2/release.json` is *"the one release identity the v2 artifacts derive from"* and RFC 0172 §D.1 makes the corpus tag the only release event, so a hand-maintained per-document version is a **second release identity contradicting the rule it sits under**. A gate asserting it equals the corpus version would be satisfied by a sed over 20 files per release: that measures churn, not freshness. `Status: Stable · RFC …` remains, and is gate-checked.

### Added

- **`docs/runbooks/KEY-ROTATION.md`**, which `VENDOR-ONBOARDING.md` has linked to without it existing. It leads with the split it needs to lead with: **a pack can be re-signed and a certification bundle cannot**, which is why a registry key may be removed after a re-sign wave and a host signing key MUST stay published forever. It also states plainly that `retiredAt` is advisory — no verifier reads it — and why that cannot be fixed at the runbook layer: `generatedAt` sits *outside* the four signed members, so "signed before retirement" is not decidable from the bundle, and making it decidable means changing a schema `const` that would invalidate every existing bundle's `over` value.
- **`OPENWOP_HOST_RELAXATIONS` and `OPENWOP_TARGET_MAJOR` are documented**, both of which change what a bundle *claims*. The first relaxes nothing in the suite and the suite cannot detect an undeclared relaxation; the second selects the scenario lane and, unlike `--target-major`, **silently falls back to major 1** on any value other than the literal `"2"` — which once produced a vacuous-pass emitter defect and no bundle at all.
- **A banned-pattern leg for `apps/workflow-engine`.** The existing scan banned two other private paths but not that one, which is exactly why two `cd apps/workflow-engine/…` lines survived the demo app's extraction and told readers to enter a directory that no longer exists.

### Documentation

- **Both quickstarts are banner-marked as v1 rather than ported**, because porting them would have made them *wrong*: `versioning.md` §1.1 requires `preferredVersion` to name a 1.x member through the overlap, so a header-less request **is** a v1 request. Rewriting the paths to `/runs` without adding `OpenWOP-Version: 2` to every call would produce a guide that silently exercises v1 while claiming to teach v2 — worse than honest staleness. `QUICKSTART-10MIN.md` gains the one thing a reader cannot infer: the header and the unversioned path, together.
- **The README's reading order is labelled `(v1)`** and points v2 implementers at `docs/IMPLEMENT-CORE.md`. Its 85 links into `spec/v1/` are **left alone, because they are correct**: three of the eleven documents have no v2 counterpart at all — `auth.md`'s scope vocabulary, `observability.md`, and `channels-and-reducers.md` (the word `reducer` does not occur anywhere in `spec/v2/core/`) — and `spec/v2/` itself cites `spec/v1/` 101 times, because the 25,000-word core budget makes v2 a declaration surface and leaves the behaviour where it was written. The dishonesty was the missing label, not the links.

## [2.5.0] — 2026-09-18 — an outsider can audit a bundle, and three gates stop reading weaker than they are written

### Added

- **`openwop-conformance --verify <bundle> [--host-key <pem>]`** — audit a certification bundle with no host and no clone of the corpus. `verifyBundleV3` was exported but only ever called as the emitter's self-check, so auditing a third party's claim meant writing a driver script, which means in practice nobody did. **The exit contract has four values on purpose:** `0` verified · `1` rejected · **`2` coherent but NOT independently verified** · `3` not a bundle. Collapsing 0 and 2 is how the vacuous pass gets rebuilt — without exit 2, every CI that runs `--verify` with no key prints green and reports "verified". The command also states what it does not do: it never re-runs anything, so a host that measured itself wrongly and signed the result verifies clean. It audits an attestation, not a host.

### Fixed

- **`check-accepted-predicate.mjs` split on an UNANCHORED `/### Falsifiability/` at both call sites**, so an RFC that merely *mentions* that heading in prose had the wrong section read. RFC 0178 (prose at §C.1) and RFC 0184 (a second mention three lines after the real heading) each presented **zero** ids to rule 4 while carrying a passing one. `check-falsifiability.mjs` was already anchored — the two gates disagreed about where a table begins. Both anchored; 21/21 still green, two RFCs move from vacuous to genuinely checked.
- **Three falsifiability tables named no requirement id while their evidence already passed.** RFC 0179 now cites `openwop.requirement.0172.preferred-version-default` (its `.member` leg asserts §A.1's two clauses by name, `executed-pass` on both certified bundles); RFC 0182 cites its own three `run-list` ids; RFC 0181 §A.3 cites `openwop.requirement.0169.declaration-parity`, the same id RFC 0180 already cites for the same corpus gate. RFC 0167 and RFC 0183 are deliberately left alone — 0167's six rows are honest corpus gates, and 0183's own prose records that its fixture cannot exercise `refine` yet.
- **`check-rfc-status-coherence.mjs`'s `Supersedes` exemption was an unanchored substring test.** Only `^\s*—` was anchored; `amends`, `corrects`, `where it` and `§` matched anywhere, so a cell reading `RFC 0035 §B — …` would silently exempt itself from RFC 0174 §A.1. No RFC is exempted by a stray `§` today — but the corpus's first supersession pair (0173 → 0035) fires **only because its author happened not to write a section sign**. Anchored.
- **The same gate's docblock claimed it checked "`Superseded` WITH A FORWARD POINTER" and only the status flip was implemented** — a docblock that outran its code for the whole program. The forward pointer is now checked both ways and reciprocally: a superseded RFC must carry a `Superseded by`, and that pointer must name the RFC that superseded it, not merely some RFC. Sabotage-proved on the live 0173 → 0035 pair in both failure modes.
- **`spec/v2` had no banner rule at all** — rules 4–5 scan `spec/v1` only, while every `spec/v2/**` `Status:` banner cites RFC numbers. A `Stable` v2 document must now cite only `Accepted` RFCs. The stricter form is deliberate: "not non-terminal" would let a Stable doc cite a **`Superseded`** RFC forever, which is exactly the stale citation a reader follows into a dead end. Measured: 39 docs with a banner, 53 citations, **0 violations** — a pure ratchet.

## [2.4.7] — 2026-09-18 — an equality no string could satisfy, and the gate that never looked

### Fixed

- **`trigger-event.schema.json`'s `deliveryId` asserted that it *"equals the `causationId` stamped on `run.started`"* — a contract no implementation could satisfy.** True in v1, where both were free strings. RFC 0184/0187 made `deliveryId` tenant-bound (`/` REQUIRED) while `causationId` stayed typed as `eventId`, whose character class admits no `/`: the grammars have **empty intersection**. Nothing relied on it — the v2 `deliveryId` kind has no read surface in `api/v2/openapi.yaml` at all, and no committed bundle carries the pairing — which is exactly why this is a Class 3 conformance-affecting correction and not a safety-fix: nothing that was conforming stops conforming, because conforming was impossible. How a run points back at its causing delivery is left to the RFC that gives the kind a wire surface (RFC 0187 §Unresolved), where RFC 0040 §Alternatives' rejection of overloading `causationId` for a cross-space pointer already applies. Recorded in `COMPATIBILITY.md`. A normative cross-field rule also should not have lived in a schema description at all.
- **The root cause: `scripts/check-v2-schemas.mjs` never validated `examples`, so 13 across 6 schemas were invalid.** The structural walk skips `examples` — correctly, an example is an instance and not a schema — and nothing else looked at them. Nine were ids minted before their kind became tenant-bound: `trigger-event` shipped `"deliveryId": "dlv_a1b2"` as the canonical example of a field whose `$ref` requires a tenant segment plus 16 characters. An example is the first thing an implementer copies, so each one taught a shape the schema refuses. All 13 re-minted, and the gate now validates every root-level example against its own schema — sabotage-proved by restoring one bare id and watching it fail. That gate is the durable half: it stops the next kind-tightening from silently re-seeding the same defect.

## [2.4.6] — 2026-09-18 — the path a third party walks, made walkable

### Fixed

- See the `[Unreleased]` notes promoted here: the npm front door, INTEROP-MATRIX §"Add A Host", the pre-registration gate, the signing-key recipe, the dead v1 profile names, and the RFC 0180 §A.2 pointer.

## [2.4.5] — 2026-09-18 — `--require-behavior` never did anything, and a soft-skipped leg counted as acceptance evidence

### Fixed

- **`--require-behavior` was a silent no-op for the entire v2 program.** The CLI never parsed it: the flag fell into an arg-loop default arm whose comment read *"Unknown flag — pass through to vitest by ignoring here."* Only `OPENWOP_REQUIRE_BEHAVIOR=true` ever enabled strict mode, in which an ADVERTISED behaviour that cannot be observed FAILS instead of soft-skipping (RFC 0148 §B). The flag is prescribed as *the* certification setting by `docs/runbooks/V2-HOST-MIGRATION.md`, and `INTEROP-MATRIX.md` records the reference host's own bundles as cut with it — so the corpus's flagship evidence named a command that measured the loose answer and reported the strict one. That is the exact failure the runbook's own thesis warns about, in the tool that measures it. The flag now sets the variable, and **an unknown `--flag` is a hard error instead of a silent pass-through** — the old arm is why this went unnoticed: a typo, a renamed flag and a working flag were indistinguishable.
- **A leg that asserted and then soft-skipped counted as full acceptance evidence.** `resolveFileRecord` has always marked such a file `partial-witness:`; `resolveItRecord` dropped the note — and the per-`it` rows are the ones RFC 0174 §B.1 rule 4 reads. Measured consequence: `openwop.requirement.0173.webhook-durable-delivery.dead-letter` ends in an **unconditional** `softSkip('blocked', 'no normative dead-letter read surface…')` on every host, and still recorded a bare `executed-pass` that satisfied RFC 0173 §B's dead-letter row on **every bundle in the corpus, certified ones included**. The note now propagates, and `check-accepted-predicate.mjs` refuses a `partial-witness:` row as a rule-4 witness and names it in the failure.
- **`v2-bound-id-kinds` told operators to set a variable it did not read.** Its blocked note said *"set OPENWOP_WEBHOOK_RECEIVER_URL"* while the leg hard-coded `https://subscriber.invalid/hook` and never called `resolveRegistrationUrl`. In a suite where one blocked row denies every claimed profile (RFC 0168 §E.1), that is inoperative advice on a fatal row — and it is the last corpus-side thing that could deny a production host's re-cut, because a guard that resolves at registration time can reject a reserved `.invalid` name. The mint now routes through `resolveRegistrationUrl`.
- **`v2-webhook-delivery-shape` ignored `OPENWOP_WEBHOOK_RECEIVER_PORT`.** Under the tunnelled posture a production host must adopt, its deliveries went to the tunnel, the tunnel forwarded to the pinned port held by `v2-webhook-durable-delivery`'s receiver — which answers `500` by design — so its `deliveries` stayed empty, its legs soft-skipped, and (because `register()` had already asserted) the rows resolved `executed-pass`. **A wire-shape scenario that never opened a delivery body went green.** It now honours the pin; two major-2 files want that port, so the webhook lane needs `--max-workers 1`, which is already the certification setting. The guard that exists to catch exactly this (`webhook-receiver.test.ts`) had a hand-maintained list whose own comment says *"any future one belongs here too"* — this file landed at 2.3.1 and was never added. It is now.

## [2.4.4] — 2026-09-18 — the corpus's first `Superseded` RFC

### Changed

- **RFC 0173 flips `Active → Accepted` and RFC 0035 becomes the corpus's first `Superseded` RFC**, both in one PR as RFC 0174 §A.1 requires and RFC 0001's timing rule allows. Gap G1 closes on exactly the condition it named, which is what moves `spec/v1/gaps.json` and therefore makes this a release rather than an editorial commit.
- **0035's tripwire is retired by REPLACEMENT, not satisfied, and the RFC says so.** It asked for a non-steward host advertising `capabilities.sandbox.supported`; none ever did. `openwop.requirement.0173.pack-isolation` is `executed-pass` across ten legs on the steward's own reference host and `inapplicable` on both production hosts. The seven protocol-tier `node-pack-sandbox-*` invariants and four `sandbox_*` error codes keep RFC 0035 as their v1 citation through the overlap to 2026-12-04 — which is why this flip waited for its replacement instead of leading it.
- **Naming the standing v1 authorities inside a `Supersedes` cell made RFC 0173 claim to supersede nine Accepted RFCs.** `check-rfc-status-coherence.mjs` harvests every four-digit number in that cell, and the leading `—` was the only thing suppressing it — so removing the dash to name RFC 0035 produced eight spurious failures at once. The cell now names 0035 and nothing else; the "leaves standing" list moved to `Affects`, where no supersession rule reads it.

## [2.4.3] — 2026-09-18 — 608 ledger rows said `blocked` about checks that had nothing to complain about

### Fixed

- **Two `spec-corpus-validity` legs asserted only on failure, so a clean file asserted nothing — and an `it` that passes with zero assertions resolves to `blocked` / "unclassified return".** 608 of the corpus ledger's 943 ids read `blocked`: 184 Markdown files with no local links, 77 schemas declaring no absolute `$ref`, and the rest of the same shape. Every one was a file that was perfectly fine. RFC 0148 §A reserves `blocked` for behaviour that *could not be exercised because a required seam, fixture, credential, or dependency was unavailable* — nothing here was unavailable, and the corpus had already ruled on this exact class twice (`spec-coherence.ts`'s `SPEC_COHERENCE_DETAIL`, and `v2-coherence-not-in-bundle`'s rc.57 note). `inapplicable` would have been the wrong repair too: the requirement *did* apply and the leg *did* run. So the fix is at the assertion, not the disposition — each leg now collects its offenders and asserts once, unconditionally, which also names every bad link or `$ref` at once instead of failing on the first. **Ledger: `blocked` 608 → 0, `executed-pass` 841 → 1449.** The id count falls 943 → 335 because a leg that now carries an explicit `req()` id maps to that shared id rather than minting one per file — one requirement, checked per file, which is what it always was.
- **Why this was inert and still worth fixing.** No gate consumed those rows: `check-accepted-predicate` reads only `executed-pass`, `check-cut-gates` reads host bundles and never the corpus ledger, and `check-spec-coherence` compares id sets while reporting result drift rather than enforcing it. A host bundle could not have inherited them either — `src/coherence` is excluded from the published tarball and `certification-bundle.schema.json` rejects any bundle carrying a `spec-corpus-validity` id. But a ledger in which two thirds of the rows read `blocked` cannot be read at a glance, and `blocked` is the token that denies certification everywhere else it appears.

## [2.4.2] — 2026-09-18 — five holes in the gate that decides what counts as Accepted

### Fixed

- **`check-accepted-predicate.mjs` computed RFC 0174 §B.1 and answered wrong in five ways.** This is the machine predicate that decides `Active → Accepted` for every v2-era RFC, so each hole is an acceptance the corpus could have recorded on evidence that does not exist. (1) The evidence-tier rule matched the *word* `tier` anywhere in `Updated`: RFC 0180 cleared it on the narrative clause "Raised by the **tier-1 host**…", having declared no tier at all. It is now anchored to the declaration `Evidence tier: …`. (2) The unticked-box escape listed the literal `Phase 4`, which reads the NAME OF A BLOCKER as an excuse — RFC 0179's only criterion is "openwop-app advertises it (Phase 4 leg)", unticked and unmet, and the gate called it satisfied. Only a labelled `reason:` / `deferred:` excuses a box now. (3) Rule 4 iterated the requirement ids a falsifiability table names and so passed VACUOUSLY when it named none — true of RFC 0167, the program umbrella, plus 0180, 0185 and 0186. A row must now be either id-witnessed or verdict-declared, and a missing section is a failure. (4) Acceptance witnesses were harvested from every bundle in `evidence/v2-host-bundles/`, certified or not, so a bundle whose own profile claims read `certified: false` supplied witnesses exactly like a certified one; §B.1 says a *cited* bundle, and an acceptance may not rest on evidence the evidence format itself refuses. (5) §B.1's closing clause — "for a program child, the parent `Active`" — was implemented nowhere, and read literally it is a TRAP: the moment the umbrella flips `Accepted` it stops being `Active` and every child still `Active` becomes permanently ineligible. The checkable form of that sentence and of RFC 0167's own "when every child is `Accepted`" is one rule, now enforced: **the parent flips last.** Each of the five was sabotage-proved by forcing 0167, 0179 and 0180 to `Accepted` and confirming the gate names exactly the right failure.
- **`openwop.requirement.0173.pack-isolation.seam` could never be witnessed by any host.** It was asserted inside `v2-pack-isolation`'s shared `invoke()` helper. `generate-requirement-registry.mjs` harvests `req(…)` only within an `it`, so the id was absent from `requirements.json` and no bundle could carry a row for it — while `check-cut-gates.mjs`, which scans every `req(` in the source, went on demanding one, and RFC 0167 §G.2 failed on a row no host could supply. The seam contract now has its own `it`, which is the copy the registry can see; the helper keeps its `req(…)` call, because RFC 0168 §A.1 makes `req(…)` the only assertion-message form in `src/scenarios` — the fix is the extra `it`, not a quieter assertion.
- **RFC 0184 had no `### Falsifiability` section.** Its `## Conformance` section carries a six-variant sabotage matrix — better evidence than most — but not in the shape the gate reads, so rule 4 had no id to look up and said nothing about the RFC at all. The section now names `openwop.requirement.0184.bound-id-path-projection`, which is `executed-pass` on the reference host bundle.

## [2.4.1] — 2026-09-18 — the leg that reads the host, not the schema

### Fixed

- **`v2-chain-pin-exact` could not see the host's rule.** Its two legs assert a 4xx for `core.ai.callPrompt@^1` — which `ids.schema.json`'s `typeId` pattern already refuses, so the legs pass on schema validation alone. Sabotage-proved on the reference host: delete its §Exact pins enforcement entirely and both legs stay green. The new third leg publishes a chain whose **external** `subChainRef` carries `version: "^1.0.0"`, which `SubChainRef.ref` `oneOf[1]` types as a semver *range* and no schema can refuse — so it reads the host's rule or nothing. A tier-1 host measured the external branch as *typed, reachable and unexercised* on both production hosts, which is the precondition for exactly this class of vacuity. When RFC 0177 flips, its `chain-pin-exact` row reads **host-enforced** only for bundles cut on 2.4.1 or later.
- **Runbook, two host-reported operational rules.** *Preflight every opt-in fixture before you certify*: one `blocked` row denies certification, so a dead fixture and a broken host give the same verdict from opposite causes — measured here when a synthetic IdP tied to its stdin exited on a detached launch and blocked three rows in a bundle whose other 226 passed. Check the FIELD the scenario reads, not that a process exists. *Wait on the workers, never on the wrapper*: a wrapper that is itself waiting is indistinguishable from one that is testing, so two cut scripts can each count the other's waiter as a runner and hold a free machine idle — the tell a tier-1 host measured was 0.06 s of CPU in 23 minutes with zero workers.

## [2.4.0] — 2026-09-18 — four bindings the hosts found

### Added

- **RFC 0187 `Active` — four rules the corpus owed after a production host measured their absence.** §A: `webhookId` IS the `subscriptionId` kind (`identity.md` §5) — the mint surface (`POST /webhooks → { webhookId }`) had inherited v1's bare `type: string`, so the kind had no HTTP surface and the HTTP surface had no kind, and §5's `403 id_tenant_mismatch` check had nothing to read. The path parameter and the register response now `$ref` the kind in the derived `api/v2/openapi.yaml`, `trigger-subscription.schema.json` binds it, and `id-field-bindings.json` moves it out of `notAKind`. §B: a `type` with no codemap row passes through unchanged on the v1 read path of an era-`3` log — a host MUST NOT drop the row and MUST NOT refuse the read for it (both production hosts chose pass-through independently; the corpus said nothing, so drop and refuse were equally conformant). §C: approver eligibility binds every WRITER of the suspension record, not every route — a host whose durable store is client-writable MUST enforce it at the store (a tier-2 host gated all three of its resolve routes, then found its store was a fourth writer and its own watch teed the rewrite as the resolution). §D: a writer that emits a property a closed def cannot seat MUST mark the row, so the refusal names the writer instead of surfacing as an unexplained read failure far from it.
- **`v2-bound-id-kinds.test.ts`** — one leg per tenant-bound kind with a wire surface. Until now every v2 scenario that read a bound id read `runId`, so a host binding one kind of five was green — which is exactly what a tier-1 host's own audit found on its wire. The `deliveryId` leg records `inapplicable` on a corpus gap, not a host one: `api/v2/openapi.yaml` serves no dead-letter read, so the kind has nowhere to appear.
- **The `typeId` kind admits the exact pin `@<major>.<minor>.<patch>`** so a chain reference can carry one (RFC 0177 §E.1) while still `$ref`ing its kind as `identity.md` §5 requires; the kind's description states the one place a pin is permitted (a document that REFERENCES a type) and the one place it is forbidden (a document that DEFINES one) and **`form-content-pack-manifest.schema.json` `FormField.when` carries the `EdgeCondition` object** (§E.4). Both scenarios recorded `blocked` on the schema gap; the pattern admits the unpinned spelling so existing manifests stay valid and the MUST stays where §E.1 put it — refusal at register.
- **`spec/v1/deprecations.json`**: the bare `webhookId`, `removalTrigger: v1-end-of-support`.

### Fixed

- **`v2-webhook-durable-delivery` counted another scenario's deliveries against its retry budget.** Both legs filtered attempts by `runId` alone; a host that cannot reach the suite's loopback registers every scenario against one tunnelled receiver URL, so a concurrently running scenario's subscription matched the same run. A tier-2 host measured 6 attempts against a `maxAttempts` of 5 at `--max-workers 2` while its own logs showed five. Both legs now filter on the delivery's own `webhookId` as well, which is exact and holds at any worker count.
- **`McpFakeServer` gains `needs_input_loop`** — `needs_input` completes on the first retry and cannot reach any ceiling ≥ 1, so `v2-mrtr-rounds-ceiling` recorded `blocked` on every host. **`v2-negotiation-authenticated` reads `details.runId`** off the closed error envelope, where `v2-minimum-version-refused` already reads it; it recorded `blocked` on a host that had emitted the event correctly.
- Two non-normative paragraphs moved out of core prose into the schemas they govern (`webhook-delivery.schema.json` `runId`, `run-event.schema.json` `$comment`), per RFC 0174 §E.2a. Core budget 24,985 / 25,000.

## [2.3.5] — 2026-09-17 — the prose stops narrating its own edit history

### Changed

- **Editorial pass over `spec/v1/` and `spec/v2/` — the prose now reads as a specification rather than as its own edit history.** No normative requirement changed; every `MUST` / `SHOULD` / `MAY` in the corpus is the same obligation it was, and `openwop:check` steps 1–8 are green on the result. What went: status banners that recited authoring dates, landing sequences and superseded wordings instead of stating what the document specifies; the retired `## Open spec gaps` stubs left behind when RFC 0174 §E.3 absorbed those tables into `gaps.json` (the register rows are unchanged — only the dangling `sources` tokens pointing at the deleted banners were dropped); errata narration (`Errata 2026-09-10: this read "any path"`), self-referential editing warnings, and rationale paragraphs that argued with earlier drafts of themselves. Requirements that were stated only inside that narration were rewritten as direct normative prose in place rather than deleted — `versioning.md` §1.2 / §1.4 / §5 and `security-defaults.md` §Threat models are the substantive cases. Added [`spec/v1/README.md`](./spec/v1/README.md), the v1 tree's missing entry point: source-of-truth precedence, task-oriented reading paths, and a grouped document map (the v2 tree already had one; v1 sent readers to a flat 60-file directory listing). `spec/v2/README.md` was rewritten to the same shape. v2 ext documents gained an explicit "Contract boundary" section stating what a discovery-only reservation does and does not promise a client.
- **`spec/v1/grpc-transport.md` keeps the sentence its deprecation register reads.** The banner rewrite dropped "REST + SSE remains the REQUIRED wire surface", which `openwop.deprecation.grpc-transport-core` names as its source token — the annotation and the normative statement are the same sentence, so `check-deprecations` failed. Restored in the condensed banner.
- **README published-artifact versions match the registries again.** The line advertised `@openwop/openwop` and `openwop-client` at v2.2.0 while both serve 2.3.0, and separately claimed suite `2.2.0` "is on npm" two paragraphs after stating 2.3.3. `check-advertised-versions` treats this line as authoritative for downstream consumers.
- **Reference host evidence re-cut on suite `2.3.3`** (`evidence/v2-host-bundles/openwop-host-v2-reference.json`, openwop-examples #48): 195 / 0 / 0 / 44 / 0, 1682 assertions, all three claimed profiles certified, signed `v2-reference-3`; the host now accepts and emits the RFC 0184 `~`-projection and renders every channel per subscriber contract. INTEROP-MATRIX row updated.
- **Second sweep: 21 remaining `spec/v1` banners normalized** to the same one-line `Status · version · scope` shape, and the factual drift they carried is fixed — seven `Added by RFC NNNN (\`Active\` …)` stamps now cite the RFC without a status that had already flipped to `Accepted`, the `grpc-transport.md` conformance paragraph no longer claims the `capabilities.grpc` schema block "does not yet exist" (it landed with RFC 0094 and is in `capabilities.schema.json`), and `portability.md` dropped the "graduates when RFC 0098 is Accepted" sentence after the banner already recorded the graduation.
- **Edit-history narration removed from normative prose.** Dated parentheticals (`added 2026-08-16`, `corrected 2026-08-16, S22 — this line read the nested shape before`, `SP-11a`, `Phase 3 T3.4`, "an earlier revision of this paragraph said … and is withdrawn") are deleted or folded into the requirement itself; convergence windows that still apply keep their dates because they are behavior, not history. The `profiles.md` "Why this is not a wire field" section now states the derivation rationale without narrating the discarded draft.

## [2.3.4] — 2026-09-17 — the registers say where

### Changed

- **36 gap rows dispositioned with evidence (X2a–c of the open-items plan).** Every `closed` cell now names the PR, scenario, schema line or bundle row that resolved it. Three rows are `externally-gated:non-steward-host` (mTLS revocation, independent seam audit — a host or auditor outside the steward organization); two are `transferred:ROADMAP.md#implementation-ecosystem` (refresh-SLA re-measure, portable `{{params.*}}`). Open gap rows corpus-wide 46 → 12. RFC 0173 G1 stays open: RFC 0035's supersession is 0173's own flip PR. Tier labels are deliberately **not** written on `Active` RFCs — `GOVERNANCE.md` states the tier at the flip, on a deployed bundle.
- **Why a cut.** `spec/v1/gaps.json` ships inside `@openwop/spec-artifacts`, so regenerating it moves the published contract; version sites → 2.3.4. This section was reconstructed from the commit in [2.3.5] — the 2.3.4 release PR wrote its `conformance/CHANGELOG.md` row and left this one unwritten, so the row's own reference to "openwop CHANGELOG [2.3.4]" pointed at nothing.

## [2.3.4] — 2026-09-17 — the registers say where

_Entry added in 2.4.0: the cut shipped without it — the edit's anchor missed behind an earlier assertion in the same script, and `check-shipped-changelog` only guards the packed per-package file. The rule the miss teaches is in CONTRIBUTING §"Two rules for the scripts you write"._

### Changed

- **36 gap-register rows on the v2-era Active RFCs carry a disposition with evidence** (RFC 0166 §A.1): `closed` naming the PR, scenario, schema line or bundle row that resolved it; `externally-gated:non-steward-host` for the three needing a host or auditor outside the steward organisation; `transferred:ROADMAP.md#implementation-ecosystem` for the two v2.x follow-ups. Corpus-wide `open` gap rows 46 → 12. RFC 0173 G1 stays `open` on purpose — RFC 0035's supersession is 0173's own flip PR.
- **RFC 0180's seven acceptance boxes and RFC 0167's §G.1 box ticked** with the section or check that proves each. Evidence-tier labels are deliberately NOT written on Active RFCs: GOVERNANCE states the tier at the flip, on a deployed bundle.
- `spec/v1/gaps.json` regenerated (it ships in spec-artifacts, which is why the register work was a cut); README advertises the SDKs at 2.3.0.

## [2.3.3] — 2026-09-17 — the retirement lane bites again

### Fixed

- **`v2-bound-id-path-projection` failed a conformant single-major host.** The double-projection leg asserted `404` unconditionally. Decoded once, a double projection is a *bare* id; through the overlap it resolves under the caller's tenant and is not found, but once a host advertises no 1.x member `identity.md` §5 requires the bare form to be refused `400 validation_error` — which is exactly what the reference host's retirement lane did, and the leg called it a failure. The leg now expects `404` on a dual-stack host and `400 validation_error` on a single-major one, read from `protocolVersions`. Second time the retirement lane has caught what no dual-stack host can surface (rc-era `v2-version-header-honored` was the first). Suite `2.3.3`, spec-artifacts `2.3.3`.
- **`v2-webhook-delivery-shape` builds one Ajv per wire, cached across legs** (code review).
- **The `payload_unprojectable` dead-letter rule is stated where core owns delivery.** 2.3.2 put "dead-letter on the first attempt, never retry" in RFC 0185 §C alone; `webhooks.md` §Durability now carries it and the RFC cites it (architect finding, normative ownership).
- **`check-accepted-predicate.mjs` roll-up cannot be satisfied by a sibling requirement.** A `<id>.<leg>` row witnesses its parent only when it is not itself a declared Falsifiability id — two such pairs exist (0168 `bundle-signature-attributable` / `.v1-root`, 0176 `well-known-one-resource` / `.v2-representation`). Sabotage-proved.
- **`check-accepted-predicate.mjs` host-tier witness check is a failure, and it rolls legs up to their requirement (W5).** RFC 0174 §B.1 rule 4 names host bundles as evidence; since #1373 an Accepted RFC's host-tier id with no `executed-pass` row anywhere was *reported*, because three RFC 0169 ids read as unwitnessed. They were witnessed on every committed bundle — as suffixed legs (`capability-record-shape.required-fields`, …) that an exact-key lookup missed. A requirement is now witnessed when it or any `<id>.<leg>` under it passes, in the ledger or a bundle, and a real gap fails the gate. Sabotage-proved: removing those legs from every bundle reds it.
- **`GOVERNANCE.md` §"Acceptance evidence tiers" regains its "Deployed, not merged" rule.** RFC 0165's `Updated` field and the CHANGELOG entry for #1222 both cite the rule "a merge is a promise; only a deployment is a witness". The paragraph that states it was written on a revert branch that never landed, so both citations pointed at text main did not have. It is restored as written, except that its history now matches #1222: the revert to `Active` was drafted, never landed, and the acceptance was re-grounded on deployed evidence instead.

## [2.3.2] — 2026-09-17 — the webhook reader read the wrong field

### Fixed

- **`v2-webhook-delivery-shape.test.ts` asserted a shape no host can emit.** The owner echo is a property of the `run.started` **payload** (`run-event-payloads.schema.json` `runStarted.owner` — v2 `{tenant, workspace?, subject}`, v1 `{tenant, workspace?, principal?, principalKind?}`, both closed), not of the event envelope, which names no `owner` in either major. 2.3.1 subscribed to `run.completed` (payload `{outputs, durationMs}`) and read `event.owner`: leg 1 could only fail, leg 3 could only pass. Found by cutting the reference host's W4 bundle on 2.3.1 — a host that had rendered nothing wrong went red with `expected []`. The file now subscribes to `run.started`, validates the delivery against `webhook-delivery.schema.json` (v2) and the payload against the per-major `runStarted` definition, and reads `payload.owner`. Leg 2 validates against the **v1** `runStarted` definition — the owner's keys are not the discriminator (v1's owner admits `subject` since RFC 0165 §B), the integer-vs-string `engineVersion` is — and registers at `POST /v1/webhooks` — v1 operations keep their `/v1/` path keys through the overlap (`versioning.md` §1.4) — and a host with no 1.x webhook surface records `inapplicable` there, not a failure. Two more 2.3.1 gates were vacuous and are fixed: leg 2 read a `versions.supported` no discovery document has (now `protocolVersions`, RFC 0172 §A.1), and leg 3 read `era2Gate`'s `null` — which means the seam IS advertised — as absence; a host that does not fan out seeded history records leg 3 `inapplicable`. Same requirement id, same gate. Suite `2.3.2`, spec-artifacts `2.3.2`.
- **`payload_unprojectable` (500, not retriable) is registered in `spec/v2/errors.json`.** RFC 0185 §C says carry or fail and named no code for the failure, so the first host to implement it minted a vendor code for a core rule. It is the property-level sibling of `event_type_unmapped` (RFC 0176 §A.3): a row whose payload the host can neither carry nor seat is not readable under that contract, on every reader — poll, SSE (`event: error` frame), fork, the webhook envelope (dead-lettered on attempt 1, never retried). `events.md` §Era-2 and RFC 0185 §C name it; `error-envelope.schema.json` regenerated.
- **`clarificationRequested` is a deletion, not a hatch (RFC 0185 §E).** Both production hosts measured no emitter (tier 2: zero in 3000 runs; tier 1: one type-list hit, zero write-seam samples). `check-payload-closure-hatched.mjs` moves it from `PENDING` to `MODELLED` with both measurements cited; RFC 0186 §Unresolved loses the row. `PENDING` is now empty.
- **`conformance/package-lock.json` carried `why-is-node-running` at `2.3.1` against a `2.3.0` tarball** — residue of an earlier global version sed. Restored; the lockfile is now audited entry-by-entry (version against resolved tarball) before a cut.

## [2.3.1] — 2026-09-17 — a channel nobody's scenario read, a register that could not bite, a status that could only go one way

### Added

- **`v2-webhook-delivery-shape.test.ts` (W3b).** `webhooks.md:22` already required a major-2 delivery body to be the v2 payload — "one source, three renderings" — and **0 of 7 webhook scenarios validated the body.** A tier-1 host had two major-2 egress channels; poll/SSE projected, the webhook fan-out forwarded the in-process event, and a major-2 subscriber received the v1 owner block with every webhook scenario green. Three legs: a major-2 subscriber's body validates against `run-event.schema.json` and `owner` is exactly `{tenant, subject}`; a **major-1** subscriber still receives `principal` (`versioning.md` §1.2 — projecting every channel is as wrong as projecting none); and, seam-gated, an **era-2** seeded run delivers projected — the tier-2 host's fan-out is era-aware, not major-aware, so a single fresh run witnesses only one branch.
- **`spec/v2/ext/README.md` + `check-ext-status-coherence.mjs` (W8).** Seventeen ext docs shipped `Draft` and nothing said what moves one. Status is now a predicate over evidence: `Stable` requires an `executed-pass` row under the family's witness id in a **certified** bundle; a `Draft` doc whose family has that row is reported **graduable**; `Retired` requires the family gone from the declaration. Both directions gated and sabotage-proved. The four `ext/` directories with no declared family (`grpc-transport`, `portability`, `provider-idempotency`, `sandbox-runtime-notes`) say in their headers that they are notes outside the rule.

### Changed

- **`spec/v1/deprecations.json` (W7).** 37 rows with `removeIn: 2.0` and no `removalTrigger` now carry `v2.0-cut` — v2.0.0 was tagged 2026-09-05, so the trigger is a fact, not a plan. Four `proposed` rows whose surface has no occurrence anywhere in `spec/v2`, `schemas/v2` or `api/v2` (`gap-local-alias`, `supported-transports-field`, `manifest-signing-method`, `seam-operations-in-canonical-api`) flipped to `deprecated` with the reason recorded. **Twenty stay `proposed`**: their surfaces still occur in v2 and each needs a per-row decision, not a script.
- **RFC 0183 §Unresolved 1 resolved — `action: 'timeout'` is record-only.** v1 `interrupt.md:165` already said `timeout` is *"host-internal — emit timeout sentinel, not a resume."* A host MUST NOT accept `timeout` on a resume request; it is minted by the host's own timer branch, and `resolvedBy` on such a row is the host's subject or absent — a client posting it could disguise a decision as expiry while `resolvedBy` named them. Stated in the `action` schema description (unbudgeted) and the RFC; witnessed by the tier-2 host's route tests.

## [2.3.0] — 2026-09-17 — three payload seats the hosts measured and the corpus lacked

### Added

- **RFC 0186 §A.1 — `conversation.exchanged` is one def, not two.** The corpus carried two closed defs for one event that could not both be satisfied: the codemap-bound `conversationExchanged` seated `outcome` and not `turn`; the orphaned `ConversationExchangedPayload` seated `turn` (required) and not `outcome`. The tier-1 host emits `turn` and never `outcome`; the tier-2 host emits `outcome` (the `ctx.suspend` resume value) and never `turn`. The bound def is now the union `{conversationId, turnIndex, turn?, outcome?}` — a strict widening of both — and the orphan is an alias. `conversation-event`'s `oneOf` cannot double-match: `opened` requires `initialTurn`, `closed` requires `finalTurn`, and the closed union names neither.
- **RFC 0186 §A.2 — `reason` on `interruptResolved` and `nodeSuspended`.** A cause axis — *what triggered a resolution without a human* — disjoint from `kind`, RFC 0183 `decision` (what) and `action` (how). String with a documented host-grown domain (`timeout`, `condition`, `false-positive`, `over-threshold`, `quorum-reject`, `timer`), not an enum. Measured single-valued `timeout` on one host; dropped on 350 rows on the other. `interrupt.resolved.outcome` is deliberately **not** seated: single-valued `'rejected'`, a duplicate of `decision`.
- **RFC 0186 §A.3 — `ApprovalData.onTimeout`** (`reject | approve | escalate`), the one field in the hosts' historical `{title, message, timeout, onTimeout}` that was unmodelled anywhere; the other three were already seated (`title`, `description`, `suspend-request.timeoutMs` — where `0` means no timer). The RFC 0185 closure gate gains a `MODELLED` disposition for a deliberate narrowing resolved by a seat rather than a hatch.

### Why a minor

`PROTOCOL-STATUS.md`: minor on scenario add/remove. `v2-payload-seats-0186.test.ts` is new, server-free, sabotage-proved three ways.

### Changed

- **RFC 0174 §E.2a — the core word budget stays at 25,000 and the rule already practised is now written down:** core prose carries the MUSTs; rationale, measurement and history live in schema `description`/`$comment` fields, which the budget does not count. Two guards from a real near-miss (a compression briefly changed `MUST NOT rewrite era-2 rows in place` before review caught it): a budget trim MUST NOT alter a normative clause — diff the RFC 2119 sentences before and after — and a paragraph that duplicates a schema description is deleted, not compressed. Raising the ceiling was rejected: 16 core RFCs have landed against it. Governance prose only; no wire change; no cut.

### Changed

- **RFC 0183 and RFC 0184: `Active → Accepted`, tier-1.** The reference host `app.openwop.dev` now runs openwop-app `0ab209b0e` on suite 2.2.1.
  - **RFC 0183:** its served certification bundle records every `interrupt-approval` requirement `executed-pass`, with no skips. That covers the refine and edit-accept round-trips and both refusal legs.
  - **RFC 0184, accept side:** measured on the production front door. `~2F` and `%2F` both answer 403 `id_tenant_mismatch`, so the id is decoded; the pre-RFC build answered 404.
  - **RFC 0184, emit side and the unaided scenario:** witnessed in the deployed commit's major-2 lane (73 pass / 0 red), with kicktodo.com `bd73008` as the second deployed witness.
  - **Recorded gaps:** the bundle's 0184 row is `inapplicable`, because the certify lane runs at major 1. The openwop-examples SQLite host does not yet implement RFC 0183, which is why Conformance Soak is red on those legs.

## [2.2.3] — 2026-09-16 — v2 closed 53 payload defs that v1 left open, with no hatch

### Added

- **RFC 0185 §B — the RFC 0177 §C.2 vendor hatch on 53 run-event payload defs.** v1's `run-event-payloads.schema.json` declares `additionalProperties: **true**`; v2's declares `false` on the same defs. 53 made that transition, **none carried a hatch, and no migration row recorded it** — so every extra key a host legitimately recorded under v1, because v1 explicitly invited them, became invalid at the cut with nowhere to go. Two production hosts lost data independently: one drops 32 distinct keys at the major-2 read (the conversation content among them); the other projects era-3 rows **on write** and lost a variable's value **at rest**. The defs stay `additionalProperties: false`; `^(openwop-|x-|vendor\.)` is carried opaquely.

### Fixed

- **`events.md` §Era-2: a projection MUST NOT silently drop a property** — carry it or fail. `additionalProperties: false` means *invalid*, not *delete the offending key*; dropping is the cheapest implementation, leaves no trace, and on a write path is irreversible. Both reporting hosts arrived at it independently and neither intended to.

### Noted, not fixed

- **`conversation.exchanged` has two closed, contradictory defs.** `conversation-event.schema.json`'s `ConversationExchangedPayload` seats `{conversationId, turn, turnIndex}`; `run-event-payloads.schema.json`'s `conversationExchanged` seats `{conversationId, outcome, turnIndex}`. A payload with `turn` fails one, with `outcome` fails the other. The codemap binds the one **without** `turn`, while a fully-specified `ConversationTurn` sits next door `$ref`d by nothing outside its own file. So the host that reported the conversation content as unmodelled was wrong — and so was I for confirming it. RFC 0185 §D records it; flipping the binding changes validation for every host emitting `outcome`, so it is not done here.

## [2.2.2] — 2026-09-16 — "read-only" did not say the v1 registry tree is deliberately behind

### Fixed

- **`packs.md` §The registry tree: "the v1 tree is served read-only through the overlap" → "frozen through the overlap, deliberately behind this one."** A tier-1 host operator read "read-only" as *current, just not accepting writes*, resolved `packs.openwop.dev/v1/index.json` from a major-2 host, and reported 154 of 156 packs drifted. A second session reproduced and confirmed the number. Against `registry/v2/` — the tree a major-2 client resolves against — the drift was **zero**. The MUST that would have prevented it was already there in the next sentence (*"a client MUST resolve every registry path through `endpoints` rather than construct one"*); what was missing was any hint that constructing the v1 path returns deliberately-older versions rather than an error. Word-neutral: paid for from two adjacent connectives.
- **`evidence/cross-repo-manifests.json` was stale against `openwop-registry`** for `registry/.well-known/openwop-registry.json` and `registry/scripts/verify-signatures.mjs`, both unchanged since 2026-09-04. **Pre-existing on `origin/main`, independent of this change** — verified by running the check on a clean `origin/main` worktree before touching anything. Re-vendored.
- **`conformance/package-lock.json` pinned the `@openwop/spec-artifacts` peer at `2.0.0-rc.0`** while `package.json` had moved to the current release. The lockfile's own `version` tracked; its `peerDependencies` did not.
- **`spec/v2/peer-dependency-aliases.json`** regenerated from the declaration (stale against the registry's published manifests).

### Added

- **Suite 2.2.1 — RFC 0183 §A.2's `edit-accept` arm is witnessed.** New fixture `conformance-approval-edit-accept` (catalogued in `conformance/fixtures.md`) and two legs in `interrupt-approval.test.ts`: the resolved payload MUST carry `action: "edit-accept"` and the `editedArtifactData` supplied, and an edit-accept with no `editedArtifactData` MUST be refused. The refine arm was witnessed in 2.2.0; this arm was stated in `spec/v2/core/interrupt.md` §Approval and measured nowhere. Gated on fixture advertisement.

- **RFC 0183 and RFC 0184 `Draft` → `Active`, and the gap that let a Draft RFC ship an enforced scenario is closed.** Suite 2.2.0 enforced both RFCs' scenarios ungated while both headers read `Draft`; a host's ratchet cannot tell a proposal from a contract, so hosts implemented against a status that promised the wire could still move. Both flip to `Active` with their windows waived and logged (`MAINTAINERS.md`, `docs/WAIVER-RETROSPECTIVE-REGISTER.md`); RFC 0184 names its RFC 0147 §A.6 override. RFC 0183 §A.1 is raised from SHOULD to a MUST conditioned on approval kind, with its normative home in `spec/v2/core/interrupt.md` §Events: `interrupt-approval.test.ts` already asserted it, and `COMPATIBILITY.md` §2.3 forbids a suite stricter than the spec on wire shape — the text rises to the test rather than the test dropping to a witness of nothing. `scripts/check-rfc-status-coherence.mjs` rule 7 now fails when a shipped conformance requirement cites a `Draft` RFC.

- **RFC 0184 — a tenant-bound id travels as one `~`-escaped path segment.** A tenant-bound id is two segments joined by `/`; a path parameter is one. The corpus said `%2F` carried the separator, and a tier-1 host's front door decoded it back to `/` before forwarding — every bound id was unreachable through the host's own origin while the direct service URL answered 200 (`v2-created-run-readable.test.ts` records the measurement). The projection escapes every UTF-8 byte outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` as `~` + two uppercase hex digits. `~` is RFC 3986 §2.3 **unreserved**, so no intermediary has license to rewrite it; `%2F` needs a front door to tell a percent-encoded *reserved* octet (§6.2.2.2: MUST NOT decode) from an unreserved one (SHOULD decode), and deployed front doors do not. A host MUST accept and MUST emit the projection, MUST still accept `tenant%2Fopaque`, and MUST NOT mint a tenant-bound id containing `~`. `spec/v2/core/identity.md` §5, `schemas/v2/ids.schema.json`, `conformance/src/lib/bound-id.ts`, `conformance/src/scenarios/v2-bound-id-path-projection.test.ts`.

- **RFC 0184 §A.5 — the projection MUST be applied exactly once.** The codec is deliberately not idempotent (`a~3Ab` → `a~7E3Ab`; escaping the marker is what makes it injective), so a second application silently corrupts and nothing marks an id as already projected. Both reporting hosts found they already compose projections on one read path. Stated on the value, not the call site: project where an id leaves the host, never re-encode its own output — and witnessed, since a double-projected segment MUST 404. §A.6 records what is NOT projected: author-minted kinds (`nodeId` already admits `:`), `Idempotency-Key` (caller-supplied and tilde-legal), and that the `{16,128}` bounds govern the id, not the projected segment.

### Fixed

- **`docs/IMPLEMENT-CORE.md` and `docs/IMPLEMENTER-PATH.md` pointed a new host at v1.** Both named `spec/v1/`, the v1 nine-scenario floor, v1 profile strings and `/v1/…` paths, though v2 is the current major. Rewritten to target v2: `spec/v2/core/` and its reading order, the `spec/v2/profiles.json` predicates and floors (`openwop-discovery-core`, `openwop-core-standard`), unversioned paths from `spec/v2/path-manifest.json`, the suite's `--target-major 2` and signed bundle v3, and the v2 table of `INTEROP-MATRIX.md`. Each keeps a labelled pointer to the v1 guidance in `spec/v1/`, and says where a v1 item has no v2 equivalent.
- **The conformance suite had no way to clear the host's mock program store, and the host-side comment said it was "called between conformance scenarios".** It was called only by the host's own unit tests. A program a scenario does not fully drain stayed pending for the life of the host process, and the four envelope-truncation scenarios seed `finishReason: 'length'` programs on purpose — so a later file dispatching on a colliding node consumed the leftovers and failed `envelope_truncation_unrecoverable` with nothing in that file to explain it (measured on `replay-observable-sequence-determinism`: red in-suite, green alone, five runs on unchanged bases, one red at load 3.4 on an idle box). `conformance/src/setup.ts` now posts the host's reset seam in a per-FILE `afterAll` — per file because programs are seeded for a whole scenario's attempt sequence; best-effort because a host without the seam answers 404 and a suite must never fail a compliant host for lacking a test seam. Suite 2.1.7's per-fixture node ids narrowed the collision; this removes the leftover. Half two of two: openwop-app #3870 adds the `POST /v1/host/openwop-app/test/mock-ai/reset` seam this calls. (#1357)
- **Five tenant-bound id grammars rejected the tenant grammar they reference.** `ids.schema.json` `tenantId` admits an `anon:` prefix; `runId`, `interruptId`, `subscriptionId`, `deliveryId` and `effectId` spelled their tenant segment `[A-Za-z0-9._~-]{1,128}`, which does not admit `:`. **Every runId an anon-tenant host minted was schema-invalid** — and `tenantId`'s own description claimed the opposite ("`:` is a legal path character … so this stays safe inside the `<tenantId>/<opaque>` run-id form"). Not hypothetical: `test-seam-unauthenticated.test.ts` records a reporting host minting `tenantId: "anon:<sid>"`. The five patterns are widened to match (additive). Found while writing RFC 0184, not caused by it.
- **`tenantId`'s rationale cited the wrong RFC.** It attributed the `anon:` prefix to "RFC 0132 anonymous tenants". RFC 0132 has no anonymous tenants — it puts `anon:` on the **principal** and §C.1 requires the host to resolve a real `tenant` (the surface owner). The citation is corrected in place; the affordance stays, because narrowing a grammar released in v2.0.0 is a major-version change, not an erratum (RFC 0184 §A.3, deferred to v3).
- **`identity.md` §5's `typeId` row contradicted its own schema and its own next paragraph.** The table printed `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*(\.[a-z][a-zA-Z0-9-]*)+$` — no underscore — so it rejected `vendor.acme.my_tools.echo`, which the paragraph immediately below it said MUST be legal, and which `ids.schema.json` accepts. The row now matches the schema (including `maxLength: 256`) and the duplicated paragraph is dropped in favour of the schema's fuller description.
- **Four scenarios each kept a private copy of the tenant-bound id regex**, and all four had drifted narrower than the schema. They now import one exported `BOUND_ID`.

- **RFC 0183** (`Draft`): `interruptResolved` cannot record four of the five resume actions. The def is `additionalProperties: false` with no seat for the action an approver took and none for the feedback a `refine` REQUIRES, while `decision` — a **different axis**, the RFC 0051 governance outcome `granted|rejected|overridden` — was an unconstrained `string`, so `decision: 'refine'` validated against no documented value. v1's `approvalReceived` required an enum-constrained `action` over five values and carried `refineFeedback` and `editedArtifactData` beside it; v2 is both narrower AND closed, so a host could not carry them even informally — and `decision`'s own description asserts the carry from v1 is complete. Adds `action`, `refineFeedback`, `editedArtifactData` (all OPTIONAL) and closes `decision`'s enum. **An earlier reading proposed widening `decision` to admit `refine`; that is rejected in the RFC — an override and a refine are orthogonal facts about one resolution, not alternatives.** Witnessed by two new legs on `interrupt-approval.test.ts` against a new `conformance-approval-refine` fixture — new rather than widening `conformance-approval`, whose `actions` every host already serves. The witness needs no seams profile, so it runs on both production hosts. Found by a host validating 3000 production runs against the v2 defs.

- `interrupt.md` §ApprovalData offered the action **`'edit'`**; every other statement in the corpus — including that document's own §"Action-name choice (`'edit-accept'`)" forty-five lines later, the `ApprovalData.actions` enum in `suspend-request.schema.json`, that enum's description (*"until accept/reject/refine/**edit-accept** fires"*), and the `ApprovalResume` union — says `'edit-accept'`. The bare form appeared **exactly once in the whole corpus**, and it was in the TypeScript declaration a host implements from: offer `edit` and the v2 schema enum rejects it. A document that contradicts itself forty-five lines apart is worse than one that is simply wrong, because the reader who checks finds agreement somewhere. Found while answering a host's question about whether `refine` is the right seat for a three-action gate's "request changes" — it is, and `interrupt.md` §Backward-compat mapping already specifies that exact normalization.

- `run-event-payloads.schema.json` `_typeIndex` now says **where its map is**. The node is a JSON Schema, so listing its keys returns `description`/`type`/`additionalProperties`/`properties` — the 118 entries are under `properties`, and the natural query "is this type registered?" reads as a miss. **Two hosts hit it independently within a day**, both while checking whether `output.chunk` is in the index, and both recovered only by distrusting their own instrument rather than the corpus claim. One trap deeper: the v2 file carries **two** `_typeIndex` nodes — `$defs._typeIndex` in v1 vocabulary (`agent.toolCalled`, `run.resuming`, informative) and a top-level one in v2 vocabulary (`agent.tool-called`, `run.resume-started`, NORMATIVE) — so finding *a* `_typeIndex` is not enough. All three descriptions now state the shape, and the v2 one records that a type absent from it is not a persisted event type, synthesized SSE frame names being deliberately absent. Descriptions only; no key, value or count changes.

- `event-codemap.schema.json` (v1 and v2) now **describes `note` and `notes`**, which carried no description at all. The fields have different provenance — `notes` is regenerated on every `--write`, `note` is hand-written and survives — and both are **naming rationale only**. Without that stated, `note: "artifact event; noun form permitted"` reads as a claim about semantics: *this event is about artifacts*.
